Capacity and preauthorization bound concurrent work; they do not promise a worst-case wall-clock completion bound for an admitted exact-ownership drain. Remaining local availability exposure is limited to an authorized active-principal process running an exact admitted RustDesk role; arbitrary Interactive Users are rejected before header wait, and neither case exposes password bytes or administrator authority.
R-S11jMUST Windows privacy-display recovery is process-local typed state, never a serialized registry-write request
An elevated Windows runtime MUST NOT deserialize a registry root, path, value name, value type, or replacement bytes from ordinary configuration and replay them under HKLM. Privacy-mode connectivity recovery may retain only a process-local, non-serializable collection constructed from registry values that the same process read immediately before and after its display transition. Each recovery object may identify only one directly enumerated child of the fixed HKLM\SYSTEM\CurrentControlSet\Control\GraphicsDrivers\Connectivity namespace and retain the prior raw Recent value; the restoring code derives the root, parent path, value name, and write operation internally, retains every changed child, and attempts every restoration. The collection cannot cross JSON, serde, generic Config, startup argv handling, or local IPC, and normal turn-off consumes it exactly once after restoring the display modes. This source-level closure deliberately makes no durable crash-recovery claim: the installed service child has read-only machine-config authority under R-S11h, and the unsupported elevated user-profile server is not an acceptable persistence authority. Any future durable recovery MUST use the documented Windows CCD topology APIs or a narrow service-owned transaction whose privileged receiver validates the complete display operation; it may not restore the deleted generic registry replay.
R-S11kMUST Linux service-owned configuration has one passwd-derived root, never an ambient HOME/XDG root
The stable Linux --service supervisor and its exact --server --service-owned-server child own unattended credentials and machine configuration under R-S11b, so their storage namespace MUST NOT be selected by inherited HOME, XDG_CONFIG_HOME, a sudo environment policy, or init-manager-specific cleanup. After the signed custom.txt identity may set the constrained application name and before the first Config read, each service-owned role MUST initialize one immutable process-local root from the current effective uid's home returned by the system account database, followed by .config/<directories-next project name>. Thereafter both Config::get_home and Config::path MUST consume that root; absence of an absolute clean passwd home, invalid project identity, or inconsistent reinitialization is fatal. The root/headless child launcher clears its environment and exports only that same passwd-derived HOME, never a copied supervisor HOME or XDG_CONFIG_HOME. Ordinary user-owned --server, viewer, and UI processes retain normal XDG redirection: the XDG Base Directory specification and directories-next project-directory contract remain appropriate where the invoking user owns both policy and storage. The security boundary is the application role and the effective uid's getpwuid account record, not the fact that one particular systemd service environment happens to omit or normalize variables.
R-S11lMUST Linux service children rebuild session environment from the selected desktop, never ambient supervisor state
The Linux --service supervisor supports systemd, SysV, OpenRC, runit, and manual launch, so its inherited process environment is launcher input rather than session authority. Every service-owned child MUST begin with environment inheritance disabled and receive only a bounded launcher-owned map. The child principal decision and the desktop-session decision are separate typed inputs: choosing the root service principal may not discard the selected Desktop snapshot, and choosing the active user may not itself prove session variables. HOME comes only from the selected principal's system account record; USER, LOGNAME, and XDG_RUNTIME_DIR are exported only for a validated non-root active-user credential drop. DISPLAY, XAUTHORITY, WAYLAND_DISPLAY, and DBUS_SESSION_BUS_ADDRESS may be exported only from the service's selected desktop snapshot. TERM is selected from a bounded value associated with that desktop uid or a fixed terminal-capability fallback. Ambient DISPLAY, XAUTHORITY, Wayland, D-Bus, terminal-multiplexer, Pulse, PipeWire, HOME, and XDG values MUST NOT be copied into the child, and the supervisor may not mutate its process-global environment as a handoff mechanism. Linux execve makes envp explicit, Rust Command::env_clear disables inheritance, and X.Org documents DISPLAY/XAUTHORITY as the X server and authorization selectors; the service therefore supplies those selectors from its chosen desktop object rather than from ambient launcher state. A hostile-environment actual-binary lifecycle test MUST prove the root and active-user launch maps remain bounded and the ambient values are absent.
R-S11mMUST Linux service-owned working directories and custom-client sidecars are deployment-bound, never ambient launcher authority
The current working directory is an inherited process input: Linux resolves every relative pathname from it, fork inherits it, and execve leaves it unchanged; Rust likewise documents that Command inherits the parent's working directory unless current_dir is set. Systemd, SysV, OpenRC, runit, and the shipped manual wrapper normally start this service at /, but application authority MUST NOT depend on each launcher preserving that convention. Before global initialization or custom-client loading, the exact Linux --service supervisor and --server --service-owned-server child MUST change to the fixed root directory / and terminate if that operation fails. The supervisor's child command MUST also set / explicitly before spawn, so the local launch API itself carries no inherited-cwd default. Custom-client loading on every build configuration MUST derive its optional custom.txt only from the absolute current-executable directory (the application Resources directory on macOS) or accept the embedding's explicit in-memory payload; it MUST NOT give a debug build a cwd-relative override or fallback. Ed25519 validity proves publisher authenticity, not that a privileged launcher's ambient cwd selected the intended deployed identity: a signed payload can set APP_NAME before Linux service config and IPC namespaces are derived. A networkless actual-debug-binary lifecycle test MUST launch both service roles from an attacker-owned directory containing a malformed custom.txt, prove both live /proc/<pid>/cwd links are /, and prove the ambient sidecar was not consumed. Ordinary user-owned viewer/server cwd behavior is otherwise unchanged.
R-S11nMUST Linux service-owned roles discard inherited non-stdio descriptor authority before initialization
An open file descriptor is a live kernel capability, not merely process metadata: Linux preserves open descriptors across execve unless FD_CLOEXEC is set, so a root-open file, directory, device, socket, or namespace handle can remain usable after the service child drops uid even when ordinary pathname permission would deny reopening it. Systemd and common privilege launchers normally close or mark unrelated descriptors close-on-exec, but the supported manual/sudo/SysV/OpenRC/runit boundary MUST NOT rely on every launcher doing so. The exact Linux --service supervisor and --server --service-owned-server final image MUST close every inherited descriptor above stderr before cwd, global, custom-client, configuration, IPC, or network initialization; descriptors 0–2 remain the launcher's deliberate process-I/O contract. Every service-child pre-exec hook MUST first mark the complete non-stdio descriptor range close-on-exec before credential changes. The one temporary exception is the already-opened, identity-checked exact executable descriptor used to prevent path substitution for an active-user child: only the forked child may clear its FD_CLOEXEC bit, and the final RustDesk image MUST close it immediately after validating the owning supervisor. The fast path MUST use Linux close_range; an unavailable or rejected fast path MUST fall back to raw, post-fork-safe close/fcntl syscalls over the canonical kernel nr_open bound, fail closed on any descriptor it cannot close or mark, and allocate, lock, or resolve no names after fork. A networkless actual-binary lifecycle test MUST inject a root-open mode-0600 file descriptor without FD_CLOEXEC into every manual service launch path and prove by device/inode inspection of live /proc/<pid>/fd entries that neither supervisor nor root/active-user/pre-pidfd child retains that capability. Ordinary user-owned viewer/server descriptor behavior is otherwise unchanged.
R-S11oMUST Linux service-originated helper launches discard inherited non-stdio descriptor authority before helper exec
Linux helper commands launched from a root/service context — including the sudo -E env probe, run_as_user tray/CM/whiteboard launches, and the argv-only delayed reopen helper — are not the exact retained service child covered by R-S11n, but they still cross a privileged process boundary after the service may have opened IPC, listener, config, runtime-record, or filesystem descriptors. The fork MUST NOT rely on sudo's site policy, sudoers closefrom, shell behavior, or an init manager to discard those capabilities. Before executing sudo, env, or a reopen child, RustDesk MUST register a Linux post-fork/pre-exec hook that preserves descriptors 0–2, marks every descriptor above stderr close-on-exec using the same parent-resolved /proc/sys/fs/nr_open bound and raw close_range(CLOSE_RANGE_CLOEXEC)/fcntl(F_GETFD/F_SETFD) fallback as R-S11n, and fails closed if the policy cannot be configured. The exact-executable descriptor exception in R-S11n applies only to the service-owned active-user child; sudo/env/reopen helpers receive only the deliberate argv, environment, and stdio contract.
R-S11pMUST Linux service-owned polkit authorization helper launches discard inherited non-stdio descriptor authority before pkcheck exec
The Linux service-owned unattended-password path invokes the fixed trusted /usr/bin/pkcheck helper from the root service after authenticating the caller and before committing the credential. That helper is an external authorization checker, not the exact retained service child covered by R-S11n and not one of the sudo/reopen helpers enumerated by R-S11o, but it still executes from a root/service process that may hold IPC, listener, config, runtime-record, filesystem, or other descriptor capabilities. RustDesk MUST build the pkcheck command as a mutable command, null stdio, set only the deliberate action/process/interaction argv, register the same Linux post-fork/pre-exec descriptor hook used by R-S11o, and fail the service-owned password authorization closed if that hook cannot be configured. The pkcheck helper may receive only argv and descriptors 0–2; it may not inherit root-service non-stdio descriptors through the default Command/execve descriptor table.
R-S11qMUST Linux same-executable helper launches discard inherited non-stdio descriptor authority before child exec
run_me_with_env is the shared same-executable launch boundary for desktop URL/tray/whiteboard helpers and for the Linux headless connection manager. The service-owned root server deliberately uses this boundary for a same-user headless CM launch, so treating it as an ordinary viewer-only convenience would leave a privileged route outside the exact service-child, sudo/reopen, and pkcheck closures in R-S11n through R-S11p. Every Linux launch through run_me/run_me_with_env MUST register the existing parent-resolved post-fork/pre-exec descriptor hook before spawn, preserve only descriptors 0–2, mark every descriptor above stderr close-on-exec through the R-S11n close_range(CLOSE_RANGE_CLOEXEC)/fcntl implementation, and return an error without launching if the hook cannot be configured. The API has no deliberate descriptor-passing contract: argv, explicit environment additions, and stdio are its complete child authority. User-owned same-executable launches receive the same descriptor hygiene without changing their functional role. A Linux actual-child regression MUST inject an open non-close-on-exec descriptor through a real exec, prove the intermediate RustDesk test image received that exact device/inode, launch the next image through run_me_with_env, and prove the final image cannot find that device/inode in /proc/self/fd.
R-S11rMUST Every production Linux helper image has one shared, explicit descriptor allowlist
The closures in R-S11o through R-S11q covered privileged sudo, reopen, pkcheck, and same-executable routes, but a root-crate-only helper policy could not protect commands launched by hbb_common or dependent crates, and the remaining Linux loginctl, desktop-notification, xdg-screensaver, xrandr, w, systemctl, FUSE unmount, and feature-inert hardware-codec check paths still inherited every ambient descriptor lacking FD_CLOEXEC. Several are reached repeatedly by the root service while selecting a desktop or managing the installed service. Every ordinary production Linux Command launch MUST use one shared hbb_common pre-exec policy that resolves and canonically validates /proc/sys/fs/nr_open in the parent, validates a duplicate-free non-stdio descriptor allowlist in the parent, marks the entire range above stderr close-on-exec in the forked child using close_range(CLOSE_RANGE_CLOEXEC) with a raw fcntl(F_GETFD/F_SETFD) fallback, and only then clears FD_CLOEXEC on each explicitly allowed descriptor. The default API MUST pass an empty allowlist, so argv, explicit environment, and descriptors 0–2 are the whole child contract. The only ordinary-helper exception is clipboard fusermount: its _FUSE_COMMFD Unix socket is a protocol input and MUST be the sole non-stdio allowlisted descriptor. RustDesk MUST NOT clear that socket's FD_CLOEXEC bit in the multithreaded parent; only the post-fork child may make it inheritable immediately before exec. Stdio, duplicate, and out-of-range allowlist entries MUST be rejected, and a real multi-image Linux regression MUST compare device/inode identity to prove the explicit descriptor survives while the same descriptor is absent under the default policy. The specialized exact service-child executable-descriptor handoff remains governed separately by R-S11n.
R-S12MUST No insecure build/runtime configuration is reachable
No feature flag, env var, config key, or CLI flag may disable/weaken the PAKE, the encryption, the no-follow write guarantee (R-S8), or the §9 assertions. Such controls MUST be removed, not defaulted off. For the controlled side's security-policy options specifically, R-S16 is the realization of this principle: the policy is a compile-time table the Config::get_option funnel returns and the is_option_can_save guard rejects, so no key can be defaulted-permissive or written back on.
R-S13MUST The initiator (viewer) is held to the responder's bar
§1 puts both roles in scope; this requirement governs the initiator (viewer) role — present on every box, since any box can connect out, including the exposed jump-host that views an inner box (§17) — whose connecting side must meet the same keyed-before-any-message guarantee as the responder (§4.4); the inherited initiator path does not, and keying the responder (R-S1) does not fix it. (A box running purely as --server never initiates, so this surface is unreachable there; (a)–(d) are how the initiator path closes it whenever a box views.) The initiator path MUST: (a) delete the unkeyed direct-IP / domain:port early-returns (client.rs) and route every initiation — direct, rendezvous, relay — through the CPace initiator handshake (R-P14), so a usable Stream is never returned without a confirmed PAKE; (b) assert is_secured() before the viewer dispatches or tunnels any byte — promoting the inherited informational check (io_loop.rs, today only a UI lock-icon) to a fail-closed abort, the initiator analog of R-A1; (c) decode only the dedicated Cpace message pre-key on the client path (parse_from_bytes::<Cpace>, replacing the full-union Message::parse_from_bytes at client.rs), and call set_max_packet_length to the small pre-PAKE bound before the first read (R-S7); and (d) connect TCP-only — gating mod kcp_stream out (R-D4) and removing the udp_nat_connect punch path (client.rs) so the kcp-sys C parser is absent in this role too. Residual (universal; documented, not closable by keying): a box deliberately connected to a hostile but password-correct peer feeds attacker bytes to the in-process native decoders (§4.4); this is bounded operationally by the deep-link confirmation and channel restriction of R-X6, not by the PAKE (Appendix C #2b), and the fork SHOULD close it by sandboxing the decode path. The TCP-only choice costs UDP NAT traversal — acceptable for the static-IP direct deployment (§17), where connecting-by-address (R-F3) is preserved over TCP.
R-S14MUST Screen capture is bound to a PAKE-authenticated session — a reused grant must not capture outside one
The security boundary on screen capture is the PAKE (R-S1/R-A1), not the OS consent dialog — a distinction that matters because this fork targets unattended access (R-S9). On Android, MediaProjection consent is a system dialog the user must physically tap and which cannot be auto-granted, so the inherited token reuse (MainService keeps the projection alive across session end via reuseVirtualDisplay/setSurface(null), MainService.kt) avoids a re-consent tap per connection where the OS permits it — bounded, since the projection is owned by the foreground service (acquired on start, and released with the VirtualDisplay, ImageReader, and Surface on service teardown — R-D7a) and Android-14 per-session re-consent and Android-15 auto-stop-on-lock cap what unattended reuse achieves. The requirement is not re-consent but binding: capture MUST begin only inside a confirmed PAKE session — no held grant (the Android projection token, or the Wayland xdg-portal restore-token at libs/scrap/…/wayland/pipewire.rs) may drive capture pre-authentication, and the held grant MUST NOT be reachable by any non-authenticated path. The persisted Wayland restore-token is a local at-rest exposure (a local reader could lift it) in the same §2 endpoint-compromise-out-of-scope class as the UUID-obfuscated config (Appendix C #14); the fork SHOULD encrypt it at rest and release the projection when the service is not running (and on the §17 Xorg build, R-X12 compiles the Wayland capture path out entirely, making this half absent by construction). (Re-prompting per session MUST NOT be added where it would break unattended access. The §17 Xorg box has no capture-consent gate at all — it captures for the authenticated peer by design, the intended unattended behavior — so this requirement is about where the gate is: the PAKE, not an OS dialog.) macOS source: the held-grant twin is the persistent TCC.db Screen-Recording/Accessibility grant — there is no fork-stored token (so the "encrypt at rest" half is N/A), but the grant is app-wide because Release.entitlements ships the App Sandbox off, held by a KeepAlive, login-window-resident --server. Capture is already PAKE-bound on macOS through the shared lazy-on-subscribe path, so the obligations are to keep that binding, minimize the entitlement set (the full per-key allow-list spans Release.entitlements, Profile.entitlements, and Debug.entitlements; cs.allow-jit is debug-only, while Profile and Release are AOT and carry no JIT exception), and ensure a --server restart re-enters capture only through a fresh PAKE session (the macOS analog of Android's START_NOT_STICKY). iOS: no capture, so N/A.
R-S15MUST The viewer's in-session message handling is a config-integrity boundary — a keyed hostile peer may not write the viewer's PeerConfig
§1 puts the initiator in scope and R-S13 keys it (whenever a box views; a box running purely as --server performs no client dispatch, so this surface is unreachable there) — but keying is where the spec stopped: a correctly-keyed but hostile peer (one the viewer was induced to connect to, §2/§4.4) then sends ordinary in-session messages that reach the viewer's persistent PeerConfig. Verified: Misc::BackNotification→PrivacyModeState wrote the peer's unbounded impl_key string under privacy-mode-impl-key (io_loop.rs) with no user click, and could also flip the saved privacy-mode boolean without proving the viewer requested a privacy transition; TerminalResponse.service_id and the peer's PeerInfo username/hostname/platform are persisted on every connect (client.rs) — all via save_config; and PeerInfo.version/platform formerly selected or rewrote the persisted PeerConfig.keyboard_mode. This is the initiator twin of R-S11, whose config-write allowlist is written IPC-only and never inspects the client dispatch. The fork MUST extend R-S11's single-allowlist chokepoint to io_loop.rs: route every peer-message-driven config write through a validated allowlist (privacy-mode-impl-key rejected unless it is in the compile-time get_supported_privacy_mode_impl() set; PrivacyModeState persistence admitted only when it matches a receiver-owned pending local outbound TogglePrivacyMode or legacy OptionMessage.privacy_mode request for the default remote-control session; PeerInfo strings length-bounded; peer-derived keyboard-mode compatibility computed only as runtime fallback and never persisted), and CI/AST-assert (R-A6) that no Config::set*/save_config is reachable from a peer-message arm except through it. (Severity is config-poisoning, not RCE — the impl map is compile-time and keyboard mode is enum-bounded — but it is the persistent-write class the §4.4 audit found that survives a correctly-keyed peer, so it is closed, not accepted as the §2 native-decoder residual is.)
R-S16MUST The controlled side enforces ONE compile-time policy at the config funnel — never a permissive default, a peer/server field, or a runtime write
Principle. The controlled side draws every security decision from exactly two input classes: config options — almost all resolved through the funnel Config::get_option (hbb_common/src/config.rs → get_or; precedence OVERWRITE_SETTINGS → config-file → DEFAULT_SETTINGS → ""), with a handful in a second namespace read via get_builtin_option (closed in (d)(iv)) — and peer/rendezvous protobuf (the control_permissions blob and the LoginRequest credential fields). RustDesk makes every one of these fail open: option2bool returns true for any unset enable-* key (config.rs), verification_method() → UseBothPasswords and approve_mode() → Both on empty (password_security.rs), and permission() honors a rendezvous-server-supplied bit before any local policy (connection.rs). Policy MUST therefore be a property of the artifact — a compile-time constant the program enforces unconditionally — not a default that can be unset, wrong, peer-supplied, or locally overwritten. The security resolvers are left unmodified (verification_method()/approve_mode()/Permission::terminal are not rewritten to return a hand-rolled constant): the funnel pin makes them return the policy transparently — a single-edit closure rather than a per-call-site rewrite.
  • (a) One source of truth. A pub const PINNED_SETTINGS: &[(&str, &str)] in config.rs — the idiom RustDesk already uses for RS_PUB_KEY/RENDEZVOUS_PORT (config.rs) — unconditional: the fork is one build and is always hardened, so the policy is enforced by every artifact, never behind a feature flag. It pins only Config server-settings keys (KEYS_SETTINGS); the parallel LocalConfig map (viewer UI prefs) is untouched. The table — opinionated and exact — is the box's entire security policy in one auditable place: 
    verification-method = use-permanent-password   # was: unset → UseBothPasswords (accept either credential)
approve-mode        = password                 # was: unset → Both (silent click-to-accept)
access-mode         = full                     # complete control for the authenticated owner — the one mode
enable-keyboard=Y  enable-clipboard=Y  enable-file-transfer=Y  enable-audio=Y  enable-camera=Y   # the content channels a remote-control box needs (§4.3)
enable-terminal=Y  enable-tunnel=Y             # full control for the authenticated owner (R-X8, R-D6)
enable-remote-restart=Y  enable-record-session=Y  enable-block-input=Y  enable-privacy-mode=Y  enable-virtual-display=N
                                               # the rest of full control — granted to the authenticated owner
enable-trusted-devices=N  2fa=  bot=           # no 2FA-bypass, no TOTP, no Telegram push (R-X7, R-D6)
api-server=  custom-rendezvous-server=  relay-server=  proxy-url=  proxy-username=  proxy-password=  key= # egress-silent; no proxy credentials or trust-anchor override (R-D6/R-S11b)
allow-websocket=N  allow-insecure-tls-fallback=N  allow-linux-headless=N  # R-D6, R-X14
stop-service=N  allow-only-conn-window-open=                              # service un-killable by a local write (R-X9); never self-DoS a headless box
    Every capability the authenticated owner exercises is Y — full control is the box's single mode (§17 sovereignty: the CPace peer is the trusted owner who already holds the OS/sudo password, §2); the only Ns left are the sovereignty pins — egress-empty, no 2FA, no insecure transport — a different class entirely (R-D6/R-X7/R-X14), while empty pins also remove stored egress endpoints, proxy credentials, and the legacy trust-anchor override. An operator who needs a different policy edits the const and rebuilds (the R-F4 build-time-choice discipline) — never a runtime knob. (direct-server/direct-access-port are not here: R-D4 makes the listener unconditional and R-F4 pins the port as a code literal, not an option.)
  • (b) Pin the read funnel. At the head of Config::get_option (config.rs), before the get_or lookup, return the pinned value if the key is in PINNED_SETTINGS. This makes every config-driven resolver return the policy with no edit to any of them — verification_method(), approve_mode(), option2bool("enable-*") inside is_permission_enabled_locally (connection.rs), is_headless_allowed(), the egress reads — and covers every un-enumerated KEYS_SETTINGS security key for free, because that funnel is singular for the KEYS_SETTINGS namespace — a second option-read funnel (get_builtin_option, the KEYS_BUILDIN_SETTINGS namespace) exists and is closed in (d)(iv). The whole-map read is the same funnel: Config::get_options MUST overlay PINNED_SETTINGS last before returning an effective option map to the UI, CLI, or typed main-IPC MainStatusSnapshot cache, so broad reads cannot observe a stale default/stored/signed-custom value for a pinned key and cannot hide a pinned-empty key as "absent." Strictly stronger than RustDesk's own OVERWRITE_SETTINGS, which is a runtime RwLock populated only if a RustDesk-Ed25519-signed custom.txt loads (common.rs) — a fork cannot sign it, and it can be left empty or mutated in-process; a const can be neither.
  • (c) Pin the write funnel. In is_option_can_save (config.rs — the single guard already gating both set_option and set_options/purify_options), return false for any pinned key, reusing the existing drop-and-purge-from-the-persisted-file behavior. A local process, a typed MainIpcRequest::SetOptions write (R-S11), or a server-pushed config merge (R-X3) thus cannot set a pinned option key — structurally, with no allowlist lookup for this subset. Scope: this guards only the options map; credential, salt, legacy identity, and unlock-pin state are struct fields, not options, so credential mutation and any whole-struct import would bypass is_option_can_save entirely. Those surfaces are absent from the ordinary config protocol and belong to R-S11's narrow receiver-authorized operations, not this guard.
  • (d) Close the two gates that bypass get_option. (i) permission() (connection.rs) consults the rendezvous-server control_permissions blob before local policy. The mediator is excised (R-D4) so it is always None (rendezvous_mediator.rs) and (b) governs the fall-through — but the fork MUST also add an early return Self::is_permission_enabled_locally(opt) at the head of permission() that skips the control_permissions branch entirely, closing the server-push vector by construction, not merely by the mediator's absence. (The #[cfg(feature="flutter")] access-mode shortcut in is_permission_enabled_locally is present — the one binary ships flutter — so the access-mode = full pin (a) governs it: the grant-everything shortcut returns true for every capability, with the explicit enable-* = Y pins as the belt behind it — any path that consults an enable-* directly still resolves to grant.) (ii) The local CM ipc::Data::SwitchPermission handler (connection.rs) reassigns conn.keyboard/clipboard/audio/file/… at runtime, bypassing permission(); once the policy is fixed a runtime re-widener has no place, so the handler arm MUST be removed (the headless --service has no CM to drive it anyway; structural, not merely covered by R-S11's allowlist). The peer's inbound disable_* overlays (connection.rs) only ever restrict the cached booleans, so they need no change. (iii) The SOCKS/proxy state is the one structured config that bypasses the string-option funnel: get_socks() reads OVERWRITE_SETTINGS/CONFIG2.socks/DEFAULT_SETTINGS directly (config.rs) and get_network_type() likewise, while set_socks writes the structured CONFIG2.socks field — so neither (b)'s read pin nor (c)'s write guard reaches it. The fork MUST therefore make get_socks()/get_network_type() return None/Direct and set_socks() inert at those accessors directly; the table pins proxy-url and the proxy credential keys for any string-option reader, but that is not sufficient alone because the structured field bypasses the string map. This keeps CheckTestNatType's is_direct constant so the IPC Drop STUN-probe never fires (R-D6/R-S11). (iv) A second option-read funnel exists outside Config::get_option: get_builtin_option (common.rs) reads the BUILTIN_SETTINGS map for the KEYS_BUILDIN_SETTINGS namespace (config.rs), which the (b) pin does not reach. The security-relevant builtin keys read on the controlled path are one-way-file-transfer (connection.rs — the directionality of an authenticated peer's file pull), allow-logon-screen-password (feeding the approve_mode==Click decision), one-way-clipboard-redirection, and enable-perm-change-in-accept-window (ui_cm_interface.rs). BUILTIN_SETTINGS is populated only from the same RustDesk-signed custom.txt a fork cannot produce, so on a fork build it is empty and these take their safe defaults — not a fail-open reachable by a non-password-knower — but it is a real second funnel the pin must account for. The fork MUST (1) assert BUILTIN_SETTINGS is empty at startup (R-A4 — no managed override leaked in), and (2) if it ever pins a KEYS_BUILDIN_SETTINGS value (e.g. one-way-file-transfer=Y to make the root box push-only), mirror the PINNED_SETTINGS consult into get_builtin_option too — the Config::get_option pin alone does nothing for these keys. (v) A third non-get_option store closes the set: HARD_SETTINGS (config.rs), read by is_incoming_only/is_outgoing_only (the conn-type key, gating real behavior at client.rs/rendezvous_mediator.rs) and get_hard_option (exposed to Dart via main_get_hard_option) — and, more pointedly, a second at-rest credential store: get_preset_password_storage_and_salt reads HARD_SETTINGS's password/salt, and validate_password (connection.rs)/has_permanent_password (config.rs) fall through to that preset when the local store is empty. Like BUILTIN_SETTINGS, it is written only by the RustDesk-signed read_custom_client (common.rs, gated on sign::verify with the embedded key), so a fork build leaves it empty — but the fork MUST assert it empty at startup (R-A4) for the same reason as BUILTIN_SETTINGS, which removes the conn-type funnel and the preset-password credential vector by construction (and reconciles R-S9's single-h1 at-rest model, which this preset branch otherwise contradicts). (This is also why reusing RustDesk's existing OVERWRITE_SETTINGS precedence would be insufficient: it too sits only on the get_option path — the decisive requirement is intercepting every read funnel — Config::get_option, get_builtin_option, get_socks, permission()'s control_permissions, the LocalConfig namespace (R-SV10), and HARD_SETTINGS ((v)) — not just the get_option path.)
The credential storage format (the password-equivalent PRS the controlled side holds) is R-S9/R-P1's concern; (a)'s verification-method pin fixes only the policy that selects it. The single self.authorized = true edge (connection.rs) collapses onto the PAKE KEYED success (R-S2). R-A6 proves the whole mechanism complete — that the keys are pinned, that the funnels consult the pin, and that the resolvers reach config only through Config::get_option (so none is rewritten to a divergent constant).
R-S17RETIRED Host-key pin / known_hosts — retired; the fork is pure password-PAKE, with no per-box identity
Withdrawn. Earlier drafts added a mandatory viewer-side host-key pin (an SSH-style known_hosts over the box's self-generated Ed25519 key, delivered as a channel-bound HostIdentity possession-proof and compared on every reconnect). It is removed entirely — the ID is retired, not reassigned, and R-S18/R-S19 keep their numbers. Why it added nothing in scope: the threat model (§2) is unlimited active MITM, and a balanced PAKE over a secret password already defeats that by construction — an attacker who does not know the password cannot key, impersonate, relay-inject, or MITM, and a non-password-knowing substitute fails the PAKE closed (§2/§4.4). The pin did not add to MITM defense (CPace already provides it) and did not stop online guessing (the salt is public and the viewer reveals its CPace confirmation tag before any host-proof); its only job was catching a substitute box that already knows the password (password reuse across boxes, a leak, or prior compromise) — and password secrecy is assumed, so that threat is out of scope (§2). Defending only an out-of-scope threat, at the cost of a cross-platform GUI pin store, a first-contact/mismatch dialog ceremony, a headless --get-fingerprint bootstrap, and an extra signed frame in the handshake, was not worth its complexity. Consequences, folded into the live spec: the PRS salt is now a fixed domain-separation constant, no longer the host-key fingerprint (R-P1); "no long-term identities / no PKI" holds unconditionally, with no local-pin carve-out (R-P5); the password is the sole authenticator and sole substitution defense (R-S9/R-S18); and the host-proof KAT, the pin dialogs (ex-R-G5), the --get-fingerprint CLI, and the known_hosts store are gone (R-A6/R-A10/§19). No live requirement in this document mandates a host key, a fingerprint pin, or a host-proof.
R-S18MUST One authentication, zero second OS-credential paths — the same class on every platform, removed symmetrically from client and server (primarily server)
The audit that proves the thesis. All five session types funnel to the lone self.authorized = true (verified: a single assignment site, connection.rs), and the type — Remote/FileTransfer/ViewCamera/PortForward/Terminal — is resolved after it as a capability tag. So remote control, file transfer, clipboard/CLIPRDR, audio/voice, camera and port-forward are free: pure post-auth capabilities gated only by their enable-*/Permission::* flag, the same shared fs.rs/codec on every platform, carrying no credential of their own (verified — fs.rs has zero pam/logon/elevate). They are kept, and there is no security argument to touch them. Exactly one class violates "one authentication": a second OS credential — a peer-supplied OS username/password driving a real OS authentication distinct from the PAKE — appearing as the same finding in three platform skins, which the fork MUST remove from the tree (every build, structurally — not merely disabled by config): (i) the terminal OS-login (Windows real / Linux stub→root PTY) — prepare_terminal_login_for_authorization, should_use_terminal_os_login_scope, the peer-credential branch of terminal session-user selection, the FailureScope::TerminalOsLogin limiter scope, the LogonUserW/CreateProcessWithLogonW token path (R-X8); (ii) the os_login→PAM desktop-start (Linux) — linux_desktop_manager::try_start_x_session → pam::Client…authenticate() (R-X14); (iii) the peer-triggered elevation-with-logon (Windows) — ElevationRequest::Logon → StartPara::Logon(username,password) → CreateProcessWithLogonW (R-X9). R-X8/R-X9/R-X14 are the platform excisions of this one requirement, unified here. Symmetric removal — server primary, client too. The exposure is the server (controlled) side, which receives these from a network peer, so its receive/process code is the primary cut. But each is protobuf-mediated and the viewer sends them, and that sender is a real hazard, not dead weight: the viewer persists OS credentials per peer (os-username/os-password options) and auto-sends them on every connect as LoginRequest.os_login (client.rs — os_username/os_password = get_option("os-username"/"os-password")), plus a stable hardware fingerprint hwid when trust-this-device — so a malicious or substituted peer (a different box answering at the same address) harvests the operator's stored OS credentials automatically, a viewer-side credential leak the server-only audit cannot see (Appendix C #22). The fork MUST therefore delete the client senders too: the os_login: Some(OSLogin…) build (client.rs), the Data::ElevateDirect/Data::ElevateWithLogon → set_logon(ElevationRequestWithLogon) path (io_loop.rs), the set_switch_sides_response send (client.rs), the persisted os-username/os-password options and the hwid emission, and the viewer UI that drives them (the "Request Elevation" toolbar menu + showRequestElevationDialog + elevationModel, and the OS-account login dialog — §19/R-G4). Delete the protocol fields so neither side can speak a second credential (message.proto, hbb_common): LoginRequest.password (field 2 — the PAKE replaces the challenge-response, R-S2/R-S6), LoginRequest.os_login (field 12) + the OSLogin message, LoginRequest.hwid (field 14), ElevationRequest/ElevationRequestWithLogon, SwitchSidesRequest/SwitchSidesResponse, Auth2fa, and the Hash oracle — R-A6 greps each absent (one build). The bypasses fold into the one edge: the alternate paths that reach authorized without a credential — SwitchSidesResponse's 10-s UUID (which also sets from_switch to skip 2FA), the 30-s is_recent_session re-admit, and the "2FA bypassed by trusted devices" hwid path (connection.rs) — are deleted with the class (R-S2); the CM ipc::Data::Authorize operator-accept survives only as the attended GUI accept, downstream of the PAKE (the headless --service shows no CM window). No verification beyond the password: with the host-key pin retired (ex-R-S17), the mutual PAKE on the shared password is the only authentication and the sole gate — there is no second check, credentialed or otherwise, to bind into the channel or to relay around, so the compound-/tunneled-authentication class cannot arise. The standing invariant: access is the password alone, and nothing beyond the one PAKE-keyed channel confers or gates it (never a second credential, never a separate channel). After this, authorized == KEYED is literally true — one edge, no second credential anywhere in the tree or on the wire, on any platform.
R-S19MUST Capability confinement by AuthConnType — every peer-triggerable capability keys on the session type, never on a decoupled per-capability flag
The class R-S2 leaves open inside an authorized session. R-S2 collapses whether a peer is authorized onto the one CPace KEYED edge; this requirement pins what an authorized session may do. RustDesk seeds a set of per-capability booleans — keyboard, clipboard, audio, file, restart, recording, block_input, privacy_mode — from the local permission policy independent of the session's authorized connection type (AuthConnType::{Remote,FileTransfer,ViewCamera,Terminal,PortForward}, resolved from the LoginRequest oneof), and the post-auth handlers gate on those booleans (or the broad self.authorized state) rather than on the AuthConnType. Only keyboard is ever force-cleared, and only for terminal/view-camera — so a narrow session type reaches capabilities outside its granted scope. This is CWE-863 (incorrect authorization), the shape of CVE-2026-58056 (a FileTransfer authorization injecting input and capturing the screen — Appendix C #24) — and the same shape recurs at every flag-gated sink the message allowlist does not cover: host clipboard-text write, peer→host audio playback, block_input, privacy toggle, and remote-restart. Under the fork's pinned access-mode=full (R-S16) every permission resolves true, so the per-capability booleans provide ZERO session-type confinement — the AuthConnType is the only real boundary. The fork therefore MUST make every peer-triggerable capability/action a function of the authorized AuthConnType, not a decoupled boolean: (a) the post-auth dispatcher gates INPUT (mouse/key/pointer) to Remote only and desktop DISPLAY capture/control (screenshot, capture/switch-display, resolution, refresh, virtual-display, privacy, restart) to Remote-or-ViewCamera — an AuthConnType allowlist, load-bearing because screen capture has no capability flag to clear; (b) the capability booleans MUST be derived from the AuthConnType at authorization time — computed before any peer-supplied LoginRequest option is applied — so no handler can contradict the session type and no login-time option can transiently re-grant a cleared capability (an ordering the ad-hoc per-branch clears did not guarantee); and (c) the flag-gated sinks the message allowlist does not cover — host clipboard-text write (kept distinct from the FileTransfer file-clipboard, which legitimately needs clipboard), peer→host audio playback (gated Remote-or-active-voice-call), block_input, privacy toggle, and remote-restart — MUST additionally key on the AuthConnType. This holds on EVERY platform: the sinks and their cfg-gating differ per target (block_input is a live local-input DoS on Windows and a no-op stub on Linux; virtual-display/privacy are Windows-cfg; the input backends differ) — the confinement is correct only if it binds the capability to the AuthConnType on Windows, macOS, Android, iOS and Linux alike, not merely where a given sink happens to have effect. R-A6 MUST gate the derivation and the sink confinement so the class cannot regress. Principle: once the booleans are a pure function of AuthConnType, a handler physically cannot grant a capability the session type was not authorized for — the CWE-863 class is closed by construction, not patched sink-by-sink. (All instances are moot under §2 — the CPace password-holder is the trusted owner — so this is defense-in-depth / least-privilege coherence, not a §2-blocking exposure; it is nonetheless normative, because an "actually-secure" fork must not carry the upstream scattered-flag authorization model.)
R-S11eMUST Helper responses carry exact operation authority, never arbitrary authenticated messages
A helper response is itself an authority-bearing operation. A connection manager or other helper MUST NOT select an arbitrary authenticated peer message by returning serialized top-level protobuf bytes. It may return only a closed typed result for the operation the owning connection authorized. Every response MUST bind the exact connection ID, a fresh per-session token, a monotonic job generation or one-shot request generation, the operation kind and path, and the receiver-owned current phase; stale, cross-kind, cross-session, reused-ID, and out-of-phase responses fail closed without consuming another pending authority. A failed helper-channel enqueue MUST retire the pending request/job authority and return a bounded peer error rather than leaving advanced state live. Peer-chosen CM job IDs MUST NOT be reused during a connection because the peer wire protocol carries no generation that could distinguish delayed confirmations. Reads MUST track monotonic current-file progression plus an exact digest-confirmation phase. Writes MUST represent a pending digest check as an exclusive phase, reject blocks and successful completion during that phase, and retain final write authority until the helper acknowledges finalization. The owning Connection is the sole constructor of peer-facing protobuf messages. Structured CM frames MUST have an aggregate decoder ceiling. Bulk bytes may bypass structured IPC encoding only as the payload of a typed file-block header whose FileTransfer mode, connection, and session token were validated first; the decoder's file-block ceiling and a finite read deadline MUST be engaged before reading its length header, and exact job generation/current-file phase MUST be validated before constructing peer traffic.
R-S11fMUST Windows Installer is the sole Windows machine-state authority
Windows Installer is the sole owner of Program Files payloads, the LocalSystem service, firewall exceptions, machine registry state, machine shortcuts, and creation plus ACL provisioning of the machine credential root at ProgramData\<Product>\config. No runtime process may create or repair that root. The application binary MUST NOT expose install, uninstall, after-install, before-uninstall, service-install, certificate-cleanup, or driver-cleanup execution modes that mutate that state; the Flutter application MUST NOT expose an in-app installation route. The setup executable is a fixed bootstrapper, not a second installer: it accepts only an empty command line, the exact silent-install option, or an internal protected marker that is rejected unless the process is elevated; its embedded payload contains exactly one fixed-name MSI; its elevated leg resolves msiexec.exe from GetSystemDirectoryW, passes the staged MSI as an explicit argument without a shell, and accepts only Windows Installer completion 0 or reboot-required 3010. Failure to resolve the running executable or match the closed command grammar is fatal. It MUST NOT load or execute an extracted application EXE or DLL, derive privileged behavior from the caller's current application image, accept a renamed legacy installer mode, or preserve a compatibility fallback. Service creation/start/stop/removal and inbound firewall authorization MUST be declarative rollback-aware MSI resources bound to the package-owned application file; basename process termination and hand-written SCM/firewall custom actions are forbidden. A custom action is permitted only for a fixed operation with no caller-selected command or target where Windows Installer has no declarative equivalent, such as bounded cleanup of the fork's exact test certificate or exact runtime-generated broker file. Uninstall and repair are MSI transactions; there is no generated batch/VBS program, public writable command staging, raw uninstall-string replay, or application-side UAC path.
R-S19aMUST Remote input execution is owned by one authenticated connection until joined teardown
R-S19's Remote-only authority must remain true through asynchronous execution, not only message dispatch. On Windows, Linux, and macOS a worker may start only after authorization resolves to AuthConnType::Remote; FileTransfer, Terminal, PortForward, ViewCamera, and unauthenticated connections own no input thread. Each Remote connection owns one bounded item/byte queue, one worker, and one shared cancellation/execution gate. Mode/union consistency, structural event limits, serialized size, and nonblocking admission are mandatory. Cancellation and dispatch admission use one atomic state transition, so no input begins after cancellation. A supervisor is created before its worker, receives the sole JoinHandle by value, and publishes completion; normal async teardown waits only on that completion off-runtime, while cancellation-time Drop returns without blocking the executor. Teardown drains queued values without execution. Every held key is resolved through the injector's backend-canonical physical identity; protocol-mode labels are not ownership identities, and semantic text/sequence actions are not represented as held keys. One process-wide arbiter serializes ownership changes with physical dispatch: only aggregate 0 -> 1 injects down and only 1 -> 0 injects up. A physical transition remains represented until its native call succeeds, so unwind cleanup can retry. Key, mouse, pointer, gesture, lock-screen, temporary-modifier, and teardown paths preserve modifiers owned by another connection under that same arbiter. BlockInput is an aggregate connection lease; one connection's off or teardown may not revoke another's lease. macOS global input state initializes only for the first Remote worker and owned dispatch is synchronous to its required queue; Linux global remaps clear only after the final Remote worker exits. Special keys enter the same validated bounded worker path, and Windows Ctrl+Alt+Del uses only service-owned SAS authority. Privacy mode may obscure or replace the served display but MUST NOT implicitly acquire local-input suppression authority; on macOS the gamma/display blackout has no CGEventTap callback or run-loop source. Explicit block-input remains a separate Remote-only, connection-owned capability. Android performs its direct JNI path only after validating both the received event and any rewritten event; iOS has no controlled-side input worker. Platform gates MUST NOT claim the desktop queue on Android/iOS, but all platforms MUST preserve Remote-only AuthConnType authority at their actual injection sink.
R-S11sMUST Fatal memory-integrity signals keep the operating system's default disposition
A synchronous SIGSEGV may interrupt any thread while an allocator, lock, logger, configuration writer, native input backend, or process-launch implementation holds inconsistent state. POSIX specifies behavior only for the async-signal-safe function set and leaves unsafe interrupted-handler combinations undefined; its sigaction contract calls out allocator/stdio-backed operations as the canonical unsafe class. Rust's normal process::exit additionally runs platform exit handlers, while POSIX's default fatal-signal action records abnormal signal termination and may preserve a core image. RustDesk therefore MUST NOT install a SIGSEGV callback on Linux, macOS, or Windows release builds. Fatal-signal context may not symbolize a backtrace, allocate, log, lock or write config, mutate graphics/codec options, release synthetic input through native backends, invoke a callback stored in mutable global state, launch a notification process, or translate the fault into exit(0). The operating system's default disposition is the complete policy: it preserves truthful failure status, supervisor Restart=on-failure behavior, and platform crash diagnostics without executing Rust code in a potentially corrupted process. Graceful SIGTERM/SIGINT handling remains on the separately owned service/direct-server shutdown paths and is not a fatal-signal recovery mechanism. The obsolete callback API, its sole input-cleanup callback, crash-notification helper/example, and direct backtrace dependency MUST be absent, with a source/mutation gate preventing their return.
R-S11tMUST Every production macOS child image excludes ambient non-stdio descriptors
Darwin preserves a descriptor across exec unless FD_CLOEXEC is set. Pinned Rust 1.75 Command uses macOS posix_spawn without Apple's close-by-default extension and falls back to fork/exec when a pre_exec closure is registered. RustDesk's LaunchDaemon authorization check, service install/uninstall, LaunchAgent management, same-executable CM/whiteboard/tray launch, lock-state/screen helpers, feature-inert codec checker, and portable-PTY terminal shell therefore MUST all enforce a stdio-only child-image boundary. Ordinary Command paths apply one shared hbb_common::platform::macos policy before status, output, or spawn. The parent MUST obtain and validate RLIMIT_NOFILE (whose soft limit is Apple's documented current maximum number of open files), reject zero, infinity, and limits above the fork's bounded one-million-descriptor operating envelope, and enumerate /dev/fd so descriptors inherited above a subsequently lowered soft limit are still covered. The post-fork closure may perform only the async-signal-safe fcntl(F_GETFD/F_SETFD) operation: every live descriptor above stderr is marked close-on-exec, EBADF alone means an absent descriptor, any other error aborts the launch, and the parent's descriptor table remains unchanged. The exact pinned portable-pty 0.8.1 source MUST be owned in-tree with provenance to RustDesk's WezTerm fork commit 80174f8009f41565f0fa8c66dab90d4f9211ae16. Its Unix PTY spawn MUST NOT allocate, enumerate the filesystem, silently ignore errors, or directly close arbitrary descriptors after fork; it prepares the finite limit and descriptor snapshot in the parent, marks every non-stdio descriptor close-on-exec with raw fcntl in the existing pre-exec closure, and preserves Rust's internal exec-error pipe until successful exec so a failed shell image is reported to the parent. No production macOS child protocol currently requires a non-stdio descriptor, so there is no allowlist or compatibility exception: argv, explicitly selected environment, and descriptors 0–2 are the complete authority. The unused hbb_common::platform::macos::alert surface and its osascript 0.3 dependency MUST remain absent because that dependency owned an inaccessible PATH-selected subprocess launch. Actual-child regressions MUST inject descriptor 9 through separate launcher images, prove its exact device/inode in intermediate images, and prove the final ordinary-command and actual PTY child images exclude that object; a separate PTY regression MUST prove a post-fork exec error reaches the parent. Source conformance may stage these tests but may not substitute for native Apple execution. Source and mutation gates MUST bind both primitives, every enumerated production launch family including the terminal service, all five descriptor/error regressions, dependency deletion and in-tree provenance, this requirement, Appendix C #142, and the hardening ledger. Native macOS compile/runtime evidence remains part of the exact-commit R-B2 Apple transaction and may not be inferred from source conformance.
R-S11uMUST Windows helper launch authority names the exact image, role, environment, and receiver-selected session
The controlled Windows image MUST NOT retain a public process-launch facade that accepts an arbitrary executable path, arbitrary session ID, generic argument vector, or generic environment map and then inherits the caller's service/elevated token. Historical run_exe_direct*, run_exe_in_cur_session*, run_exe_in_session*, and run_background/ShellExecuteW surfaces are forbidden even when they have no current caller: they encode caller-selected execution authority and preserve a future path around the receiver-owned service/session model. Pinned Rust 1.75 Command calls CreateProcessW with bInheritHandles=TRUE after making its standard handles inheritable; the standard-library mutex coordinates only that implementation's temporary handles, not arbitrary native handles created elsewhere in the process. Win32 documents that bInheritHandles=TRUE inherits every inheritable caller handle. A LocalSystem or cross-session helper transition therefore MUST use the fork's explicit CreateProcessAsUserW launcher with a validated absolute application path, separately quoted argv, the executable's parent as current directory, a token selected by the privileged receiver, and handle inheritance disabled. The public Windows helper API is a closed typed request, not an argument/environment facade: it may request only Tray (receiver emits exact --tray with no added environment), ConnectionManager (caller supplies only an exact base64-decoded 32-byte launch token; receiver emits --cm, the CM token key, and its own parent PID), or Whiteboard (the corresponding token-only request and receiver-emitted whiteboard role/token/parent state). There is no type-level representation for an extra role, environment key, parent PID, executable, or session. In LocalSystem mode the receiver derives its own current session and current executable before the token-switched launch. In non-System user-owned mode the same closed API may spawn only current_exe() under the same principal; it is not a privilege or session transition. The fixed SCM service-child launcher remains independent and derives its image from the fixed Program Files service root. Source and mutation gates MUST bind the closed typed policy, token validation, receiver-derived parent/current-image/current-session, disabled handle inheritance at the token launcher, deletion of every generic facade, Appendix C #143, and the hardening ledger. This closure is preventive authority minimization, not a claim that the dead APIs had a current untrusted caller or that a sensitive inheritable handle was demonstrated in the Windows service.
R-S11vMUST Windows privacy-broker lifetime and window authority are exact-process owned, never basename- or title-selected
The Windows controlled side MUST NOT discover or terminate a privacy broker by process-image basename, enumerate the machine for termination candidates, invoke taskkill /IM, or adopt a pre-existing top-level window merely because its title equals RustDeskPrivacyWindow. The installed --server can run as LocalSystem, so basename termination would apply the service token to every accessible RuntimeBroker_rustdesk.exe owned by another session, installation, test, or portable instance without proving process object, creation, parent, executable, or service generation. A title match is likewise only discovery metadata: it is not proof that the window belongs to the broker this server created. Each privacy-mode instance MUST instead create one fresh unnamed job, configure JOB_OBJECT_LIMIT_KILL_ON_JOB_CLOSE, initialize a correctly sized STARTUPINFOW, create the fixed installed broker image suspended with handle inheritance disabled, and assign that exact process to the job before DLL injection or thread resume. The instance retains the job, process handle, immutable PID, and thread handle; it commits them only after exact-process window readiness. Every pre-commit failure — invalid token, incomplete process identity, job-assignment failure, injection failure, unexpected suspend state, early process exit, or readiness timeout — drops pending ownership and terminates only that exact suspended process/tree. Closing the final job handle is normal and abnormal owner-death cleanup, including the Windows 8+ nested-job case when the service child already belongs to the SCM-owned job. Window discovery may use the fixed title only as a candidate filter and MUST additionally require GetWindowThreadProcessId to equal the retained live process PID, with retained-handle liveness rechecked after selection; a pre-existing foreign window is never adopted. The portable wrapper does not own installed-service privacy mode and MUST NOT copy or kill a broker as extraction-time compatibility work. This follows Microsoft's job-object, AssignProcessToJobObject, nested-job, and STARTUPINFOW contracts. Source and mutation gates MUST bind pending ownership, valid suspended launch, job-before-injection/resume ordering, PID-bound window selection and liveness, removal of both machine-wide cleanup paths, Appendix C #144, and the hardening ledger. Exact native Windows execution remains part of R-B2 rather than being inferred from Linux source conformance.
R-S11wMUST Windows UAC process-state decisions authenticate the fixed system image in the current process session
The non-installed Windows controlled side temporarily changes magnification privacy capture while a UAC consent prompt is active and reports that state to an authorized viewer. A machine-wide process basename is discovery metadata, not authority: another session's real consent.exe, or an ordinary same-user executable renamed to consent.exe, MUST NOT change this process's capture state. ToolHelp enumeration may identify only fixed exact-name candidates. Before a candidate can represent UAC state, the receiver MUST prove that its Remote Desktop Services session equals ProcessIdToSessionId(GetCurrentProcessId()), open that process with only PROCESS_QUERY_LIMITED_INFORMATION, retain the process handle through both the full-image query and a second same-PID session observation, and require QueryFullProcessImageNameW(..., 0) to equal the exact consent.exe path derived from GetSystemDirectoryW and the existing no-reparse trusted-system-file proof. A wrong-session or wrong-image candidate is ignored; a current-session candidate that cannot be opened and authenticated is an error, so privacy capture fails closed rather than guessing that it is the system consent process. Candidate enumeration MUST NOT confer termination, window-control, token, or launch authority. The unused substring-based get_pids/is_logon_ui probe is deleted rather than preserved as a second heuristic process-state abstraction. This follows Microsoft's ProcessIdToSessionId and QueryFullProcessImageNameW contracts and the documented UAC rule that prompts use the interactive user's secure desktop by default. Source and mutation gates MUST bind fixed-name candidate discovery, receiver-derived session, retained-handle full-image and repeated-session proof, exact System32 target, the pure path/session regression, removal of the dormant LogonUI heuristic, Appendix C #145, and the hardening ledger. Native Windows behavior remains part of R-B2.
R-S11xMUST Root-to-user helper launch is platform-owned and typed, never a generic current-image privilege bridge
A helper launch that changes credential or desktop context is an authority boundary even when the executable is fixed to current_exe(). Linux installed-service topology already owns the transition by credential-dropping the exact supervised --server --service-owned-server child before exec, while the macOS service-owned server runs as the per-user LaunchAgent; neither platform needs a second generic root process that accepts an argument vector, environment map, or caller-supplied user tuple to start CM/whiteboard. Linux therefore MUST NOT retain the sudo -E environment probe, sudo/env launch candidates, or a generic run_as_user API. The sudoers environment contract makes preservation dependent on command-line and site/PAM policy, so it is not an application-owned exact environment boundary. macOS MUST NOT use launchctl asuser as a credential drop: Apple's launchctl(1) contract says asuser adopts bootstrap, exception-server, and audit-session context but does not modify UID/GID or import the user's environment. A root Linux/macOS server that unexpectedly needs a console-user CM or whiteboard MUST fail before spawn and require the correct service supervisor/LaunchAgent topology; a headless Linux root server deliberately serving the authenticated root owner and an ordinary user-owned server retain same-principal run_me_with_env launches with their exact token/parent proof. Windows is the sole platform exception because the installed server is LocalSystem and must enter the active desktop session: its API MUST be the R-S11u typed current-image request whose caller can provide only the role-specific 32-byte launch token (or the tokenless tray variant), while the receiver derives role argv, environment keys, parent PID, current executable, and current session and disables handle inheritance at the token-switched launch. Source, Apple-conformance, and semantic mutation gates MUST bind both Unix abstraction deletions, both fail-closed call sites, the retained same-user launches, every typed Windows role/token/receiver-derived value, Appendix C #146, and the hardening ledger. This closes conceptual privileged-launch authority; source inspection found no repository path where an untrusted ordinary user selected the deleted argument/environment/user values, so it does not claim a demonstrated promptless local privilege escalation.
R-S11yMUST Linux service-owned authorization helpers receive no ambient process environment
The Linux root service's unattended-password receiver executes the fixed, metadata-authenticated /usr/bin/pkcheck image and accepts only its successful exit status as the authorization decision. The pkcheck(1) contract describes that program as a wrapper around the polkit D-Bus interface, while the D-Bus specification makes DBUS_SYSTEM_BUS_ADDRESS the environment-selected system-bus address and uses the well-known local system-bus socket only when that variable is absent. A privileged authorization subprocess therefore MUST NOT inherit the supervisor's bus selector, loader configuration, search path, home, locale, or any other ambient variable. Immediately after constructing the trusted absolute image, the receiver MUST apply Rust Command::env_clear, add no replacement environment variables, then supply only the fixed action ID, kernel-bound process subject, interaction flag, null stdio, and the R-S11p non-stdio descriptor policy before spawn. Authorization fails closed on every non-success, timeout, shutdown, identity change, or launch error as already required by R-S11b/R-S11e. An actual-child two-hop regression MUST place a hostile DBUS_SYSTEM_BUS_ADDRESS in an intermediate launcher's inherited environment, apply the production policy to the final child, and prove that the final image receives no environment entry except its test-only role marker. Source and mutation gates MUST bind the environment-clear primitive, its ordering before descriptor configuration and execution, absence of explicit replacement variables, the actual-child proof, Appendix C #147, and the hardening ledger. Packaged service-manager environments and current callers expose no proved ordinary-user-controlled route into the root supervisor environment, so this closes conceptual privileged authorization-endpoint/future-deployment authority rather than claiming a demonstrated promptless local privilege escalation.
R-S11zMUST Linux session discovery is a receiver-owned typed logind query, never ambient desktop state
The installed Linux supervisor and its service-owned child choose the active user, seat, display protocol, lock state, and replacement eligibility from logind. Those values influence which principal receives the supervised server and whether X11/Wayland-specific service behavior is admitted, so the query protocol is authority-bearing even though it is read-only. The official loginctl(1) contract identifies show-session as the computer-parsable interface, provides --property=, --no-legend, and --no-pager, defines nonzero exit as failure, and warns that inherited pager variables are an elevated-command boundary. The pinned systemd 252 and current systemd 260 implementations preserve SESSION UID USER SEAT as the first four list fields while changing the trailing presentation fields. The logind interface defines session Type as unspecified, tty, or a graphical protocol and State as online, active, or closing. The fork therefore MUST NOT expose a generic loginctl argument-vector helper, inherit the default legend or pager, inherit DBUS_SYSTEM_BUS_ADDRESS, SYSTEMD_PAGER, loader/search-path state, or any other process environment, or accept substring matches such as inactive for active. A closed query type MUST construct only local list-sessions --no-legend --no-pager or show-session --property=<enumerated read-only property> --no-pager calls, place an option terminator before the separately passed session identifier, retain the trusted absolute root-owned executable and R-S11r descriptor policy, and reject every non-success exit. The receiver MUST parse strict UTF-8, validate the stable leading SESSION UID USER SEAT fields including a canonical decimal UID, ignore version-dependent trailing list presentation fields, and require exactly-once requested Type/State/Seat/LockedHint rows; missing authority fields and duplicate, malformed, missing, or unrequested property data fail closed. Seat selection compares the parsed seat field to seat0, never the whole row, and state/lock decisions compare complete values to active/yes. A queried session whose Type is empty, tty, unspecified, or unavailable falls back only to the fork's compile-pinned X11 backend: XDG_SESSION_TYPE belongs to the calling process and MUST NOT substitute for another queried session. Actual-child and pure-parser regressions MUST prove hostile bus/pager/session-type environment removal, exact argv, stable-field validation across the pinned and current list shapes, property rejection, exact state matching, and binary-owned fallback; strict smoke fixtures, source gates, semantic mutations, Appendix C #148, and the hardening ledger bind the contract. Current packaged service environments expose no proved ordinary-user write into the supervisor environment, and logind output remains a local OS service result, so this closes conceptual privileged session-confusion/future-launch authority and concrete deterministic correctness defects rather than claiming a demonstrated promptless local privilege escalation.
R-S11aaMUST Linux system-service lifecycle requests own their complete target and child process authority
The Linux --install-service/--uninstall-service paths run only in an already-privileged RustDesk process and ask systemctl to enable, start, disable, or stop the installed system service. The official systemd 252 systemctl(1) contract defines the request as systemctl [OPTIONS...] COMMAND [UNIT...], distinguishes system from user scope, states that enable searches unit-file directories and creates persistent symlinks, documents --no-ask-password for disabling secret and privileged-operation prompts, recommends disabling pagers for elevated calls, and requires exit zero for success. The pinned systemd 252 acquire_bus source reads SYSTEMCTL_FORCE_BUS and switches a manager-focused request to the full D-Bus path; the corresponding bus transport source uses the default system bus on that path, whose address is environment-selectable. The systemd 252 unit-load contract separately states that SYSTEMD_UNIT_PATH overrides unit lookup, while systemctl also consumes pager, offline-mode, locale, loader, search-path, home, and site environment. A privileged lifecycle child therefore MUST NOT inherit any ambient environment or accept a generic action string, arbitrary unit, path, glob, suffix, or option-shaped target. The receiver MUST expose only a closed four-variant Enable/Start/Disable/Stop type; locally revalidate the signed application name as 1–64 ASCII bytes beginning with a letter, ending with an alphanumeric byte, and containing only alphanumerics or hyphens; lowercase it and append the explicit .service suffix. It MUST invoke the already-required canonical root-owned fixed systemctl image with an empty Command::env_clear environment, null stdin, exact --system --no-pager --no-ask-password -- <typed verb> <validated unit> argv, the R-S11r non-stdio descriptor policy, and accept only a successful exit. The option terminator precedes the complete verb/unit positional protocol, so no subsequent value can be reinterpreted as an option; the explicit suffix also prevents systemctl's type inference and SysV-name ambiguity. Pure tests MUST bind every action, exact argv, normalization, length/alphabet rejection, and suffix. A two-hop actual-child regression MUST place hostile DBUS_SYSTEM_BUS_ADDRESS, SYSTEMCTL_FORCE_BUS, SYSTEMD_UNIT_PATH, SYSTEMD_PAGER, and SYSTEMD_OFFLINE in an intermediate image, apply the production environment policy, and prove the final child receives no environment entry except its test role marker. Source and semantic mutation gates, Appendix C #149, and the hardening ledger bind the complete call graph and ordering. The inspected packaged launchers and call graph expose no proved ordinary-user write into the privileged process environment or lifecycle target; this closes conceptual privileged helper/future-deployment authority and deterministic receiver correctness, not a demonstrated promptless local privilege escalation. Exact installed-artifact execution remains part of R-B2/R-S11c-27.
R-S11abMUST Linux X11 endpoint selection remains bound to the exact active logind session
The installed Linux supervisor first selects one exact active logind session, UID, username, and seat, then passes its desktop environment into a root or privilege-dropped service-owned server. That display endpoint is authority-bearing: choosing another local X server can expose or control the wrong desktop and can make a privileged root child parse an unrelated local server. The official systemd 252 login1 interface exposes a read-only Display property on the exact session object; graphical session creation supplies that display, and only the session controller may later call SetDisplay. The pinned systemd 252 receiver binds the property directly to that session's stored display and requires controller authority for mutation, while pam_systemd obtains the initial value from PAM_XDISPLAY. By contrast, the X server contract defines /tmp/.X11-unix/Xn only as the Unix socket for display number n; the pathname does not identify a logind session or selected user. RustDesk therefore MUST obtain X11 DISPLAY only by querying the already-selected session's enumerated logind Display property through R-S11z's typed, environment-empty, status-checked helper. The value MUST normalize completely to local :<decimal display>[.<decimal screen>] syntax. Empty, malformed, remote/hostname-qualified, unavailable, or non-local values fail closed to no display; RustDesk MUST NOT infer an endpoint from same-UID process names, human-formatted w output, Xorg argv, the global X11 socket directory, another socket owner, hostname stripping, or an invented :0. A retained active X11 session MUST requery that exact session, so an initially unavailable or controller-updated property can recover without preserving stale state. XAUTHORITY is a credential-file hint, never endpoint authority. A process environment may supply it only when DISPLAY and XAUTHORITY are read from the same selected-UID process image, that display normalizes to the selected endpoint (screen-number differences are equivalent), and the credential path is absolute and control-free. Otherwise the child receives no override and Xlib uses the selected user's passwd-derived HOME/.Xauthority; the existing selected-UID GDM runtime file is the sole explicit user-scoped fallback because Xauthority files carry display-specific records. No server-side Xorg -auth file may independently choose a credential. Pure regressions, exact loginctl fixtures, source and semantic mutation gates, Appendix C #150, and the hardening ledger MUST bind both the positive chain and complete legacy-fallback absence. This closes deterministic cross-session/cross-owner endpoint confusion and a conceptual privileged-local-endpoint confused-deputy path. It does not claim a demonstrated promptless local privilege escalation: the selected standard-user child already drops to that UID, same-UID inputs are not a stronger principal, and turning a wrong endpoint into root code execution would require an additional reachable X-client defect or privileged root-session precondition. Exact installed-artifact execution remains part of R-B2/R-S11c-27.
R-S11acMUST Linux service lifecycle never infers Xorg process authority from the global process table
The installed Linux supervisor runs with signal authority over every ordinary host process. A process basename or command-line argument is discovery text, not proof that RustDesk created or owns that process: Linux /proc/<pid>/cmdline explicitly presents the command line the process wants an observer to see, and execve(2) accepts the caller-supplied argument vector. R-X14 deleted RustDesk's PAM/Xorg/window-manager session launcher and child tracking; an operator who deliberately pre-starts an X server owns that external lifecycle. The supervisor therefore has no supported Xorg child identity to recover, classify, or terminate and MUST NOT enumerate global processes for a basename Xorg, infer ownership from an /etc/<app>/xorg.conf argument, send a signal to such a candidate, or treat any command line containing that path as proof that the exact selected logind desktop is a RustDesk subprocess. Service headless state MUST derive only from absence of the receiver-selected logind session ID. The packaged startwm.sh/xorg.conf administrator resources may remain root-trust conffiles under R-X14/R-S11c-10t, but their path confers no runtime process authority. The headless CM lifecycle is independently bound to its exact server parent by R-S11ad; R-S11ae deletes the separately adjudicated tray/current-image process-table residue. A focused regression, source and semantic mutation gates, Appendix C #151, and the hardening ledger MUST bind selected-session-only headless classification and complete absence of the Xorg matcher, global kill path, config-path classification, and stale generic cleanup name. This closes an ordinary-user-triggerable root-signal availability/confused-deputy path and deterministic service-state spoofing, not a promptless privilege escalation: the root signal terminates or perturbs a chosen process but does not grant the caller root execution.
R-S11adMUST Linux headless connection-manager lifetime is bound at spawn to its exact server parent
The installed root supervisor MUST NOT rediscover or terminate --cm-no-ui helpers by enumerating the global process table, comparing /proc/<pid>/exe and mutable argv text, and later signaling the numeric PID. Those observations do not retain a process object or ownership generation, and the check-to-signal interval can cross an execve, exit, or PID reuse. The exact service-owned or same-principal --server process already creates each headless CM. That launch MUST use the current executable, closed role argv and launch-token/parent environment of R-S11c-11, and the R-S11q non-stdio descriptor policy, while registering a Linux parent-death hook first. The hook captures the creating server PID in the parent, uses only raw syscalls in Rust's pre_exec child context, sets PR_SET_PDEATHSIG to SIGKILL, and then requires getppid() to equal the captured parent before exec. Arming before the comparison closes the already-dead-parent race; a later parent-thread exit makes the kernel signal that exact child, and an ordinary non-privileged RustDesk exec preserves the setting. Linux defines this parent as the thread that created the child, so premature loss of that server runtime thread may conservatively retire the CM; the server can create a fresh generation, while a helper whose owning server is gone may not remain as an ambient fixed-endpoint authority. The no-UI CM retains its existing last-client idle exit for graceful drain. A real three-process regression MUST launch the exact test image through the production parent-bound helper, retain the worker by pidfd, terminate its launcher, and prove the worker exits.
