Source and mutation gates MUST bind hook-before-descriptor ordering, syscall/error semantics, headless-only call-site selection, behavioral proof, complete absence of the supervisor's global CM sweep, Appendix C #152, and the hardening ledger. R-S11ae separately deletes the remaining tray/current-image process-table authority. This closes root-originated process-table confused-deputy and PID-race availability authority; it does not claim a demonstrated promptless privilege escalation.
R-S11aeMUST Linux server and tray lifecycle use owned state, never current-image process-table presentation
After the exact service-child, Xorg, and headless-CM closures, Linux still enumerated every process whose /proc/<pid>/exe pathname equaled the current image and treated mutable argv as lifecycle authority in two places. The installed supervisor considered any exact --cm argument proof that a connection manager existed; disappearance of that presentation could trigger a delayed child restart, while keeping it present suppressed an inherited hourly restart. Every Linux --server separately selected every exact --tray argument and applied the server process's SIGTERM authority before spawning another tray. Linux documents /proc/<pid>/cmdline as the command line a process wants an observer to see, execve(2) accepts caller-selected argv, and kill(2) applies the sender's UID or capability-based signal authority. Executable-path equality plus role text therefore does not prove that the supervisor or server owns the candidate. The supervisor MUST NOT infer a service-child replacement decision from global --cm presence or elapsed time. Its complete replacement vocabulary is receiver-owned selected-logind state (headless transition, selected UID change, or display/Xauthority change), observable exit of the retained exact child, and explicit service shutdown; the inherited 60-second/one-hour restart workaround belonged to the excised SpotUdp/rendezvous topology and has no direct-only owner. A Linux server MUST NOT enumerate or signal global --tray candidates. Only a non-root user-session server may spawn the current image with the exact tray role; the tray receiver's existing same-UID singleton check may make that new helper exit when a tray already exists, but process discovery grants no signal authority. R-S11af independently binds the principal boundary. The current-image enumerator, argv authority matcher, numeric signal helper, CM heuristic, elapsed restart state, and tray cleanup API MUST be absent. A pure regression MUST bind the complete receiver-owned replacement vocabulary, and source plus independent semantic mutation gates MUST bind the deletion, retained non-root tray launch/singleton receiver, Appendix C #153, and the hardening ledger. This closes a concrete ordinary-user-triggerable service-restart-state spoofing/availability path plus conceptual privileged or same-principal global-signal authority; it does not claim a promptless privilege escalation because neither process termination nor restart-state influence grants root code execution.
R-S11afMUST Linux privileged service children never create user-session UI
The installed Linux supervisor selects a root service-owned --server for the root desktop and for the GDM Wayland login screen. Although that child receives a cleared, bounded environment, its selected desktop values include DISPLAY/XAUTHORITY or WAYLAND_DISPLAY/XDG_RUNTIME_DIR plus the session bus. The inherited common server startup then removed an autostart entry and spawned an independent --tray process under the server's effective principal. On a root child, the tray therefore entered the selected graphical session as root; its Open action launched the same executable with no arguments and the same root principal, and neither launch was bound to the service-owned server's retained Child lifetime. This crosses a privilege boundary without a controlled-side requirement. The freedesktop Desktop Application Autostart specification defines autostart as launching applications during a user's desktop environment after login and places user policy under that user's configuration home. The Wayland client API defines WAYLAND_DISPLAY and XDG_RUNTIME_DIR as the compositor connection endpoint. The polkit privileged-execution security notes deliberately omit DISPLAY/XAUTHORITY when changing users and describe retaining them for privileged GUI programs as discouraged legacy behavior. RustDesk's root service-owned server needs its selected display authority for capture/control, but it does not need a root tray or root viewer GUI. Every Linux path that automatically creates a tray MUST require a non-root numeric effective UID before reading or changing per-user autostart state or spawning --tray; account-name equality is not privilege identity, so the shared Linux root predicate MUST use get_effective_uid() == 0, never compare the resolved username to the literal root. The Linux --tray receiver MUST independently reject that UID-0 principal with a non-success exit before process discovery or UI construction, so a future privileged caller cannot recreate the edge. Non-root user-session servers retain the exact tray launch and receiver-owned same-UID singleton behavior required by R-S11ae; the root server, listener, capture, and input paths remain unchanged. Pure UI-principal and numeric-effective-UID regressions, a source gate, independent semantic mutations, Appendix C #154, and the hardening ledger MUST bind the numeric root predicate, empty-argument automatic tray guard, server-side autostart/tray guard, receiver-side root refusal, and preserved non-root behavior. This closes a concrete promptless service-to-root-GUI creation path and an unbound privileged-UI lifetime, but does not claim that an ordinary user had already converted that GUI into root code execution: doing so would require an additional interaction or GUI/display exploit. Exact installed-artifact execution remains part of R-B2/R-S11c-27.
R-S11agMUST macOS privileged service authority is numeric and enforced at every service entry
The macOS --service role owns the root LaunchDaemon/PrivilegedHelperTools IPC endpoint and credential-snapshot authority; the ordinary controlled-side server is separately owned by the per-user LaunchAgent. The inherited macOS root predicate nevertheless compared the presentation-layer account name with the literal root, and both the common --service dispatch and the dedicated service executable could enter start_os_service without a receiver-side principal check. Apple's getuid(2)/geteuid(2) contract defines effective UID as the process credential used for additional permissions, and Apple's intro(2) contract states that access control uses effective UID and that effective UID 0 is superuser. Account-name resolution is therefore not privilege identity. The shared macOS root predicate MUST obtain the numeric effective UID through the narrow unsafe { hbb_common::libc::geteuid() } FFI call, compare it to 0, and MUST NOT compare crate::username() with root. start_os_service MUST be result-bearing and reject every nonzero effective UID before logging the service principal or calling the service IPC listener. Both the common --service dispatcher and dedicated service executable MUST propagate that rejection as a non-success process exit. Existing connection-manager and whiteboard root-transition refusals consume the same numeric predicate; the non-root LaunchAgent server topology remains unchanged. The independent service-client audit-token, UID-0, trusted PrivilegedHelperTools code-signature, receiver peer-credential, and installed-plist checks remain mandatory and do not rely on this entry gate. A pure numeric-identity regression, source and Apple-conformance gates, independent semantic mutations, Appendix C #155, and the hardening ledger MUST bind the numeric predicate, effective-UID source, rejection-before-listener ordering, both error-propagating entries, and the absence of account-name authority. This closes a concrete ordinary-user service-endpoint occupancy and availability path plus conceptual privileged-helper principal confusion; it does not claim credential disclosure or promptless privilege escalation because the independent client and receiver identity checks still reject an untrusted service peer. Native macOS compilation, execution, and signed-artifact evidence remain part of R-R2 and R-B2.
R-S11ahMUST Linux service-child principal selection is numeric, receiver-owned, and fail-closed
The installed root supervisor chooses one exact active session through R-S11z's typed logind boundary and then launches either a root service-owned --server or a child whose complete credentials are dropped to the selected desktop user. The inherited branch nevertheless selected the root path when the presentation username equaled the literal root, while the launch function accepted a caller-supplied principal enum. A UID-0 account with any other name was routed to the active-user path and then deterministically rejected by its existing UID-0 guard; conversely, an internally inconsistent selected record containing name root and a nonzero UID selected the no-drop path. The official systemd 252 login1 interface defines every listed session as carrying separate numeric user-ID and user-name fields and defines the session User property by its Unix UID, while Linux credentials(7) defines user identities as integers and effective IDs as the kernel permission authority. Account-name text therefore MUST NOT classify the selected session's root privilege. A single receiver-owned derivation MUST parse the already-selected Desktop.uid as canonical decimal uid_t: UID 0 selects RootService, every nonzero selected user selects ActiveDesktopUser, and the deliberate existing selected GDM/SDDM Wayland login-screen exception remains RootService. Both UID and username empty means no selected desktop and no child; a partial identity, malformed/noncanonical UID, or attempt to launch without a selected desktop MUST fail closed. The launcher itself MUST derive this principal and MUST NOT accept a caller-selected principal argument; the non-root path still resolves the username through the password database and requires its returned UID and name to match before setgroups/setresgid/setresuid. Pure regressions, a source gate, independent semantic mutations, Appendix C #156, and the hardening ledger MUST bind renamed-UID-0 correctness, misleading-name non-root classification, the login-screen exception, empty selection, malformed/partial rejection, launcher-owned derivation, and complete absence of the old name branch. This closes deterministic UID-0 service availability and conceptual privileged child-principal confusion. It does not claim a demonstrated ordinary-user promptless privilege escalation: the session record comes from the trusted local logind receiver, and forging its UID/name pair would already require an OS-service or root-integrity failure. Exact installed Debian artifact execution remains part of R-B2/R-S11c-27.
R-S11aiMUST Service-owned server process role is one exact fail-closed argument protocol
The internal service child is launched on Linux, Windows, and macOS with exactly --server --service-owned-server, and protected peer authenticators already require that complete shape. The shared in-process role helper nevertheless searched the entire argument vector for the marker, while Windows configuration bootstrap repeated the same search. A marker in the wrong position, on another command, duplicated, or followed by an extra argument could therefore select service-owned configuration, IPC, credential-replica, SAS, terminal, or helper policy even though it was not the role that the owning service launched and the receiver would later authenticate. Rust's std::env::args contract warns that argument zero is caller-selectable and is not security identity; non-Unicode-safe args_os preserves the actual remaining tokens. Apple's ProgramArguments contract likewise defines a tokenized string array, while Windows CreateProcessW exposes the child command line for argument parsing. A single platform-independent classifier MUST ignore argv[0] and classify only the remaining tokens as Exact when they are exactly --server followed by one --service-owned-server and nothing else, Malformed when the internal marker occurs in any other shape, and Absent otherwise. Every service-child role consumer MUST treat only Exact as service-owned. Desktop process entry MUST terminate unsuccessfully on Malformed before global initialization, platform bootstrap, configuration-root selection, or server dispatch; it MUST NOT silently downgrade malformed internal role text to an ordinary server. Windows machine-configuration selection MUST remain closed over the shared exact supervisor and exact child roles: R-S11ak routes the exact supervisor to the SCM-owned ServiceMain writer, while ordinary bootstrap may initialize only the exact child's read-only replica; only the supervisor may receive write authority. Executable image, OS principal, parent/supervisor generation, installed-root, and peer identity proofs remain separate mandatory authorities and MUST NOT derive from argv[0]. Pure regressions, source and semantic mutation gates, Appendix C #157, and the hardening ledger MUST bind exact admission, absent-role preservation, every malformed ordering/count case, early rejection, both Windows configuration consumers, and complete absence of marker-search role admission. This closes deterministic role/configuration confusion and conceptual privileged-process policy ambiguity, not a demonstrated promptless privilege escalation: malformed text alone does not provide the required service principal, executable, parent, or peer credentials. Native Windows/macOS and exact installed-artifact evidence remain with R-R2/R-B2.
R-S11ajMUST Desktop service-supervisor role is the exact singleton --service protocol
Every supported launcher of the shared desktop service image already emits one complete supervisor role: the Linux systemd, SysV, OpenRC, runit, and manual definitions execute the installed image with exactly --service, and the Windows MSI installs the LocalSystem service with exactly that one argument. The common entry path nevertheless dispatched on only the first argument, Linux selected its root-owned configuration/descriptors/working-directory policy from only argv[1], and Windows bootstrap granted the machine-configuration writer role from only argv[1]. A trailing, duplicated, reordered, or other-command --service marker therefore created a process shape no supported service manager emits while still selecting privileged supervisor policy. Microsoft's CreateServiceW contract states that the registered binary path may include arguments and that those arguments are passed to the service entry point. Apple's ProgramArguments contract likewise defines a tokenized program-and-argument array; the installed macOS LaunchDaemon uses the separate dedicated no-argument service binary, so that entry remains outside the shared image's --service role. Rust's args/args_os contracts require treating argv[0] as non-authoritative and using the OS-string form when tokens may be non-Unicode. One platform-independent classifier MUST inspect only arguments after argv[0], return Exact only for the singleton vector [--service], return Malformed whenever the reserved marker occurs in any other vector, and return Absent for marker-free roles. Desktop entry MUST reject Malformed before global initialization, Linux service-owned configuration setup, Windows bootstrap, or command dispatch. Linux service-root selection and the shared dispatcher MUST consume only the exact state; Windows machine-configuration selection and its write-authority bit MUST consume the same exact-only predicate. There is no trailing-argument compatibility mode. Numeric root/LocalSystem authority, installed-image provenance, service-manager ownership, and protected IPC peer proofs remain separate mandatory checks. Pure regressions, source and semantic mutation gates, Appendix C #158, and the hardening ledger MUST bind the exact singleton, marker-free roles, every malformed position/count/mixture, pre-initialization rejection, all three consumers, shipped exact launcher shapes, and absence of first-argument-only service admission. This closes deterministic supervisor/configuration-role confusion and conceptual privileged-process policy ambiguity. It is not evidence of a promptless local privilege escalation or compromise: argument text alone does not grant UID 0, LocalSystem, an installed executable, or authenticated service IPC authority. Native Windows/macOS and exact installed-artifact evidence remain with R-R2/R-B2.
R-S11akMUST Windows service authority begins only after the own-process image connects to SCM
The exact Windows --service process is installed as SERVICE_WIN32_OWN_PROCESS, but the inherited shared entry performed global/custom-client initialization, selected the durable machine-configuration writer, initialized service logging, and only then called StartServiceCtrlDispatcherW. Its wrapper logged and swallowed dispatcher failure, so a console-launched process with the exact role could run service-specific setup before Windows proved that SCM owned the entry and could then terminate with success when the dispatcher returned ERROR_FAILED_SERVICE_CONTROLLER_CONNECT. The machine-config receiver already independently requires the LocalSystem token, so this did not let an ordinary user become SYSTEM or write the durable configuration; it was deterministic service-manager ownership/initialization-order confusion and false-success status under an already privileged principal. Microsoft's StartServiceCtrlDispatcherW contract says an own-process service's main thread should call it immediately, perform all initialization in ServiceMain, and treat a console launch as ERROR_FAILED_SERVICE_CONTROLLER_CONNECT. Microsoft's ServiceMain contract requires the SCM-created service thread to register its control handler, report pending status, perform initialization, and report SERVICE_STOPPED with an error when initialization fails; its first argument is the service name supplied by SCM. The shared exact-role branch therefore MUST run only the mandatory process-wide safe-DLL loader bootstrap and then call a result-bearing service dispatcher before global initialization, custom-client loading, machine-config selection, log initialization, listeners, or common command dispatch. Dispatcher failure MUST propagate to a non-success process exit; logging and returning success is forbidden. Only the SCM-invoked service entry may initialize the durable machine-config writer. It MUST derive a nonempty registration name from SCM's first ServiceMain argument, register the control handler, and report SERVICE_START_PENDING before performing global/custom identity initialization, calling initialize_windows_service_owned_root(..., true), and initializing the service log; only then may it create any service IPC listener or child. Initialization failure after registration MUST report SERVICE_STOPPED with a service-specific error before returning. The config receiver's independent LocalSystem-token check remains mandatory, making SCM ownership and OS principal two distinct proofs. Windows bootstrap outside ServiceMain may initialize only the exact service-owned child replica with write authority false; it MUST NOT grant supervisor write authority. Runtime failures after successful registration remain reported through SCM service status under R-S11c and need not reinterpret the dispatcher thread's eventual return as a second status channel. Source and independent semantic mutation gates, Appendix C #159, and the hardening ledger MUST bind early exact-role dispatch, failure propagation, SCM-owned service-name selection, handler/status-before-initialization ordering, SCM-only writer selection, the LocalSystem receiver check, read-only child bootstrap, and absence of pre-dispatch service-specific initialization. Native Windows and exact signed-artifact execution remain with R-R2/R-B2.
R-S11alMUST macOS service configuration and logging have one password-database-owned root before initialization
The root LaunchDaemon's dedicated PrivilegedHelperTools executable and the shared application's exact --service entry own the same unattended credential/configuration namespace and protected service IPC listener. Both entries nevertheless loaded custom identity and initialized file logging before start_os_service checked effective UID, while Config::path, Config::get_home, and Config::log_path selected their macOS base through directories-next/dirs-next, whose Unix implementation consults inherited HOME. A UID-0 invocation outside the installed LaunchDaemon's controlled environment could therefore make the privileged receiver read and write credential/configuration or rotating-log state beneath a caller-selected, potentially ordinary-user-writable home. Apple's Elevating Privileges Safely guidance identifies inherited environment as an attack surface and recommends launchd's controlled environment; its secure-helper guidance says privileged helpers must treat user-writable preference files as untrusted. Apple's getpwuid_r(3) contract supplies the password-database home for the numeric principal without consulting HOME. Both supported service entries therefore MUST call one centralized bootstrap before ordinary global/config/log initialization. That bootstrap MUST first require numeric effective UID 0, resolve that same UID through getpwuid_r, and reject an absent, relative, non-clean, nonexistent, non-directory, non-root-owned, group-writable, or world-writable home. Only after the principal proof may it load the signature-verified custom identity. Before the first Config or file-log access it MUST initialize one immutable process-local service root. The config directory is exactly <passwd-home>/Library/Application Support/<directories-next organization.application name>, matching the repository's vendored directories-next 2.0.0 macOS ProjectDirs::config_dir() mapping; the rotating-log directory is exactly <passwd-home>/Library/Logs/<application name>. Config::get_home, Config::path, and Config::log_path MUST consume that stored root for the remainder of the service process, and invalid identity or inconsistent reinitialization is fatal. Only then may logging and the independently UID-gated IPC listener start. Ordinary macOS viewer, user server, and per-user LaunchAgent processes retain their normal user-owned HOME behavior. A pure derivation regression, source and Apple-conformance gates, semantic mutations, Appendix C #160, and the hardening ledger MUST bind root validation, password-database lookup, immutable path derivation, all three config consumers, both centralized entries, and principal-home-before-identity-before-config-before-log-before-listener ordering. This closes a concrete privileged configuration/credential/log namespace confused-deputy path for non-LaunchDaemon UID-0 invocation and makes the installed topology independent of undocumented environment details. It does not claim that an ordinary user could alter the installed root-owned LaunchDaemon plist, obtain UID 0, or bypass the independent audit-token, code-signature, peer-credential, and plist checks. Native macOS compilation/execution and exact signed-artifact evidence remain with R-R2/R-B2.
R-S11amMUST Authority-bearing desktop IPC listener loss remains a fatal process outcome after graceful drain
The desktop main IPC receiver and its password channel, the Linux/macOS protected service receiver and its password channel, and the Windows service-owned control and credential receivers are process-lifetime authority boundaries. Their listener loops distinguish expected cancellation from unexpected end-of-stream, stop accepting, cancel the process-wide server token, drain admitted transactions and credential mutations, drop the listener-state guard, and return an outcome to the retained lifecycle owner. The shipped systemd and launchd policies require truthful failure status for recovery and diagnosis. Every unexpected end of those six listener streams therefore MUST latch a monotonic process-wide failure outcome before requesting cancellation. The latch MUST use release/acquire synchronization across listener/owner/finalizer execution, MUST NOT be cleared during the process lifetime, and MUST survive transaction, password-mutation, IPC-worker, and authenticated-session drain. Under R-S11as the sole desktop process-finalizer caller MUST select status 1 when the failure latch is set and status 0 only for ordinary requested shutdown, after the exact public task and desktop IPC worker have been joined. Protected Linux/macOS service IPC instead MUST return the recorded error after the same receiver-owned drain to its foreground owner under R-S11ar. A normal SIGINT/SIGTERM/service-manager stop MUST NOT set the failure latch, and no later clean request may downgrade a latched failure. A pure exit-status regression, exact source and Apple-conformance gates, semantic mutations, Appendix C #161/#167, and the hardening ledger MUST bind all six listener-loss sites, failure-before-cancellation ordering, the monotonic latch, acquire-before-exit selection, protected-service error return, preserved drain ordering, and clean-shutdown status. This is lifecycle correctness and recovery/observability hardening; it does not claim a remote trigger for ending the local listener stream, privilege escalation, or native failure injection. Exact installed-artifact behavior remains R-R2/R-B2 work.
R-S11anMUST The Linux root supervisor owns protected service IPC readiness, failure, and complete drain
The Linux root --service supervisor historically launched _service and raw _service_password on a detached thread::spawn whose body wrapped the complete listener future in allow_err!. Failure to secure/scrub the parent, bind either socket, set its required mode, or activate the process-lifetime listener guard was therefore logged and discarded while the supervisor continued selecting and launching a service-owned controlled child. On normal SIGTERM/SIGINT or on a service-loop error, the supervisor likewise returned without cancelling or joining that thread, so process exit could destroy an already-admitted polkit/password transaction despite the R-S11/R-S11c-26 non-aborting drain contract. The root supervisor is the owner of this privilege boundary. It MUST create the listener worker with fallible thread::Builder::spawn, retain its JoinHandle, and wait on a bounded, disconnection-aware one-result readiness channel before refreshing the desktop, selecting a principal, or starting any child. Readiness MUST NOT be sent until the generic protected listener, raw password listener, and shared listener-state guard are all successfully active. Any listener-setup error, readiness timeout/disconnection, unexpected clean worker return, worker error, or panic MUST become a supervisor error and hence a nonzero service outcome; no allow_err! or detached fallback may remain. The service signal handler MUST close protected IPC admission by requesting process cancellation after latching the service loop stop. Every still-running loop iteration MUST observe whether the retained worker terminated. Every supervisor-loop return path, including an internal error, MUST converge on one cleanup sequence: request cancellation, join the exact IPC worker without an artificial drain deadline, and only after that join attempt terminate the active-user and root service-owned children. This order preserves the child required by an admitted credential commit until protected IPC transactions and the no-eviction admission ledger have drained; ordinary stop is successful only when IPC drain and both child terminations succeed, while the first failure remains the process error and later cleanup failures remain visible. macOS keeps its synchronous service listener entry and Windows keeps its SCM-owned endpoint supervisors. Focused readiness/outcome regressions, an exact source gate, independent semantic mutations, Appendix C #162, and the hardening ledger MUST bind listener-before-ready order, ready-before-child order, fallible thread creation, retained liveness observation, signal cancellation, join-before-child termination, error propagation, and removal of the detached allow_err! path. Native installed-service stop/failure injection and exact artifact evidence remain R-R2/R-B2 work.
R-S11aoMUST macOS LaunchDaemon termination reaches the protected service IPC drain
The root LaunchDaemon has two exact entries—the deployed PrivilegedHelperTools executable and the app binary's exact --service role—which converge on the synchronous generic _service plus raw _service_password listener. That listener already closes admission on process cancellation, joins every accepted generic/password transaction, drains the no-eviction macOS password-mutation ledger without an artificial deadline, clears replay material only after drain, and drops its process-lifetime listener guard. The inherited LaunchDaemon entry installed no termination handler, however, while the only Tokio Unix signal task belonged to the separate service-owned controlled-side server process. Apple's launchd daemon contract states that system shutdown sends SIGTERM to every daemon launchd started and instructs daemons to install a SIGTERM handler before their service loop; its daemon lifecycle contract says shutdown later escalates to SIGKILL. Under POSIX's default SIGTERM disposition the complete root process terminates, so cancellation-aware drain code that is never signalled does not establish admitted-work finality. After numeric effective-UID-zero proof and before either protected listener is opened, every macOS root service entry MUST install one fallible termination handler through the already termination-enabled signal dependency. Registration failure MUST fail the service before listener admission. The callback MUST perform only the capacity-independent process cancellation request: it may not block on the async runtime, join work, exit, set the fatal-listener-loss latch, or run a second drain. The owned listener future MUST remain the sole admission-close, transaction-join, mutation-drain, replay-clear, and listener-guard owner and MUST return normally after a requested signal. Unexpected listener loss remains a distinct fatal outcome under R-S11am. The separate LaunchAgent controlled-side server retains its own R-T9 signal path; Linux retains its supervisor-owned R-S11an lifecycle and Windows retains SCM control ownership. An exact shared source gate, Apple source-conformance gate, independent semantic mutations, Appendix C #163, and the hardening ledger MUST bind root-proof-before-handler, handler-before-listener, fallible registration, cancellation-only callback shape, the existing drain order, both service entrypoints' convergence, and the absence of a false native-runtime claim. Native launchd stop/upgrade execution and exact signed-artifact evidence remain R-R2/R-B2 work.
R-S11apMUST Desktop controlled-side termination and public-listener completion have one retained owner
The Linux service child, macOS LaunchAgent server, and Windows service-owned server all enter the same desktop start_server(true) → start_direct_only direct-listener path. That path previously spawned direct_server and discarded its Tokio JoinHandle, then spawned and discarded a separate signal task after the public listener could already bind, and finally parked the parent future forever. A clean return or unwind of the sole public-listener task therefore left main IPC and the process apparently alive without the advertised TCP service. Unix signal-constructor failure was merely logged inside the detached task; more subtly, Tokio documents that installing a Unix signal stream replaces the default disposition for process life even after the stream is dropped, so loss of the sole receiver after successful registration can leave later termination captured with no owner. The desktop controlled-server entry MUST synchronously create the exact platform signal receivers before marking the server running or starting main IPC, Windows service-control IPC, or the public listener: Unix owns fallible Tokio SIGTERM and SIGINT streams, while Windows owns the fallible Tokio Windows Ctrl-C stream and retains authenticated SCM child shutdown through the independent service-control cancellation request. Registration failure MUST terminate unsuccessfully before admission. start_direct_only MUST receive that already-installed owner and retain the sole JoinHandle returned by spawning direct_server; it may not detach either the signal receiver or listener and may not use an unconditional desktop parking loop as lifecycle authority. One receiver-owned select MUST observe process cancellation, a termination event, or listener completion. A termination event requests ordinary cancellation; every ordinary service-control/signal path then joins the exact listener so its bound-listener guard and future locals are destroyed before the existing shared authenticated-session and local-IPC finalizer runs. An unexpected signal-stream end, clean listener return without a shutdown request, cancelled listener task, or listener unwind MUST set the existing release/acquire fatal-listener latch, run the same drain, and produce status 1 rather than a false-healthy parked process. Android retains its foreground-service generation teardown and iOS remains mobile-process-owned; neither mobile target may consume the desktop process-global shutdown token or signal owner. Exact shared and Apple source gates, independent semantic mutations, Appendix C #164, and the hardening ledger MUST bind pre-IPC registration, fallible Unix/Windows constructors, receiver transfer, retained task selection/join, listener-before-finalizer ordering, fatal outcome classification, detached-task/desktop-park absence, and the unchanged mobile generation boundary. Native init/launchd/SCM stop and exact-artifact proof remain R-R2/R-B2 work.
R-S11aqMUST The graceful-shutdown finalizer is non-returning and reachable only after retained lifecycle ownership is released
R-S11ap initially composed one retained public-listener owner with three independent process-finalizer callers. The first correction made finish_graceful_shutdown non-returning and forced losing callers to wait, preventing the main thread from returning while a detached IPC caller drained. R-S11as now removes the underlying concurrency: ordinary main IPC and Windows service-main IPC return their post-drain outcomes to one retained desktop owner, which also owns the public listener. The finalizer MUST remain crate-private and explicitly non-returning, but it MUST have exactly one source caller: the retained desktop lifecycle completion helper, after the exact public-listener task and native IPC worker have both completed and been joined. The obsolete atomic finalizer election, follower pending future, returning begin_graceful_shutdown, and local-IPC polling barrier MUST be absent; they would mask an ownership regression instead of proving it impossible. R-S11am's release/acquire fatal-outcome latch remains selected by the sole finalizer after authenticated-session drain. Protected Linux/macOS service IPC continues to return to its separate foreground service owner under R-S11ar and never calls this finalizer. Exact shared and Apple source gates, semantic mutations, Appendix C #165/#167, and the hardening ledger MUST bind the non-returning type, sole caller, exact join-before-finalizer order, stale-election/barrier absence, and drain-before-exit order. Native supervisor execution and exact-artifact proof remain R-R2/R-B2 work.
R-S11arMUST Protected Unix service IPC returns failure to its foreground lifecycle owner after complete drain
R-S11an made the Linux root supervisor retain, observe, and join its protected service IPC worker, classify a returned error as a service failure, and only then terminate its exact service-owned children. The shared Linux/macOS protected receiver nevertheless still called the process-exit finalizer after an unexpected listener end, making the retained Linux join and macOS synchronous error path unreachable. The receiver owns socket admission, admitted transaction join, password-ledger drain/clear, and listener-guard release, while the foreground Linux supervisor or macOS service entry owns subordinate cleanup and process outcome. After any unexpected protected generic or password listener end, the receiver MUST latch fatal cancellation, complete its platform-specific admitted-work drain, drop its LocalIpcListenerGuard, and return the recorded listener error. It MUST NOT call finish_graceful_shutdown, process::exit, create another runtime, detach another task/thread, or apply a timeout/fallback after drain. Ordinary signal cancellation MUST complete the identical drain and return success. Linux MUST receive the error through the retained worker join, preserve it as the service result, and still attempt both exact child terminations in the R-S11an order; macOS MUST return the error through its synchronous service entry so both supported entrypoints terminate unsuccessfully. Under R-S11as the desktop process finalizer has one retained desktop-owner caller; protected service IPC remains excluded from it. A focused outcome regression, exact shared and Apple source gates, semantic mutations, Appendix C #166/#167, and the hardening ledger MUST bind error-versus-cancellation classification, drain/guard-before-return order, Linux join/error/child-cleanup ownership, macOS result propagation, worker-finalizer absence, and the sole desktop finalizer caller. Native init/launchd failure injection and exact-artifact proof remain R-R2/R-B2 work.
R-S11asMUST Desktop local IPC readiness, completion, and native-thread lifetime have one retained owner
The desktop start_server(true) path historically started ordinary main IPC and, for a Windows service-owned child, service-main credential/control IPC on detached native threads before start_direct_only checked R-A4 startup invariants or bound the public listener. Neither thread reported readiness, both could terminate the process independently, and the retained public-listener owner could neither observe nor join them. This allowed authority-bearing local IPC admission before mandatory policy proof, false public-service startup without required local control/credential endpoints, and process exit while another runtime still owned admitted work. Desktop R-A4 invariants MUST run before signal or IPC admission. After fallible signal registration, one named thread::Builder::spawn worker MUST own a single current-thread Tokio runtime created only on that new native thread. It MUST prepare ordinary main plus password IPC and, for the exact Windows service-owned role, service-main credential plus control IPC; readiness MUST NOT be sent until every required listener and LocalIpcListenerGuard is active. Setup error or readiness/completion-channel loss MUST fail before the public listener starts. The already-installed signal owner MUST remain active while readiness is awaited. Only after readiness may the same async lifecycle owner spawn the public listener. Its one selection MUST observe cancellation, signal receipt/loss, public-listener completion, and IPC-worker completion; unexpected completion or panic MUST latch fatal cancellation. Main and Windows service-main IPC MUST return their listener outcome only after transaction/password drain and guard release, never finalize or exit from the worker. Shutdown MUST join the exact public task, receive the IPC outcome, and join the exact native thread through bounded-runtime-safe blocking delegation before the sole non-returning finalizer call. The detached IPC spawns, independent Windows runtime, optimistic unused server-running flag, multi-finalizer election/follower wait, and local-IPC polling barrier MUST be absent. Protected Unix service IPC retains its separate foreground owner; Android retains foreground-service generation teardown; iOS remains mobile-process-owned. Exact shared/Apple gates, a focused semantic validator and complete mutation matrix, Appendix C #167, and the hardening ledger MUST bind pre-admission invariants, fallible named spawn, one current-thread runtime, all-listener readiness, signal-aware startup, completion selection, exact task/thread joins, sole finalizer caller, stale-surface deletion, and the unchanged protected/mobile boundaries. Native init/launchd/SCM injection and exact-artifact proof remain R-R2/R-B2 work.
R-S11atMUST Linux protected-service identity work is permit-owned and cache-prefiltered
The installed Linux root supervisor deliberately exposes the generic _service and raw _service_password Unix sockets to local principals, then relies on kernel peer credentials, the selected active-session UID, current-executable identity, and action-specific authorization as the receiver boundary. Both accept branches previously performed the fresh active-session lookup before acquiring their fixed transaction semaphore. That lookup synchronously invokes the trusted loginctl/login1 boundary as root, so an arbitrary local socket peer could repeatedly induce root subprocess/D-Bus work outside the receiver's admission budget even though it would later fail UID or executable authorization. Every accepted protected-service socket MUST acquire its endpoint's existing fixed transaction permit before any active-session or executable-identity work, and that exact permit MUST remain owned through authorization and the dispatched transaction; if no permit is available, the receiver MUST reject before performing that work. On Linux, UID 0 MUST NOT perform an active-session lookup merely to pass the root UID gate. A non-root peer may reach the fresh lookup only when its kernel UID equals the root supervisor's already-maintained cached active UID. That cache is a negative-only availability prefilter: a missing or nonmatching cache rejects without a live lookup and can never authorize a peer. A cache match merely permits the existing fresh seat lookup, whose result MUST remain the final UID authority, so a just-switched-out user still fails closed; a just-switched-in user may be refused only until the service loop refreshes its cache. The cached-only accessor MUST NOT fall back to loginctl, and stale alternate authorization helpers that can invoke the fresh lookup outside this owned path MUST be absent. macOS retains its audit-token, console-owner, code-signature, authorization-permit, and transaction-permit model. A focused Rust policy regression, an independent semantic validator and complete mutation matrix, Appendix C #168, and the hardening ledger MUST bind permit-before-identity order for both Linux endpoints, cached-only negative prefiltering, root short-circuit, fresh final authority, exact permit transfer, and stale-path deletion. Native installed-service contention and exact-artifact proof remain R-R2/R-B2 work.
R-S11auMUST macOS privileged helper is bound to the current signed app build
A Developer Team ID and helper identifier prove who signed a privileged helper, but they do not prove that the deployed helper belongs to the currently installed app build. The legacy launchd flow previously treated the two plist files as complete installation state and runtime IPC accepted any otherwise-valid same-Team helper. After replacing the app, an older deployed helper could therefore remain active indefinitely; a separately obtained older signed helper could also be selected as the installer input if it was placed beside the app executable. The currently installed app bundle is the build authority. Its fixed Contents/MacOS/service nested executable MUST be sealed by a strict outer app signature validated recursively across every architecture, and privileged installation MUST reject any helper input other than that fixed path. The installer MUST validate the root-owned, non-writable installed app and its pinned app requirement before copying, validate the helper requirement across every architecture, install through the existing root-owned temporary path, and immediately before launch revalidate the outer app plus byte equality between the bundled and deployed helpers. Runtime trust of the deployed helper MUST likewise require the trusted installed app, strict nested/all-architecture static validation, and bounded streaming byte equality with the installed app's helper. Service status is current only when both launchd plists exist and the deployed helper bytes equal the current app's helper; a mismatch MUST expose the existing admin-authorized reinstall action instead of reporting success. Uninstall MUST remain available for stale or partial service artifacts and may not depend on current-helper equality. A focused state regression, an independent semantic validator and complete mutation matrix, shared and Apple source gates, Appendix C #169, and the hardening ledger MUST bind the closure. Native signed-bundle upgrade/relaunch evidence and exact-artifact proof remain R-R2/R-B2 work.
R-S11avMUST macOS file-creation modes obey the C variadic ABI
Apple declares open and openat as C variadic functions when a creation mode is supplied, while Darwin's Rust libc::mode_t is a narrow 16-bit type. Rust's E0617 diagnostic and variadic-argument contract require integer arguments narrower than c_int to be explicitly promoted before a C variadic call; Apple's open-source fcntl.h confirms that both entry points use an ellipsis. Every macOS-reachable Rust open/openat call that supplies an O_CREAT mode MUST use a C-variadic-safe promoted integer type and MUST NOT pass Darwin's narrow mode_t directly through the ellipsis. A mode value held as mode_t MUST be promoted to libc::c_uint, matching the existing portable file-transfer helper; the existing unsuffixed 0o0600 PID-file literal is already inferred as promoted i32 and remains valid. Fixed-prototype APIs such as mkdir, mkdirat, and fchmod MUST retain their declared mode_t arguments; this is a targeted ABI correction, not a blanket mode-type rewrite. The protected config transaction, macOS clipboard paste target, and macOS clipboard placeholder creation MUST retain their existing exclusive/no-follow flags, permissions, descriptor-bound type checks, and cleanup behavior. A standalone semantic validator and complete mutation matrix, shared and Apple gates, Appendix C #170, and the hardening ledger MUST bind all three corrected calls, both already-correct call shapes, the fixed-prototype distinction, and the source-conformance topology. The pinned SDK-free x86_64-apple-darwin compiler cross-check MUST pass these Rust ABI checks; native SDK-backed execution, clipboard/config behavior, signed artifacts, and exact-artifact proof remain R-R2/R-B2 work.

8. Paths to EXCISE (block / remove)

The audit found several inherited paths that cannot be made safe on an internet-exposed host and add nothing to a serverless fork. These MUST be compiled out / removed so they cannot be re-enabled.

R-X1MUST Excise the auto-updater (fetch-and-run RCE)
updater.rs's check_update downloads a binary from a server-supplied URL (the version-check JSON's url field) with no signature or hash check and executes it privileged (--update / MSI). A second identical path exists in the Flutter UI (the flutter_ffi update-me/download-new-version keys). Whoever controls that response — or DNS/TLS for the version host, or a re-pointed fork — gets privileged code execution. MUST remove src/updater.rs (its mod in lib.rs, desktop-cfg-gated), the --update handler in core_main, and the Flutter update keys. The fork ships its own releases (§12); if update-checking is ever re-added it MUST verify a pinned SHA-256 of the artifact before execution (R-B2) — never fetch-and-run. macOS source twin: the sharpest form is the root DMG path — macos.rs's update_me/update_from_dmg run an osascript … with administrator privileges shell that strips com.apple.quarantine and re-installs as root, and mod updater compiles on macOS — so it MUST be excised on the Apple source too. The version-check egress and the universal cross-platform form are §18 (R-SV2/R-SV3).
R-X2MUST Excise the native-plugin loader
plugin/plugins.rs dlopens any plugin_*.{so,dll,dylib} from a system path and calls its C entrypoints — with the validation left as a literal // to-do; install_plugin_with_url unzips a download with no verification. That is arbitrary native code in-process. It is already double-feature-gated and not in the default build, and is not remotely installable (the PluginRequest message is post-auth and only invokes already-loaded plugins) — but it is dead weight on a hardened fork. MUST never compile plugin_framework, and MUST drop the src/plugin/ tree and the --plugin-install/--plugin-uninstall args so it cannot be re-enabled by accident.
R-X3MUST Excise the remote ConfigureUpdate server-list rewrite
rendezvous_mediator.rs lets the rendezvous server rewrite the client's rendezvous-servers list at runtime and restart — re-homing the client to arbitrary infrastructure. A serverless fork's server list MUST be fixed by local config; make the ConfigureUpdate arm a no-op. Note the twin — and that it is not the mediator's: a malicious rendezvous server can also rewrite rendezvous-servers from inside an unauthenticated TestNatResponse, but the cu field → set_option("rendezvous-servers") + set_serial handler lives inside test_nat_type_() (common.rs), reached by the startup test_nat_type() probe — not by RendezvousMediator. So excising the mediator (R-D4) does not remove it: on the viewer/Android/iOS builds the running app's startup probe still TCP-calls Config::get_rendezvous_server() (default rs-ny.rustdesk.com) and would adopt an attacker-supplied rewrite from the reply. It is removed only when the test_nat_type subtree is made cfg-absent and its startup callers deleted (R-SV4(d)). And a third twin: hbbs_http::sync::handle_config_options (sync.rs) writes arbitrary config keys — including the trust-anchor key, custom-rendezvous-server, api-server, even direct-access-port — from a /api/heartbeat strategy.config_options response, with no key allowlist (inert once mod sync is gated out per R-D4 and api-server is pinned empty per R-D6, but uncaught otherwise). R-A6 MUST grep all three re-home paths (ConfigureUpdate · TestNatResponse/common.rs · handle_config_options/sync.rs) so a partial fix — or a server-assisted rebuild that keeps hbbs_http::sync — cannot leave a twin live.
R-X4MUST Pin the trust anchor; remove the key/server overrides
The public key (trust anchor of the rendezvous-secured channel) and the server set are overridable via the "key" config option, a license/"config string" (which even accepts unsigned JSON — custom_server.rs), a renamed executable (or the RUSTDESK_APPNAME env var that spoofs the exe-name license without renaming, windows.rs), and the registry (common.rs, custom_server.rs, core_main.rs; on Windows the HKLM license read at windows.rs). A malicious installer name or shared config string silently points the client at attacker infrastructure with the attacker's key. The fork MUST bake its own values, make get_key() ignore the runtime override, and remove custom_server.rs + the --config path; it MUST NOT accept the unsigned-JSON branch. The same class includes three ungated CLI paths beyond --config: --import-config <path> overwrites the entire config (incl. trust anchor + servers) from an attacker-suppliable file with no is_root gate (core_main.rs); --remove <path> is an ungated file-delete gadget; and --assign/--deploy/--set-id presuppose a server account this fork removes and leak tokens/passwords on argv. All MUST be excised and CI-grepped (R-A6). macOS · iOS source: the trust-anchor override — get_key() reading the "key" option (the rendezvous anchor, distinct from the per-device get_key_pair()) and overriding RS_PUB_KEY — is live on both Apple platforms (the deep-link ?key= reaches it via mod client); custom_server, --config and --import-config exist on macOS but are absent on iOS (no core_main); the RUSTDESK_APPNAME/renamed-exe spoof is Windows-only, hence absent on Apple. So get_key() MUST ignore the override on macOS and iOS, and the macOS config-import paths MUST be excised on the Apple source.
R-X5MUST Excise LAN discovery; the recent-session re-admit is deleted with the FSM collapse
LAN discovery (lan.rs, UDP 21119) discloses MAC, RustDesk ID, hostname, active username, and platform to any LAN pinger. Per §8's "compiled out / removed" bar (not merely "disabled"), the fork MUST excise the lan.rs UDP listener — absent, not firewalled (cf. R-D4). On Android this is acute: start_lan_listening is hard-forced true (rendezvous_mediator.rs), so the excision MUST extend to the Android build (R-D7a), not Linux alone. The is_recent_session 30-s auto-re-admit (connection.rs) is not a separate weaker path: R-S2's collapse deletes it outright — authorization is the CPace KEYED edge, established fresh per connection, so there is no re-admit path to gate and the SESSION_TIMEOUT window (connection.rs) goes with it. Reconnect simply re-runs the sub-second handshake; there is never an unauthenticated re-admit.
R-X6MUST Excise the trust-anchor/password deep-link writes; confirm the connect deep-link
A completeness sweep of the platform/UI layer found the one cross-trust-boundary surface the Rust-core audits could not see: the rustdesk:// URL-scheme handler — a malicious web page, QR code, or co-installed app can fire it, pre-authentication. rustdesk://config/<b64> writes the rendezvous/relay/api server and the public key (trust anchor); rustdesk://password/<pw> sets the permanent password — both default-OFF behind compile-time flags but with no install+root gate (unlike the CLI), and both are the same trust-anchor / credential-injection class as R-X4. The fork MUST remove the config/server/key/password-writing deep-link authorities entirely. The third path, rustdesk://<id>?password=…&/r@<server>, silently auto-initiates an outbound session to an attacker-chosen peer/relay on all platforms; the fork MUST require explicit user confirmation for any deep-link-initiated connection and MUST NOT honor an embedded password/relay/key (incl. the ?key= trust-anchor injected into the outbound session) without it. The strip MUST be enforced in both layers: the Dart parser (flutter/lib/common.dart handleUriLink) and the Rust core, where the embedded ?key= is adopted into other_server (client.rs) and where the raw URI also reaches the core via bind.sendUrlScheme (the desktop_multi_window child-window path) — a Dart-only fix is bypassed, since the trust-anchor adoption lives in the Rust initialize (client.rs). (flutter/lib/common.dart; client.rs.) The Linux delivery transport is a D-Bus session-bus method (src/server/dbus.rs: bus org.rustdesk.rustdesk, method NewConnection(id)) that ignores the caller (no peer check) and forwards the URI verbatim into the same handler — any co-installed same-session app can invoke it, and it claims the bus name with replace_existing=true (a name-hijack that lets a malicious process intercept legitimate links). The fork MUST remove or caller-restrict NewConnection and drop replace_existing (CI-grep org.rustdesk.rustdesk · NewConnection · start_dbus_server). The Windows and macOS existing-instance delivery transports are a local-IPC boundary — desktop URL handoff MUST use authenticated _url IPC, not public window messages or caller-blind sockets: the listener authenticates the sender before delivering Data::UrlLink (same user/session plus current executable, with a restricted Windows named-pipe DACL). All transports still route through the explicit-confirmation gate above. On Android the deep-link is the manifest's rustdesk:// VIEW/BROWSABLE intent-filter on an exported MainActivity, and the manifest also ships an exported BootReceiver with a DEBUG_BOOT_COMPLETED broadcast any installed app can fire (AndroidManifest.xml: MainActivity, BootReceiver); the fork MUST remove the debug broadcast, set those components exported=false where possible, scope the intent-filter, set android:allowBackup="false" (else the app-private config and mobile at-rest envelope carrying connect-equivalent PRS state are exfiltratable via backup) + a cleartext-deny network-security-config, and minimize the permission set to the dual viewer+controlled minimum (the app is one binary in both roles, R-R2, so it cannot be pared to "viewer-only"). Drop the legacy READ_EXTERNAL_STORAGE/WRITE_EXTERNAL_STORAGE (superseded by MANAGE_EXTERNAL_STORAGE on the pinned targetSdk 33) and requestLegacyExternalStorage. Keep (each maps to a preserved feature) INTERNET, ACCESS_NETWORK_STATE, FOREGROUND_SERVICE (+ the typed FOREGROUND_SERVICE_MEDIA_PROJECTION if targetSdk is bumped to ≥ 34), BIND_ACCESSIBILITY_SERVICE (controlled-side input injection), RECORD_AUDIO, MANAGE_EXTERNAL_STORAGE, POST_NOTIFICATIONS, WAKE_LOCK, REQUEST_IGNORE_BATTERY_OPTIMIZATIONS and RECEIVE_BOOT_COMPLETED (controlled auto-start). SYSTEM_ALERT_WINDOW is the one high-risk permission to drop, and this profile decides it: cut the FloatingWindowService and drop SYSTEM_ALERT_WINDOW — the controlled-status surface is already carried by the mandatory foreground-service notification (MainService.kt), and a TYPE_APPLICATION_OVERLAY window is an overlay/tapjacking primitive a single-purpose box does not need. The inherited floating window is moreover default-on (disable-floating-window is opt-out, == "Y", MainActivity.kt) with cosmetic size/transparency/untouchable/SVG sub-options — all removed with it. Cutting the permission MUST sever, not silently disable, the boot-auto-start dependency that currently requires it (BootReceiver.kt; canStartOnBoot). (If a status affordance is ever retained, it MUST be a single non-configurable minimal form — no runtime variability.) iOS source (the transport this list omits): iOS has no Rust IPC — the natively-registered rustdesk:// CFBundleURLSchemes (ios/Runner/Info.plist) flow OS → FlutterAppDelegate → the uni_links plugin (uriLinkStream/getInitialLink) — which natively supports iOS (its iOS impl is UniLinksPlugin.m; the Dart API carries no platform guard), with rustdesk's own initUniLinks/listenUniLinks (common.dart) routing only isLinux to D-Bus instead, so the iOS path is live — into the Dart handleUriLink, and the Rust ?key=→other_server adoption (in mod client's LoginConfigHandler::initialize) is reachable there too — so the strip MUST cover the iOS transport in both layers, and the iOS-only config/password deep-link write authorities MUST be removed, not left flag-gated. The iOS plugin has a second ingress this openURL framing misses: UniLinksPlugin.m also implements application:continueUserActivity: (NSUserActivityTypeBrowsingWeb — the Universal-Links/Handoff path), forwarding a matching https:// webpageURL into the same handleUriLink sink; the strip MUST remove that handler too, and the build MUST keep the associated-domains entitlement / NSUserActivityTypes absent (today's safety rests on that absence — R-A6's per-key entitlement sweep flags any future associated-domains addition). Desktop URL IPC detail: start_ipc_url_server is a separate listener that bypasses the main IPC handler; it MUST authenticate its sender before emitting on_url_scheme_received (peer uid + executable on macOS; named-pipe session/access checks + executable on Windows).
R-X7MUST Excise the one-time-password mechanism and the TOTP second factor; pin to the permanent password
RustDesk carries two credentials: the permanent password and an in-memory, rotating one-time password (TEMPORARY_PASSWORD, hbb_common/src/password_security.rs), and the default verification-method accepts either (UseBothPasswords; validate_password tries temporary first, then permanent, connection.rs). A balanced PAKE authenticates from one shared secret, so a second live credential is dead weight at best and a non-PAKE bypass at worst; the one-time password is also attended-only (screen-shown, rotating) and useless for the unattended access this fork targets. The fork MUST excise the temporary-password code (TEMPORARY_PASSWORD, get_auto_password, update_temporary_password, check_update_temporary_password) and hard-pin verification-method = use-permanent-password, making the permanent password the sole credential and sole PAKE PRS (R-P1, R-S9). (With R-S2's collapse, validate_password is itself deleted, so verification-method no longer feeds any auth decision — the hard-pin and its CI-grep persist purely to keep the OTP/temporary-password code absent, not because the resolver is still consulted.) The TOTP second factor is excised on the same logic (R-S2): Auth2fa (connection.rs), the require_2fa state, generate2fa/verify2fa/get_2fa (auth_2fa.rs), the trusted-device store (add_trusted_device, the RemoveTrustedDevices/ClearTrustedDevices IPC arms), and the totp-rs dependency (Cargo.toml) MUST be removed: TOTP is enrolled only through the 2FA UI ceremony this excises, and R-D6 pins the 2fa option empty, so it is un-instantiable dead surface, and a post-keying second factor would re-admit the multi-step pre-authorized state the R-S2 collapse eliminates. Dropping totp-rs also retires a puller of the idna 0.5 RUSTSEC-2024-0421 vuln (Appendix D, R-R3). This also deletes the consecutive-failure temporary-password rotation (connection.rs) — a remotely-triggerable lockout where 10 failed attempts rotate the password out from under a legitimate user, already gated on temporary_enabled(), so it vanishes with the excision. Enforcement MUST be fail-closed and immutable: verification_method()/approve_mode() today read a runtime-writable option and fail OPEN — an unset/unknown value yields UseBothPasswords/Both, re-enabling OTP and click-to-accept (password_security.rs) — and a DEFAULT pin can be shadowed by a written option (the get_or read-precedence at config.rs returns a set value over the DEFAULT; set_option writes) — which is precisely why the policy is pinned at the funnel, not in a DEFAULT tier. The fork pins verification-method and approve-mode in the controlled-side policy table (R-S16): the resolvers stay unmodified, and the Config::get_option funnel returns the policy while is_option_can_save rejects every runtime write — so the funnel pin wins over any written value, the failure these resolvers' DEFAULT-only pin could not prevent. R-S16/R-A6 own the enforcement and its CI proof.
R-X7aMUST Excise the Android approve-mode / verification-method selector UI — not merely fix it off
R-X7/R-S9 pin verification-method = use-permanent-password and approve-mode = password in the controlled-side policy table (R-S16), so the Config::get_option funnel returns the policy and is_option_can_save rejects any write to those keys (R-A6 AST-checks the resolvers reach config only through that funnel). But the Android server-settings UI still presents and writes the multiplicity — selectors for password / click / both and one-time / permanent / both, plus the one-time-password length and the numeric-OTP toggle, via bind.mainSetOption (flutter/lib/mobile/pages/server_page.dart; writers) — gated only by the soft isOptionFixed managed-config lock (config.rs), the exact "defaulted off, not removed" pattern R-S12 forbids. Once the R-S16 pin lands, the funnel overrides any written value and the write guard rejects it, so the UI is a second, divergent presentation of a setting it can no longer change. The fork MUST remove these selectors (leaving only Set permanent password), not disable them — the same toggle-and-surface removal R-X8 applies to the terminal. The same selectors ship on the desktop front-end — the Safety tab of desktop_setting_page.dart presents the identical verification-method (incl. the kUseBothPasswords case) and approve-mode choices through the same mainSetOption FFI — so this removal MUST generalize from Android to every front-end the fork ships (Flutter desktop + mobile); it is the first-named instance of the broader UI-conformance sweep §19 (R-G1/R-G4) carries.
R-X8MUST Pin the terminal ON — full control is the single mode (locking it off would be theater on the sovereign shared-password box)
Post-PAKE the Terminal session type spawns a shell as the service user — root on the exposed box (R-D3) — with no second credential on Linux: the OS-login/PAM re-auth (should_use_terminal_os_login_scope, connection.rs) is Windows-only by construction (cfg!(target_os = "windows")), and Linux select_terminal_launch_authority selects the process owner and yields a root PTY via spawn_command (terminal_service.rs). The reflex reading is that one PAKE password buys a direct root shell — more permissive than the bar. On this box that reading is void. §17 is a single-user sovereign machine and the CPace-authenticated peer is its owner, who holds the box's shared password — which here is also its OS/sudo/login password. §2 is explicit that the fork does not confine a password-knower ("confining it would mean defending against a password-knower, which we do not"), so denying that owner a terminal is security theater: with the keyboard on the desktop and the shared sudo password they already reach root in one step (xterm → sudo), exactly as they reach a tunnel (socat/ssh -R, R-D6). And the fork's own yardstick is SSH — a full interactive shell after a single authentication — so withholding the terminal would make this box strictly weaker than the bar it measures itself against. Full control is therefore the honest, correct, single mode. The fork MUST pin enable-terminal = Y in the controlled-side policy (R-S16) so the Self::permission(OPTION_ENABLE_TERMINAL, …) gate (connection.rs) resolves true through the Config::get_option funnel and the LoginRequest.Terminal arm is admitted. It stays defensive precisely because the value is a pin, not a flippable default: the funnel returns it and is_option_can_save rejects every runtime write (a password-knower cannot mutate policy mid-session in either direction), and the runtime SwitchPermission widener is removed (R-S16(d)(ii)) so the single mode is un-flippable — CI-proven per R-A6. What is not reversed — the second-credential path stays excised (R-X14/R-S18): the terminal's OS-login credential (os_login→token, the Windows LogonUserW terminal token, and the elevation-with-logon path) is deleted from the tree on every build — that removal is not theater but a real second-authentication surface — so an attended terminal runs as the service user (root on the exposed box), never via a peer-supplied OS credential, and granting the terminal re-introduces no PAM/LogonUserW step. The session-type code stays in the one binary (§14); an operator who wanted a different policy edits the pin and rebuilds (the R-F4 build-time-choice discipline), not a runtime knob.
R-X9MUST Excise the Windows run-mode, elevation, and service-disable plurality — one mode, one privilege transition
The Windows binary is a multi-tool selected by argv, executable name, install state, elevation state, and runtime config — the opposite of "one mode, the correct one." The controlled host needs exactly one: an installed, auto-start LocalSystem service (--service → start_os_service, windows.rs) that launches the session --server via the winlogon-token CreateProcessAsUserW — the single privilege transition — which launches the same-user --tray. Everything else MUST be compiled out, not gated:
  • Non-service run modes. The portable / un-installed in-process service (start_portable_service, StartPara, portable_service.rs; core_main.rs) and quick-support (set_quick_support, is_quick_support_exe, --quick_support, core_main.rs) are removed — they run the controlled side without the installed-service privilege model.
  • The stop-service runtime toggle. get_create_service honors a runtime-writable Config::get_option("stop-service") (windows.rs) that suppresses the service entirely — a local process (via --option/IPC) can disable the controlled host's service. This is the textbook R-S12 "security-relevant toggle, default-off-but-reachable": the branch MUST be excised and the key removed from the IPC-writable set (R-S11). The installed service is always present and always auto-start — the desktop daemon model (this Windows service; the Linux systemd --service, R-D1), whose direct listener is unconditional for the life of the unit; this is distinct from the Android controlled side, where the foreground service owns the listener and releases it on a graceful stop or process death (R-D7a). The excised stop-service key is dead on every target (the listener reads no such option), so removing it and its IPC-writable entry takes nothing from Android's foreground-service stop, which is an OS-lifecycle transition, not a config write.
  • Redundant & peer-reachable elevation. Three of the four SYSTEM routes are removed: the interactive UAC re-launch (run_uac/elevate, windows.rs); the SYSTEM token-theft git dependency impersonate_system (Cargo.toml) and its run_as_system wrapper (windows.rs); and — the sharp one — the post-PAKE peer-triggered elevation: Misc::ElevationRequest → handle_elevation_request → start_portable_service (connection.rs) lets a connected peer spawn a SYSTEM portable service. On the installed-service deployment it is dead-by-state (is_installed() true skips it), but the code path and the token-theft primitive remain compiled in — they MUST be structurally absent, not merely unreachable. The winlogon-token launch above is the sole sanctioned transition.
CI-greps these absent (R-A6). Persistence & installers: the auto-start service is the only persistence owner; Windows Installer declaratively owns its creation, recovery policy, start, stop, and removal, and no application-side service-creation path exists. The independent all-users Startup-folder --tray shortcut is absent. (The Sciter UI is dropped by R-B6; the world-creatable main pipe is tightened by R-S11; the trust-anchor / config sources are pinned by R-X4.) macOS source twin (the X9/X11-class obligation): the analog is a two-tier launchd model — a root LaunchDaemon running the service binary plus a per-user LaunchAgent running --server, the cross-uid step being launchctl asuser (the macOS equivalent of the winlogon-token launch) — installed by an osascript … with administrator privileges script. macOS additionally carries two in-process root-exec primitives: the osascript-admin elevate (re-launches the binary with arbitrary argv as root) and the AuthorizationExecuteWithPrivileges-based Elevate in macos.mm (linked even where dead-by-state). Both MUST be excised so launchctl asuser is the sole privilege transition — the source twin of R-X11's "carry no in-process sudo-driving primitive."
R-X10MUST Excise the Linux run-mode plurality — rustdesk --server (via the service or a supervisor) is the sole controlled entry
The Linux twin of R-X9. The controlled side runs as rustdesk --server — the capture/listener process — launched either by the installed auto-start systemd service (--service → start_os_service, core_main.rs, platform/linux.rs, which drops root→user via the single sanctioned sudo -u transition, run_as_user, linux.rs; R-D3a) or directly by a per-user process supervisor as the session user, capturing the supervisor-provided $DISPLAY/$XAUTHORITY with no logind seat required (the supervisord+Xvfb variant, R-D8). The --server entry is therefore the one sanctioned controlled entry both paths use — not compiled out. What MUST be compiled out, not gated, is the run-mode plurality around it: the --no-server flag (core_main.rs) — the Linux portable/quick-support twins — and the run-from-terminal "no-args starts the server" fallback (core_main.rs), so the only way to reach the controlled side is a deliberate --server (under a supervisor) or the --service that spawns it. The --cm/--cm-no-ui connection-manager UIs (core_main.rs) are cosmetic under approve-mode=password (R-S9) and MUST reduce to at most one non-configurable form. The canonical controlled config tree is whichever home the persistent --server reads (config.rs): under the root --service model it is the root service account's /root/.config/RustDesk — not the transient, seat-following user --server's ~/.config — whereas under a supervisor the --server is the persistent session user, so its own ~/.config/RustDesk is the tree. The operator configures it via --option (R-D2). (Sciter's second controlled UI is dropped by R-B6.) CI-greps the excised entries absent (R-A6).
R-X11MUST Excise the gtk_sudo interactive-elevation cluster — sudo -u is the sole privilege transition
Beside the sanctioned root→user drop, the Linux binary carries a second, interactive privilege route: gtk_sudo (src/platform/gtk_sudo.rs), a GTK/PTY front-end that drives sudo -S/su - <user> with injected-password elevation, entered via -gtk-sudo (core_main.rs) and reached from run_cmds_privileged (linux.rs), check_super_user_permission (linux.rs), and the UI/tray service install/uninstall that shells systemctl enable/start through it (linux.rs; ui_interface.rs, tray.rs). On the .deb-installed, always-auto-start deployment (R-D1) this is dead by state — systemd owns the unit and service lifecycle goes through Debian's package helper layer — but, exactly as R-X9 requires of the Windows token-theft primitive, the path MUST be structurally absent, not merely unreachable: a root-exposed service must carry no in-process sudo/su password-driving primitive. Drop gtk_sudo.rs, the -gtk-sudo arm, run_cmds_privileged, and the UI-driven install/uninstall callers; package lifecycle is the .deb's postinst/prerm through deb-systemd-helper/deb-systemd-invoke. CI-greps gtk_sudo · -gtk-sudo · run_cmds_privileged absent (R-A6).
R-X12MUST Pin the capture backend to X11; compile the Wayland/pipewire path and its selector out
The binary contains both capture backends, runtime-selected by session type — Display::primary() branches on is_x11() into Display::X11 vs Display::WAYLAND (libs/scrap/src/common/linux.rs) — and the root Cargo.toml hard-enables Wayland (scrap = { path = "libs/scrap", features = ["wayland"] }). §17 is Xorg; Wayland is N-A. To make "one capture mode" structural, the fork MUST drop features=["wayland"] so #[cfg(all(x11, feature="wayland"))] pub mod wayland (scrap/src/lib.rs) and the xdg-portal ScreenCast / restore-token persistence (wayland/pipewire.rs) are absent — which resolves R-S14's restore-token at-rest exposure by construction rather than mitigating it. The fork MUST also remove the RUSTDESK_FORCED_DISPLAY_SERVER env override (hbb_common/src/platform/linux.rs) — a runtime knob that re-selects the security-relevant capture/input path, the precise R-S12 "control removed, not defaulted off" — and make is_x11() a pinned constant true (asserted at startup, R-A4) — which also subsumes the one legitimate reason real deployments set this knob today: in a container or minimal session loginctl may exist with no seat, so the inherited get_display_server() falls back to a stray XDG_SESSION_TYPE and --server can mis-select the capture/input backend or refuse incoming sessions outright ("Unsupported display server type"); the compile-time x11 pin makes that determinism a property of the binary, so no operator ever needs the env override the fork removes. CI-greps the wayland feature, mod wayland (in scrap), and RUSTDESK_FORCED_DISPLAY_SERVER absent (R-A6). macOS source (assert, not excise): macOS already has one compile-time capture backend — Quartz/CGDisplayStream — with no runtime selector and no RUSTDESK_FORCED_DISPLAY_SERVER analog, so the obligation is to assert that sole backend, not excise an alternate. iOS: no capture backend exists (viewer-only). One macOS nuance: ScreenCaptureKit audio is a second consumer of the Screen-Recording TCC grant (R-S14).
R-X13MUST Pin input injection to XTEST; excise the dormant uinput service and the rdp_input path
Correcting a common misreading: on the Xorg box the controlled-side input backend is XTEST/xdo (the enigo default on X11, libs/enigo/src/linux/), not uinput — uinput is the Wayland path (wayland_use_uinput() = !is_x11() && is_server(), input_service.rs; gated !is_x11()), and rdp_input the Wayland-portal/flatpak path. Yet the root --service unconditionally stands up the uinput IPC listener (start_uinput_service(), linux.rs) with its world-mode 0o0666 _uinput_* sockets (Appendix B / R-S11a) that the X11 --server never connects to — dormant cross-uid surface. Once X11 is pinned (R-X12), wayland_use_uinput/wayland_use_rdp_input are constant-false; the fork MUST compile out src/server/uinput.rs, the start_uinput_service() call, the setup_uinput client path, and src/server/rdp_input.rs, so XTEST is the sole injection backend and the unused _uinput_* sockets are absent — shrinking the R-S11a cross-uid socket surface to _service alone on this build. CI proves them unreachable from the service entry (call-graph, not symbol-grep — the sanctioned XTEST/enigo injector legitimately stays; R-A6). macOS source (assert): the sole macOS injector is CGEvent (HID tap) — there is no uinput/rdp_input twin to excise, so this reduces to CI-proving CGEvent is the only injector reachable. iOS: enigo is an empty stub — no input backend (viewer-only).
R-X14MUST Excise the os_login → PAM desktop-session-start — a second, root-context credential path
The Linux controlled side carries an entire second authentication subsystem the PAKE does not subsume: a peer's LoginRequest.os_login{username,password} drives handle_login_request_without_validation (connection.rs) → try_start_desktop → linux_desktop_manager.rs's try_start_x_session, which performs real PAM authenticate()/open_session() on the attacker-supplied OS credentials to start an X session as an arbitrary OS account — and runs /etc/<app>/startwm.sh, which sources that user's ~/.bash_profile as root, with a predictable /tmp/.Xauthority (a sticky-dir cookie race). The module compiles unconditionally on Linux (platform/mod.rs) — it is not behind the terminal and is not the Windows-only/stub OS-login-scope path R-X8 disposes of (select_terminal_launch_authority is the terminal process-owner selector; linux_desktop_manager is the live desktop one). This is failure-mode-C, full stop — a root-context credential + exec path on an internet-exposed box, the same class §8 excises (updater R-X1, plugin R-X2, the terminal's peer-supplied OS-credential path R-X8/R-S18). It is gated on the allow-linux-headless option (default-OFF) — a documented headless-access toggle, not a path that fires by default (verified: LinuxHeadlessHandle::try_start_desktop returns early unless is_headless_allowed, connection.rs).
