Excision is correct anyway on two grounds: (i) the §17 box does not need it — greeter/seat0 capture (R-S14) draws its DISPLAY/XAUTHORITY from an independent discovery path (platform/linux.rs mod desktop: get_display_x11/get_xauth_x11), not from this PAM/session-start path (verified clean split — different file and module: mod desktop in platform/linux.rs vs the PAM path in linux_desktop_manager.rs, no shared mutable state — the only overlap is the read-only seat0/loginctl helpers (get_values_of_seat0/get_username/is_headless) that R-X14 keeps anyway); and (ii) when an operator does enable headless access, on upstream's plaintext direct path the os_login/desktop-start runs in the without_validation function ahead of validate_password (direct listener passes secure=false), so an unauthenticated network peer drives PAM authenticate() + a root startwm.sh: a remote, pre-auth root-context PAM oracle (rate-limited by the per-IP failure counter at connection.rs, so not an unthrottled brute-force, but still network-reachable and root-exec-capable). A runtime toggle that opens a root-context PAM+exec subsystem the box has no use for is exactly the R-S12 "no security-relevant toggle reachable" / one-mode case — and, as R-X8/R-S18 treat the terminal's peer-supplied OS credential, default-off is not sufficient. The shipped direct build MUST compile out only the session-spawn + PAM-auth in linux_desktop_manager.rs — try_start_x_session/start_x_session/start_x_session_thread/start_x11, the pam::Client authenticate()/open_session() calls, add_xauth_cookie, the /tmp/.Xauthority default and the startwm.sh exec — collapse try_start_desktop to existing-session-discovery-only and make LinuxHeadlessHandle::try_start_desktop return "" on Linux; MUST keep the capture-side discovery untouched (mod desktop in platform/linux.rs, and the loginctl helpers get_username/is_headless) so seat0/greeter capture (R-S14) still works; MUST pin allow-linux-headless off (a security-relevant toggle, R-S12); and MUST treat /etc/<app>/{startwm.sh,xorg.conf} + /etc/pam.d/<app> as root-trust files (reconciling the .deb-vs-Flutter xorg.conf install-path inconsistency). Scope: peer-supplied os_login is absent from the terminal authority path too: select_terminal_launch_authority derives only the process owner or the service-owned server process's Windows session user (R-X8/R-S18). The R-A6 desktop check is therefore pam::Client/authenticate/try_start_x_session/the os_login-desktop path unreachable from the connection accept path (call-graph, not symbol-absent). The session-discovery part is kept (collapsed to existing-session-only); R-S18 governs the credential itself: pam::Client authentication on peer-supplied OS creds is a second OS credential, deleted from the tree (an attended headless box pre-starts its own X server — the Xvfb model — rather than PAM-creating a session from a peer's credentials), so the PAM/session-spawn path is absent (cf. R-X8/R-D4). macOS source (negative finding — document, do not excise): macOS has no twin — linux_desktop_manager is Linux-only, the macOS select_terminal_launch_authority accepts no peer credential and selects the current process owner, and ElevationRequest::Logon is Windows-only — so the remote-pre-auth-root oracle is absent on macOS by construction; the R-A6 Apple-cfg check asserts pam::Client/try_start_x_session = zero there. (iOS has no controlled side at all.)

9. Secure-by-assertion requirements

Every property in §7–§8 MUST be guarded by a check the program, the build, or the test suite refuses to pass when the property is false.

9.1 Runtime (the program aborts rather than run insecure)

R-A1MUST No message is processed on an unkeyed stream
Before any protobuf message is dispatched — on the responder's accept path and, when the box views, the initiator's connect path (R-S13) — assert the secretbox is engaged and a confirmed PAKE produced its key; otherwise terminate (and panic in debug). Because authorization is the CPace KEYED edge (R-S2), this assert and authorized coincide: no message, and no authorized, before keying. Makes the unkeyed-direct-path bug unreachable in either direction (the inherited viewer is_secured() check at io_loop.rs is informational only — it MUST become this fail-closed assert on the initiator path).
R-A2MUST No AuthConnType resolves and authorized cannot set without a confirmed PAKE
authorized is set only on the CPace KEYED success edge (R-S2/R-P14a), and the AuthConnType is resolved from LoginRequest's session-type oneof after that edge. The inherited resume paths that set authorized without a fresh handshake — the 30-s is_recent_session reconnect and SwitchSidesResponse — are deleted (R-S2), not merely asserted: the CPace KEYED edge is upstream of every path that reaches the lone authorized=true assignment — its several callers of send_logon_response_and_keep_alive (the password path and the CM Data::Authorize arm) all run on an already-keyed stream, so no path sets authorized without a confirmed mutual key on this exact stream.
R-A3MUST set_raw() cannot strip a keyed session stream
The key-strip is removed; any residual must assert it operates on a stream carrying no session secrets, else abort.
R-A4MUST Startup self-check — including the live port surface
On listen, assert the genuinely-runtime invariants and refuse to listen otherwise: the only reachable stream constructor is the PAKE-secured one; the trust anchor equals the pinned value; verification-method == use-permanent-password / approve-mode == password read back (through Config::get_option) as the pinned policy values, and BUILTIN_SETTINGS and HARD_SETTINGS are empty so the get_builtin_option funnel and the HARD_SETTINGS/preset-password funnel carry no managed override or preset credential (R-S16(d)(iv)/(v)); the empty-permanent-password case is enforced at runtime rather than by this startup gate — while no permanent password is set the direct listener binds nothing (the R-S9 park) and every connection is refused per-connection (R-S9), so an unprovisioned box fails closed by presenting no listener; and the process's bound sockets match the audited surface — exactly one TCP listener on the pinned v4 port (21118, R-F4) once bound (and legitimately none while that park holds), and zero UDP listeners (enumerate /proc/self/net/{tcp,udp,udp6} after startup; a stray UDP listener means the LAN module or a probe leaked back in, R-D4/R-X5). Assert zero UDP sockets of any kind on the running --server process — not merely "no UDP listener." There is no carve-out for an ephemeral NAT-punch UDP socket, because the deletions make both roles TCP-only — the mediator (R-D4), KCP and udp_nat_connect (R-S13(d)), and LAN discovery (R-X5) are gone from the tree — so the --server process legitimately binds no UDP. (A box that is also viewing connects out over TCP; an outbound connect binds no UDP and is not a listener.) Enumerate /proc/self/net/{udp,udp6} and refuse to listen if any UDP socket exists — closing the blind spot whereby an ephemeral (OS-assigned-port, non-listening) UDP egress socket — a STUN probe (R-S11) or a dependency phoning home — would slip past a listener-only check (§4.1). (That the updater/plugin/mediator modules are absent (deleted), that the terminal is policy-pinned ON (full control — R-X8/R-S16) and mod client is unreachable from the --server entry (R-A6 call-graph) — and that egress is silent (R-D6) and the IPC write-allowlist and action-arm handling (R-S11) hold — are compile-time/CI facts proven in R-A6 plus, for egress, the operator firewall; they are not re-checkable runtime assertions. R-A4 is a bind/listener-surface check: an outbound TCP connect has no listener row, so TCP-egress silence rests on R-D6's compile-time removal plus the firewall, not on this assertion — acceptance criterion 8 MUST NOT over-credit R-A4 with catching egress. The two tiers are not interchangeable, and §9's "guarded by a check" is met at whichever tier — runtime, build, or test — each property names.) macOS · iOS source: this socket self-check reads /proc/self/net/*, which does not exist on Apple — so a macOS build MUST use a Darwin equivalent (proc_pidfdinfo / sysctl net.inet.*.pcblist) or record that the runtime check is unavailable and the surface rests on the §18 compile-out plus the build smoke-test (R-B4); iOS binds no inbound socket, so the check is moot there.
R-A5MUST Frame-length cap + no-follow writes asserted
Assert max_packet_length is set (not usize::MAX) on every connection and that writes use no-follow opens (R-S8) — all before any byte is accepted/written. Also assert the secretbox layer has two distinct per-direction keys engaged (R-P2/R-P10) — never one shared key both ways — and that a counter approaching wraparound forces a fresh-key rekey, not a counter reset. This is the one inherited catastrophic bug (same (key, nonce) both directions); the wire-capture test (R-A9) proves ciphertext but would not catch a regression to a single shared key, so key-distinctness MUST be asserted directly — and on the engaged cipher state, not the derived values: the assertion MUST read back the send-key and recv-key actually installed in the running Encrypt and confirm they differ. Comparing the two derived keys (k_c2s ≠ k_s2c, R-A10) is insufficient — they differ by HKDF construction even if a regression re-engages a single key for both directions (the precise way the catastrophic bug returns, and one R-A9 cannot see). The assertion MUST further confirm the role→direction binding (R-P2): the responder seals with k_s2c / opens with k_c2s, the initiator the mirror.

9.2 Build-time (insecure code cannot compile/link)

R-A6MUST CI proves the excised & deleted paths are absent
CI fails if the plaintext constructor, the hardcoded-false secure flag, a b"direct-ok"-style constant ack, the updater/plugin modules, custom_server, the ConfigureUpdate body, the temporary-password mechanism (TEMPORARY_PASSWORD/update_temporary_password), or any "trust unverified"/key-override config reappear (grep/AST guards). Deletion is verified, not assumed. The exact tokens CI greps (each must return zero hits in the shipped build): mod updater · "--update" · "download-new-version"/"update-me" (R-X1); plugin_framework · mod plugin · install_plugin_with_url · --plugin-install (R-X2); the ConfigureUpdate arm (R-X3); mod custom_server · "--config" · "--import-config" · get_custom_server_from_string (R-X4); TEMPORARY_PASSWORD · update_temporary_password · check_update_temporary_password · get_auto_password · use-temporary-password · add_trusted_device · Auth2fa · require_2fa · generate2fa/verify2fa · the totp-rs crate (R-X7); SignedId · set_signed_id · Union::PublicKey · box_:: in the connection handshake · the secure parameter (R-P5/R-P14); set_raw (R-S5 — absent, or assert-only); direct-ok and any constant byte-string ack (R-P3 — must stay absent); the lan.rs broadcast listener / RENDEZVOUS_PORT + 3 bind (R-X5); uri.authority == "config"/"password" and the DEBUG_BOOT_COMPLETED manifest action (R-X6). Plus the controlled-policy AST checks (R-S16): every key R-S16 names is present in PINNED_SETTINGS; Config::get_option (config.rs) consults PINNED_SETTINGS before get_or and is_option_can_save rejects every pinned key; the security resolvers (verification_method/approve_mode/is_permission_enabled_locally) reach config only through Config::get_option (never a direct CONFIG2.options/OVERWRITE_SETTINGS read that bypasses the pin, and — left unmodified — not rewritten to a hand-rolled constant either); no KEYS_BUILDIN_SETTINGS security key (one-way-file-transfer, allow-logon-screen-password, one-way-clipboard-redirection, enable-perm-change-in-accept-window) is read via get_builtin_option without a matching pin, and BUILTIN_SETTINGS is asserted empty (R-S16(d)(iv)); permission() takes its early is_permission_enabled_locally return and never consults control_permissions; and the SwitchPermission widener is absent. Plus a check that get_direct_port() returns the pinned 21118 literal (not the inherited RENDEZVOUS_PORT + 2 derivation) and never reads direct-access-port from config (R-F4); and that the direct listener in start_direct_only() starts unconditionally, not gated on a direct-server option (R-D2/R-D4). Add the terminal grant check — enable-terminal is pinned Y in PINNED_SETTINGS so the Self::permission(OPTION_ENABLE_TERMINAL, …) gate (connection.rs) resolves true through the funnel and admits the LoginRequest.Terminal arm, while is_option_can_save still rejects every runtime write and the SwitchPermission widener is absent so the single mode cannot be flipped at runtime (R-X8/R-S16); and the os_login/PAM tokens — pam::Client · authenticate · try_start_x_session · LoginRequest.os_login must be unreachable from the connection accept path (call-graph, R-X14); and a positive AST check that no Config::set*/save_config is reachable from a peer-message arm in io_loop.rs except through the viewer config-write allowlist (R-S15). Host-identity removal tokens (ex-R-S17, now R-P5): the retired host-key pin leaves zero hits for the HostIdentity frame/message, --get-fingerprint, build_host_identity/verify_host_identity, the rustdesk-fork/host-proof label, and any viewer-side known_hosts/pin store — the box presents no host-proof and the viewer pins and compares nothing (R-P5). Address-normalization KAT (R-SV5): the peer-address normalization (lowercase, IDNA to_ascii, default-port fill, IPv4 canonicalize) keeps its one frozen KAT so the re-keyed peer list feeding Client::_start (R-SV5) is deterministic. GUI-conformance tokens (§19, extending this grep into the Dart/asset/lang/ layers): the dead lang/ keys (connecting_status · not_ready_status · relay_hint_tip · the /r/@public clauses of id_input_tip · web_id_input_tip · websocket_tip · enable-2fa-title/enable-bot-tip · powered_by_me), the deleted assets (insecure.svg · secure_relay.svg · insecure_relay.svg · the unused auth-*.svg OIDC icons), the removed widgets/symbols (the numeric IDTextInputFormatter · buildPasswordBoard2's OTP path · the "ID/Relay Server" + changeSocks5Proxy editors · the verification-method/approve-mode selectors on every front-end · _forceAlwaysRelayAction/_wolAction · the update banner) and the rustdesk.com/github.com/rustdesk link literals MUST return zero — exactly as this gate already greps download-new-version/enable-2fa on the Rust side — plus the positive check that the connect box rejects a bare ID (R-SV10). One-authentication tokens (R-S18): zero hits for the OSLogin message · LoginRequest.os_login/.password/.hwid fields · ElevationRequest/ElevationRequestWithLogon · SwitchSidesRequest/SwitchSidesResponse · Hash (message.proto); the server paths pam::Client/authenticate · try_start_x_session · LogonUserW/CreateProcessWithLogonW · prepare_terminal_login_for_authorization · handle_elevation_request; and the client senders set_logon/Data::ElevateWithLogon (io_loop.rs), os_login: Some(OSLogin (client.rs), set_switch_sides_response, and the persisted os-username/os-password options — so no second OS credential survives on either side or on the wire. FSM-collapse tokens (R-S2): because authorization collapses onto the CPace KEYED edge and the legacy second authentication is deleted from the tree, these must return zero hits — validate_password · verify_h1 · is_recent_session · SwitchSidesResponse · the Hash{salt,challenge} emission (set_hash / the on_open hash send). The one surviving accept edge — the CM ipc::Data::Authorize arm (attended GUI accept) — is retained only downstream of the PAKE, so the positive AST check also holds: self.authorized=true has exactly one assignment site (connection.rs, inside send_logon_response_and_keep_alive) and every caller of it — the password path and the CM arm — is reachable only downstream of the CPace KEYED edge (none runs on an unkeyed stream). Two-key cipher tokens (R-P2/R-P10/R-A5): the symmetric single-key keying MUST NOT survive — grep that no single-key Encrypt::new(key) / single-arg symmetric set_key remains (the rewritten set_key carries a role/direction), plus the R-A5 check that the engaged send-key and recv-key differ (not merely the derived k_c2s/k_s2c values). Role-reachability checks (R-R2b — one binary): mod client, the VP8/VP9 video decode path (scrap's VpxDecoder; AV1/libaom is runtime-quarantined by Appendix C #87), the flutter UI, and the rustdesk:// deep-link receiver are present — the one binary is a full viewer — so absence is not a symbol grep here; CI MUST instead assert by call-graph/AST that they are unreachable from the --server/service entry (start_os_service/start_server), the build-time twin of R-A4's live socket-surface assertion. The hardware codecs (hwcodec/vram/ffmpeg HW) are compiled out on every build (CPU-only), so their absence is a sound symbol-grep; the software decoder stays linked (a viewer needs it) but the headless --server instantiates none, which R-A4 asserts on the live process. Where a mod client grep is used it MUST target crate::client::, not a bare client::, since four unrelated inner modules (keyboard::client · portable_service::client · uinput::client · rdp_input::client) would false-positive. (The genuinely deleted surface — mod kcp_stream, the mediator, the updater/plugin — is symbol-grep-absent, above.) And the egress tokens — post_conn_audit · post_alarm_audit · post_file_audit · record_upload · send_2fa_code_to_telegram/api.telegram.org must be unreachable from the accept path, or the api-server/bot/2fa options pinned empty in PINNED_SETTINGS (R-D6/R-S16). Critical caveat on the UDP/probe guards: the rendezvous probes (test_nat_type, test_rendezvous_server, stun_ipv4_test/stun_ipv6_test/test_bind_ipv6) and the UDP constructors (new_udp_for, FramedSocket::new, UdpSocket::bind) live in common.rs/socket_client.rs and are shared library code linked into the one build — so a tree-wide zero-hit grep on those symbols is wrong (they stay compiled for legitimate uses and a grep would pressure deleting needed code). The guard MUST instead be a call-graph / AST check that those symbols are unreachable from the controlled-service entry (start_os_service/start_server), not a symbol-absence grep. (Excising the mediator, R-D4, removes the start_all caller — but it is not the only service-path reach: the IPC handler also reaches test_rendezvous_server via Data::TestRendezvousServer (ipc.rs) and test_nat_type via the CheckTestNatType Drop guard (ipc.rs), independent of start_all — so the call-graph check MUST traverse Drop impls and IPC Data arms, and those reaches MUST be severed (R-S11/R-D4) or the check fails. The viewer-side callers — flutter_ffi, the cli feature, mod client — are present but unreachable from the --server entry (covered by the same call-graph check), so the residual the service entry can reach is exactly this IPC reach.) The check MUST run per target — including from the Android JNI startServer entry (R-D7), a third service-path root alongside start_os_service/start_server. Add the completeness tokens from the pre-auth sweep: the Linux deep-link D-Bus transport (org.rustdesk.rustdesk · NewConnection · start_dbus_server, R-X6); the TestNatResponse and hbbs_http::sync::handle_config_options (sync.rs) server-list-rewrite twins (common.rs, R-X3); and the IPC write-arms beyond the trio — with the legacy proxy/config write shapes asserted absent and any future handler write pinned by reachability/count gates — which R-A6 MUST enforce as the completeness check: a positive assertion that no Config::set*/set_socks/set_permanent_password/set_id/set_salt/set_unlock_pin is reachable from the IPC handle() dispatch (including via Drop impls) except through named receiver-authorized operations — so completeness across every write-arm is machine-checked, not left to per-arm review. Plus a positive AST check that the unkeyed-stream handshake decodes only the dedicated Cpace message (parse_from_bytes::<Cpace>) and that Message::parse_from_bytes is unreachable before set_key (R-P14/R-S7). Per-target Apple pass (R-R2 source-conformance): because iOS/macOS are not built but the source MUST conform, CI MUST also run these greps under cargo check --target {x86_64,aarch64}-apple-darwin and aarch64-apple-ios, adding the Apple-cfg tokens: the macOS DMG updater (update_me/update_from_dmg/extract_update_dmg, R-X1), the two root-exec primitives (elevate · AuthorizationExecuteWithPrivileges, R-X9/R-X11), the _url sender-auth (start_ipc_url_server, R-X6), the iOS deep-link write authorities and the ?key= adoption (R-X6), get_key() ignoring the "key" override on Apple (R-X4), and the §18 egress tokens (R-SV10); plus the negative assertion that pam::Client/try_start_x_session are absent on macOS (R-X14). And — because entitlements and Info.plist keys live only in the Xcode layer, invisible to cargo check/cfg — a full per-key allow-list across all four entitlements files: macos/Runner/Release.entitlements, macos/Runner/Profile.entitlements, macos/Runner/Debug.entitlements, and ios/Runner/Runner.entitlements. The Apple pass asserts every key is allow-listed or justified, and that com.apple.security.cs.allow-jit appears only in Debug because Flutter Profile/Release are AOT; plus — extending the same per-key allow-list to both Info.plist files (the sweep otherwise stops at the entitlements): the iOS UIFileSharingEnabled/UISupportsDocumentBrowser (both true upstream, exposing the Documents container), the privacy usage strings (NSCameraUsageDescription/NSPhotoLibraryUsageDescription on iOS, NSMicrophoneUsageDescription on macOS — the capability-prompt twins of the swept device.audio-input entitlement), the CFBundleURLSchemes deep-link registration on both plists (R-X6, not iOS-only), and ITSAppUsesNonExemptEncryption (=false upstream — stale once the PAKE lands since the build is now cryptographic; remove or set correctly) — each off or justified. And two further Xcode-layer surfaces cargo cannot see: a per-pod allow-list over ios/Podfile.lock and macos/Podfile.lock — every pod justified against a preserved Flutter-plugin feature, CI failing on any addition (the present SDWebImage fetches remote images and MUST be justified or dropped; assert no Firebase/FCM/Crashlytics/Sparkle/analytics pod appears, R-SV8); and a grep over both Runner.xcodeproj/project.pbxproj that every PBXShellScriptBuildPhase is one of the four stock Flutter/CocoaPods phases, so a future fetch-and-phone-home run-script (the classic Crashlytics upload-symbols vector, invisible to cargo/plists/Podfile) fails the gate.

R-A6 helper-provenance companion: privileged or privilege-adjacent Rust cleanup paths MUST NOT invoke PATH-selected helpers when a native syscall/API exists. The Linux clipboard FUSE stale-unmount path is pinned to direct no-follow umount2, with CI rejecting Command::new("umount") and helper fallbacks.

R-A7MUST Pinned crypto; clean dependency audit; --locked
curve25519-dalek pinned ≥ 4.1.3 (RUSTSEC-2024-0344); the in-tree CPace implementation gated on the draft's published test vectors (plus a self-consistency KAT for the confirmation MAC, which has none); scripts/audit.sh runs cargo-audit and cargo-deny check advisories against the pinned RustSec snapshot with only documented deny.toml accepts; scripts/dart-audit.sh runs the Dart/Pub OSV gate against the pinned OSV snapshot; release builds use --locked; no alternate cipher suite or compatibility mode compiled in.

9.3 Test-time (adversarial proof)

R-A8MUST Injection is dead across all five types (all five reachable — the terminal is pinned ON by the policy, R-X8)
Per type, an active-attacker test proves post-auth injected frames are rejected (tag/sequence failure) and a first-contact MITM cannot complete key confirmation. Any landed injection fails the build. A separate test MUST prove the per-IP limiter (R-S10) is owner-safe: a flood from one source never blocks a different source, a tripped block decays (no permanent lockout), and a key-confirmation failure emits the structured record — the cardinal "a limiter must never lock the owner out of their own machine" property, otherwise untested.
R-A9MUST Wire-capture proves no plaintext — including the RDP tunnel
Capture a port-forwarded/RDP session and assert the bytes are indistinguishable from random; assert the login transcript yields nothing offline-crackable.
R-A10MUST PAKE known-answer & transcript-binding tests
The draft's published test vectors (which cover the protocol through ISK), plus a self-consistency KAT for the R-P3 confirmation MAC (both sides compute equal tags; any mutated input — including CI's role/port (§10.4) — flips the tag; no published vector exists for this step), plus negative tests that mutate each transcript element (ephemerals, sid, the version label / CI, AD) and assert the handshake aborts. Add a KAT that the two derived directional keys satisfy k_c2s ≠ k_s2c (R-P2/R-P10) — but this is necessary, not sufficient: add a two-peer end-to-end test driving an actual controlled↔viewer handshake and asserting each direction decrypts only under the mirrored key (responder seals k_s2c/opens k_c2s; initiator the mirror), which catches a role-binding inversion or a single-key regression that the derived-value KAT and the wire-capture test (R-A9) cannot (R-A5). Add a project KAT over the fork-specific CI byte-encoding (no draft vector covers it, R-P1). Add a second project KAT over a full 32-byte production sid (sid_a‖sid_b, R-P1/R-P14): the §10.4 anchor reuses the draft vector's 16-byte sid for reproducibility, but a live session always carries 32 bytes, so the 16-byte KAT never exercises the production sid length. The construction is parametric in sid length (length-prefixed in the generator and ISK; bare-concatenated under the fixed-width 64-byte ISK trailer in mac_key, which stays unambiguous), so a conformant implementation passes both — but the 32-byte KAT is what stops an implementer hard-coding a 16-byte sid buffer (§10.4 pins both anchors). Add a non-ASCII PRS KAT exercising the password normalization (R-P1): a combining-character password (e.g. "Café" with a decomposed e+U+0301) MUST normalize to its NFC form and yield the expected g/ISK, and an empty-after-normalization input MUST be rejected (R-S9) — the ASCII "Password" anchors never exercise normalization. (The fork's NFC-only normalization deliberately does not perform the OpaqueString non-ASCII-space map or FreeformClass rejection, R-P1; a non-ASCII-space password is therefore expected to differ from a full-OpaqueString peer — acceptable because both ends run the same build and any mismatch fails closed.) The negative AD test MUST confirm the abort is taken by the explicit ADa/ADb equality check (R-P5), not only by incidental transcript divergence. The suite MUST further include wire-choreography negative tests against the R-P14a state machine: a step delivered out of order (③ before ①), a duplicated step (two ① frames), a partial/dribbled frame that never completes (asserting the R-P14b timeout fires and the connection drops), a frame exceeding the ≤ 4 KiB pre-PAKE cap, and a replayed prior-connection transcript (asserting it aborts at the R-P3 tag, not via any sid cache). Each MUST assert fail-closed abort with no set_key, and that only the tag-mismatch case increments the R-S10 limiter — extending R-A8's owner-safe property to the pre-key path.

10. The PAKE specification

The handshake is small but unforgiving. Every step below is normative to the byte — each is a place real PAKE deployments have failed — and the construction follows the CPace draft and its published security proof, not intuition.

10.1 Primitive, suite & implementation

Primitive — balanced CPace

CPace, a balanced composable PAKE that authenticates from the shared password alone, with a published formal proof (Abdalla–Haase–Hesse 2021, adaptive UC). Suite: ristretto255 + SHA-512. This is a deliberate, documented deviation: the CFRG draft's recommended suites are CPACE-X25519-SHA512 (first) and the NIST-P curves; ristretto255 is a permitted "can also be implemented" option, chosen here because it is a prime-order group — eliminating by construction the cofactor / small-subgroup / twist pitfalls the draft enumerates for X25519.

Implementation reality — implement the draft; the crate is non-conformant

Settled empirically (and independently re-verified). A from-scratch draft-21 CPACE-RISTR255-SHA512 reference passes all of the CFRG draft's published ristretto255 test vectors (from github.com/cfrg/draft-irtf-cfrg-cpace): 18/18 protocol checks + 13/13 encoding checks, both abort cases included. Those published values are the derived CPace generator g = 222b6b19… (not the ristretto255 base point) and the symmetric-mode ISK ISK_SY = 544199d7… — the canonical ristretto255 vector, carried in testvectors.json under the group codename G_Coffee25519 and matching the draft body's worked example (the order-independent o_cat transcript reproduces it, R-P6). (testvectors.md lists the same vector — identical PRS/sid/scalars/AD — under a different example CI, giving a6fc82c3…/980dcc5a…; that is a channel-identifier difference, not a second vector, so do not conflate the two.) Computing jedisct1's pake-cpace on the same inputs diverges: the crate is on-the-wire non-conformant — it implements the 2019 draft-haase-cpace-01, unaudited, last released 2023. Its security-relevant deltas (no sid in the ISK, initiator-chosen sid, no key confirmation) live inside the hashed transcript and cannot be patched at the application layer — fixing them means forking lib.rs, i.e. rewriting draft-21 minus the vectors. There is no viable "use the crate" path.

Therefore the fork MUST implement draft-21 CPACE-RISTR255-SHA512 in-tree over curve25519-dalek — zero novel curve math (dalek supplies the audited, constant-time from_uniform_bytes map, scalar×point, decompress, is_identity; you write lv_cat/o_cat, the 64-byte generator string, the ISK transcript, and the R-P3 MACs). The construction core (encoding + generator/DH + ISK/MAC) is ~150–270 lines; counting everything §10 makes mandatory and security-relevant — the R-P14a handshake state machine, the two-key secretbox rewrite (R-P2/R-P10, which ripples through tcp.rs/websocket.rs/stream.rs), bounded reads (R-P14b), the dedicated Cpace protobuf, and the dual-role integration — the security-relevant diff is ~450–850 lines, with a comparably-sized KAT/negative-test harness on top. CI MUST gate on the draft's published test vectors — they catch the real failure modes (encoding off-by-ones, the zero-pad length prefix, 32-vs-64 hash length, transcript ordering, the aborts). Pin curve25519-dalek ≥ 4.1.3 (RUSTSEC-2024-0344). This adds no new external crate: every primitive the handshake needs is already in the 1.4.7 lock — curve25519-dalek 4.1.3, sha2 0.10.8, hkdf 0.12.4, hmac 0.12.1, subtle 2.6.1, zeroize 1.8.1, rand 0.8.5 (CSPRNG; getrandom 0.2.15 also present), and unicode-normalization (NFC, present via idna) for the PRS — which holds only because the PRS normalization is NFC-only, a deliberate subset of RFC 8265 OpaqueString (R-P1); a fully-conformant OpaqueString would need a new precis-profiles crate, which this fork does not add, and the inherited session layer is sodiumoxide 0.2.7 (libsodium: secretbox + the Ed25519 device key to be removed per R-P5 — there is no ed25519-dalek) — so the PAKE adds no new dependency, over crates already compiled in (exact lock versions in §3.2). The §11 review is vector-checkable for the construction core (encoding, generator derivation, ISK), but its scope is not bounded by that small line count: the R-P14a state machine, the two-key secretbox rewrite, the constant-time/identity guarantees, and the R-P3 confirmation-MAC composition (which has no published vector — its self-consistency KAT cannot catch a self-consistently-wrong composition) require review by someone fluent in protocol/nonce-misuse analysis. The independent expert audit R-V3 mandates is therefore necessary, not merely tractable-because-small.

No registry, no stored keys — the most sovereign anchor there is

Because a balanced PAKE authenticates from the shared password, this fork uses no long-term public keys, no signatures, no certificates, and no key registry of any kind. TLS anchors trust in CA registries; SSH anchors it in known_hosts (trust-on-first-use — a local registry). The PAKE anchors it in the password you already share — nothing to store, distribute, pin, or look up. One credential, one primitive, one path; no second PAKE to select, negotiate, or fall back to. (The per-connection ephemeral group elements on the wire are key-exchange values, not identities, and are kept nowhere.)

10.2 The handshake

R-P1MUST Inputs & channel binding
PRS = base64(Argon2id(pwd, salt)) — a memory-hard salted hash, not the plaintext. pwd = the NFC-normalized, no-case-fold UTF-8 of the permanent password (the sole credential, since the one-time-password path is excised — R-X7); salt = a fixed domain-separation constant, SHA256("rustdesk-cpace-prs-salt-v1")[..16] — the same 16 bytes compiled into every build, bound to no per-box value. It is computed with libsodium crypto_pwhash (Argon2id) at the INTERACTIVE ops/mem limits — phone-safe, since Android/iOS viewers derive it too — with a 32-byte output, base64-encoded to a fixed-length ASCII string; that string is the PRS the §10.4 construction consumes as its input (the §10.4 published-vector anchors pin that construction over the draft's fixed literal PRS for reproducibility, R-A10, while production feeds this Argon2id value). Both ends derive the same PRS with no salt exchange — the fixed constant salt is compiled into both roles, so the box (at --password time) and the viewer (at connect time, from the typed password) reach an identical PRS from the password alone, with nothing per-box to distribute, pin, or agree; an empty PRS still yields no usable credential (fail-closed, R-S9). At-rest honesty (fixed salt): the salt is not a secret and never was — the old host-key-derived salt was equally public (the box hands its Ed25519 key to every viewer that connects) and deterministic — so at-rest confidentiality never rested on it and is unchanged: the stored PRS is still the memory-hard Argon2id hash, never the plaintext. The one property a fixed global salt drops relative to a per-box one is cross-box precomputation-separation (a single Argon2id dictionary now applies to every fork box under a given password), but that bears only on offline guessing across many endpoints, which §2 places out of scope (endpoint at-rest read and password secrecy are assumed); the in-scope posture (no plaintext, memory-hard) does not change, and this is not a strengthening. The NFC normalization (now the Argon2id input) is a deliberate NFC-only subset of the RFC 8265 OpaqueString profile: it applies NFC (the only normalizer in the lock — unicode-normalization via idna) but not OpaqueString's non-ASCII-space→U+0020 map or FreeformClass disallowed-codepoint rejection — chosen so the PAKE adds no new crate (§10.1), and safe because both peers run the same fork build (R-R2b), so any normalization divergence fails closed at the R-P3 tag (wrong PRS → divergent generator → divergent ISK → abort) rather than weakening; a balanced PAKE compares by exact PRS equality, so there is no collision surface. The password MUST be non-empty after normalization (R-S9) — CPace has no empty-PRS guard — and a non-ASCII KAT exercises this (R-A10). Because CPace is balanced, the controlled side must hold this PRS — a connect-equivalent value, but now the Argon2id salted hash rather than the plaintext password — to run the handshake; protect it as R-S9 requires. The channel identifier CI MUST carry the protocol/version label and any per-connection channel binding, and MUST be identical and pre-agreed on both sides (CI is never sent on the wire). The session id sid MUST be a unique per-connection value agreed by both parties — e.g. a fresh 16-byte random nonce contributed by each side, concatenated initiator-then-responder (a 32-byte sid). That contribution exchange rides the still-unkeyed wire pre-PAKE, so its integrity rests solely on the R-P3 confirmation abort: sid MUST enter the ISK transcript (draft-21 symmetric — the ISK_SY vector), not merely the generator, so a tampered nonce yields a divergent ISK/mac_key and aborts. CI MUST be a fixed both-sides-identical string (fork tag · suite · version · port binding) — the per-side role instead lives in AD (ADa=b"viewer" / ADb=b"server"), since the draft requires both sides to hold the same CI; no per-connection value that would need a tamperable exchange. The port folded into CI MUST be the pinned compile-time direct-access port (21118, R-F4) — the same constant compiled into both roles, never a runtime option and never a value read from the live socket (NAT/port-forwarding would otherwise desync CI between peers and silently abort the handshake) — and its byte-encoding MUST be pinned (§10.4); since no published draft vector exercises a fork-specific CI, the build MUST add a project KAT over CI and the derived generator (R-A10). No identity keys (R-P5).
R-P2MUST Exchange & key derivation
Each side sends its CPace message (ephemeral group element); both derive the Intermediate Session Key ISK. The raw DH output K MUST NOT be exposed, logged, or used directly (a leaked K re-enables offline attack). From ISK, derive two directional secretbox keys (controlled→viewer and viewer→controlled) by HKDF-SHA-512 with distinct info labels; derive the confirmation key separately by the draft's own construction (mac_key = H("CPaceMac" ‖ sid ‖ ISK), R-P3). All three are domain-separated by construction — MUST NOT slice one hash for several keys, nor reuse one key across both directions (R-P10). Role→direction binding (load-bearing): both peers derive the same k_c2s and k_s2c, so correctness depends on each installing them in mirrored slots — the controlled side (responder) seals with k_s2c and opens with k_c2s; the viewer (initiator) the mirror. The inherited symmetric set_key(key) (one key both directions) MUST be replaced by a keying call that carries the role/direction, and the symmetric form MUST NOT survive (R-A6); a mis-binding that engages one key both ways re-creates the catastrophic reuse and is not caught by the derived-value k_c2s ≠ k_s2c KAT — only by R-A5's engaged-key assertion and R-A10's two-peer test.
R-P3MUST Explicit mutual key confirmation (the proven construction, not a transcript MAC)
After ISK: mac_key = H("CPaceMac" ‖ sid ‖ ISK); each party sends MAC(mac_key, lv_cat(its-own element, its-AD)); each MUST verify the peer's tag in constant time and abort before authorizing. The MAC MUST be HMAC-SHA-512 (the draft offers HMAC or CMAC; HMAC-SHA-512 matches the suite hash and carries its own RFC 4231 test vectors). This is the CPace draft's own Key confirmation construction, verbatim — the draft defines a single key-confirmation MAC — the parallel/no-message-ordering property of our symmetric mode (R-P6) lives in the order-independent o_cat ISK transcript that feeds mac_key, not in a distinct MAC variant — so this is the draft's construction, not an ad-hoc transcript MAC. The reply MUST NOT be a constant — it is a function of ISK by construction (the fix for the prior fork's b"direct-ok"). Do not MAC "the whole transcript and both identities" — there are no identities, and that diverges from the construction the proof covers. Explicit confirmation also upgrades CPace's forward secrecy from weak to perfect. In the symmetric mode these tags are sender-bound only once R-P5's role/AD discipline is applied (otherwise a tag is reflectable in a loopback) — R-P3 MUST NOT be implemented without R-P5.
R-P4MUST One handshake at the choke point, before the type is parsed
Run once where the single stream is created (server.rs), keying all six transports and all five types before any application message.
R-P5MUST No PKI/registry; defend the loopback
No trust anchor beyond the shared password — no CA, certificate, registry, rendezvous-issued identity signature, and (with the host-key pin retired, ex-R-S17) no long-term per-box identity key and no local pin of any kind: the fork holds no cryptographic identity, authentication is the password via CPace, and nothing about a per-box key is persisted, pinned, or proven (R-P1/§10). The existing SignedId/PublicKey/Ed25519 session-key bootstrap in the connection handshake is removed (§8) — but that is the device-identity keypair only: the rendezvous trust anchor (RS_PUB_KEY, R-X4) stays only as a baked constant. A server-assisted build would have needed the register_pk/key_confirmed registration-liveness gate (rendezvous_mediator.rs) preserved or the box goes unreachable — but §18 (R-SV4) compiles the mediator out of every shipped artifact, so there is no server-assisted build to preserve it for: register_pk is absent (R-SV10 greps it out), not preserved, and this carve-out is now dead for everything the fork ships (the direct-only deployment already sidestepped it). Consequence of having no identities: CPace will key with anyone presenting the same PRS+sid — including the host looping back to itself. The fork MUST add a loopback/relay defense: assign fixed initiator vs responder roles per (PRS,sid) and forbid one host playing both, or enforce unique ADa/ADb and reject a received AD that matches a locally-active session. Resolved (§10.4): pin fixed roles — the controlled side is always responder, the viewer always initiator — and carry the role as the per-side associated data ADa = b"viewer" (initiator) / ADb = b"server" (responder), with only the shared suite/version/port in CI (the draft requires both sides to hold the same CI, so the role cannot live there). Because AD is per-side, sent, and folded into both the transcript and the R-P3 tag, each tag is sender-bound, so a host cannot reflect one role's messages as the other for one (PRS,sid) — reflection fails the role/AD-equality check. (The residual self-loopback — a host genuinely driving both state machines against itself — still completes, but is bounded by the deliberate-connect posture (a box run purely as --server never initiates), not by the AD check.) The AD check MUST be explicit: the responder MUST verify the received ADa == b"viewer" and the initiator MUST verify ADb == b"server", aborting on mismatch — folding the received AD into the transcript is necessary but not sufficient; the literal equality check is what makes each confirmation tag sender-bound.

10.3 Construction details (each is a place real PAKEs have failed)

R-P6MUST Symmetric-mode ordering
Both ends are peers, so message order is undefined ⇒ the symmetric setting: combine with the draft's lexicographic o_cat(lv_cat(Ya,ADa), lv_cat(Yb,ADb)), OR impose fixed initiator/responder roles (which also satisfies R-P5). Never a fixed Ya‖Yb order without roles. Note: the fork pins fixed roles and uses the symmetric o_cat transcript — a deliberate superset of the draft's initiator-responder transcript_ir (safe: o_cat binds both elements + ADs). The ISK_SY vector is therefore the gating KAT; an implementer MUST NOT substitute transcript_ir (it fails the vector and breaks interoperability with a conformant fork peer).
R-P7MUST Abort on invalid / identity point
If the shared point is the group identity, or a received element fails ristretto255 decode(), abort — never continue with a degenerate key. The trap: the all-zeros encoding decodes successfully to the identity, so decode-success alone is insufficient — the K.is_identity() test after the multiply is what catches a malicious identity input (§10.4).
R-P8MUST sid uniqueness; no secret reuse
sid unique per connection (e.g. random_a ‖ random_b); ephemeral secret scalars and sid MUST NOT be reused.
R-P9MUST Pinned domain separation
Pin the exact DSI strings for the chosen draft version and a distinct HKDF label per key; a DSI mismatch MUST make keys not agree, never silently cross-confuse.
R-P10MUST secretbox nonce discipline
The 24-byte XSalsa20-Poly1305 nonce MUST be unique per key and never reused — including across directions. The inherited layer derives the nonce from a per-direction sequence counter alone (tcp.rs get_nonce = LE64(seq), no direction tag) and installs one key with both counters at 0 (set_key Encrypt(key,0,0)) — so a single shared key makes each side's frame n seal a different plaintext under the same (key, nonce): catastrophic XSalsa20-Poly1305 reuse. Per-direction keys (R-P2) close it — the invariant is key-uniqueness, not nonce-uniqueness, so identical per-direction counters from 0 are safe and one MUST NOT "optimize" back to a single key; also specify a derived-counter generation and an abort/rekey policy before counter wraparound. A rekey MUST install fresh per-direction keys — never reset the counters under the existing keys, which would re-create the very (key, nonce) reuse this requirement closes. (The u64 counters make wraparound unreachable in practice — millennia at any real frame rate — so the rekey is defensive belt-and-suspenders against a reset bug, not the load-bearing closure; the closure is key-distinctness, which is why R-A5 asserts the engaged send/recv keys differ, not merely that the derived values do. The pre-increment makes the first nonce LE64(1), not 0 — immaterial to the argument.) Ripple — not a one-file change: the two-key fix reshapes the public Encrypt tuple (tcp.rs) and changes the set_key signature to carry the role/direction (R-P2) across Stream::set_key's three variants (stream.rs) and the WsFramedStream that reuses tcp::Encrypt (websocket.rs). The peer↔peer relay session is keyed at the same choke point (server.rs) so R-P14 covers it; the rendezvous-signaling secure_tcp path (common.rs) is removed by R-D4 (the fork is direct-only, so no rendezvous-signaling path is left to touch).
R-P11MUST No downgrade, no suite negotiation
Hard-pin one suite, one draft version, one DSI set; carry the version label in CI so any mismatch yields non-agreeing keys. No negotiation handshake exists to downgrade. The pinned draft is draft-irtf-cfrg-cpace-21 — the current CFRG revision as of 2026-06 (latest revision 2026-04-22, in IESG review; not yet an RFC), and its published ristretto255 vectors are exactly the ones §10.4 reproduces — which MUST be taken from the draft-irtf-cfrg-cpace-21 tag (or vendored), not the live CFRG master, whose later example-CI change produces different vector values (R-A10). If CPace later reaches RFC, adopt it only as a deliberate, re-vectored change — never an automatic version bump.
R-P12MUST K-secrecy & constant-time
K MUST NOT be exposed or logged (only ISK leaves the PAKE layer). Generator computation, scalar sampling, scalar multiplication, and every tag comparison MUST be constant-time (curve25519-dalek provides this for the curve ops — assert it). Scalar sampling MUST use Scalar::from_bytes_mod_order_wide / Scalar::random, never the deprecated Edwards-only Scalar::from_bits (§10.4); and the tag comparison is the one CT op not inside dalek — use a constant-time comparator (subtle / Mac::verify), never ==. Constant-timeness MUST be machine-checked — a dudect-style statistical timing test in CI over the sampling and the tag compare, plus the type-level guarantees of subtle/curve25519-dalek — so it is verified, not reviewed by eye — removing the timing axis from the review burden (but not the state-machine, nonce-misuse, or MAC-composition axes, which still need expert review — §11/R-V3). Secret-wiping is manual, not automatic: curve25519-dalek 4.1.3's Scalar/RistrettoPoint implement the Zeroize trait only — no Drop/ZeroizeOnDrop — so the zeroize crate's presence is not coverage: the CPace code MUST explicitly .zeroize() the ephemeral scalars (y_a/y_b), the DH point K, and the PRS-derived material, and R-V3's audit MUST confirm it.
R-P13MUST Quantum-annoying, not PQ — record it
CPace is "quantum-annoying" (a quantum attacker solves one CDH per password guess), not post-quantum. The port folds into CI and the role into the transcript-bound AD (§10.4 keeps role out of CI — both sides must hold the same CI); per the draft it is the fresh per-connection sid that forces one CDH per guess, with AD contributing via the transcript; the fork MUST document that long-term confidentiality rests on password entropy — a recorded transcript is offline-recoverable by a future quantum attacker against a weak password. A PQ-hybrid is out of scope for a single-user box unless that threat is.

Estimated change size: the CPace construction core is ~150–270 lines, but the full security-relevant diff — the handshake state machine, the two-key secretbox rewrite, the §10.3 invariants, the §8 excisions, and removing the SignedId/Ed25519 exchange — is ~450–850 lines (§10.1), with a comparably-sized test harness. The code diff is small; the work around it is not — every line in §10.3 is a place a real PAKE deployment has failed, and the verification (§11), independent audit (R-V3), and per-platform build pipeline (§12) dwarf the diff. That asymmetry is the point, not a reassurance.

R-P14MUST On-wire choreography: where the handshake runs and the messages it replaces
The CPace exchange runs inside create_tcp_connection (server.rs) on the unkeyed Stream, inserted unconditionally where the secure-gated SignedId ↔ PublicKey key bootstrap runs — and that bootstrap is wrapped in if secure && … (server.rs) while the direct listener passes secure=false, so on the flagship direct path nothing ran there before: the direct path gains keying it never had, rather than swapping one block for another (on the secure=true rendezvous/relay paths it does replace the old flow — controlled sends SignedId; peer replies PublicKey{symmetric_value,asymmetric_value}; set_key(Encrypt::decode(…)))). That block (server.rs), its client counterpart, the box_ seal/open path (tcp.rs), and the SignedId/PublicKey protobuf messages are removed (R-P5, §8). The new flow is pinned:
  • Message — a dedicated top-level type, not a Message-union variant. Add Cpace as its own top-level protobuf message in libs/hbb_common/protos/message.proto (a oneof over the steps below), framed through the existing bytes_codec but parsed and serialized on its own on the unkeyed stream — parse_from_bytes::<Cpace>, never Message::parse_from_bytes. Routing it through the application Message union (the obvious shortcut) would make the pre-auth decoder reach the entire Message schema — every VideoFrame/file/clipboard field an attacker could populate — whereas the dedicated type keeps the reachable pre-key decoder surface to the handshake's ~4 fixed fields (R-S7). Both roles change: the responder parses only Cpace pre-key, and the initiator (when a box views) sends/parses only Cpace pre-key — which also protects a connecting viewer from a malicious server's malformed pre-key frames. Both sides switch to the normal Message framing only after keying. No other message type is accepted, or even decoded, on the unkeyed stream.
  • Fixed roles & sequence (viewer = initiator, controlled = responder). The generator g needs the full sid = sid_a‖sid_b and CI, so the sid contributions precede the group elements: ① initiator → responder { sid_a(16B), ADa=b"viewer" }; ② responder → initiator { sid_b(16B), ADb=b"server", Yb(32B) }; ③ initiator → responder { Ya(32B), Ta }; ④ responder → initiator { Tb }. Ta/Tb are the R-P3 HMAC-SHA-512 tags (64 B). Two round-trips.
  • Gate. Each side verifies the peer's tag in constant time, derives the two directional keys (R-P2), and calls set_key only on success; no set_key, no Connection::start, no application message, no authorized before both tags verify (R-A1/R-A2). Any decode/length/timeout/tag failure aborts fail-closed.
  • Framing. set_max_packet_length is capped small before the first byte (R-S7); each CPace message is ≤ ~120 B. With KCP dropped (R-S7/R-D4), this dedicated, fixed-shape Cpace message (parsed on its own, never via the Message union — R-P14) is the only attacker-reachable parser before keying.
This choreography is in scope for the §11 adversarial review (R-V1).
R-P14aMUST Strict step-ordering and no-duplicate handshake state machine
§10.2 gives the message sequence; this pins the state machine that enforces it. The handshake MUST be driven by an explicit four-state machine per role (responder: WAIT_1 → INIT2 → WAIT_3 → SEND_4 → KEYED; initiator: INIT → WAIT_2 → SEND_3 → WAIT_4 → KEYED). In each receiving state the implementation MUST accept exactly one Cpace oneof variant and MUST abort fail-closed on any other — a future-step variant, an own-side variant, or a repeat of an already-consumed step (no peer may send two step-① frames). A step arriving before its prerequisites (e.g. step-③ before step-① at the responder, which then holds no sid_b/g/yb) MUST be a wrong-variant abort, never buffered; no state may silently ignore an unexpected frame and keep waiting (that re-opens the half-open / duplicate surface). Each step is consumed exactly once, and set_key is reachable only from the tag-verify success edge (R-P14, R-A1).
R-P14bMUST Bounded reads — the timeout is the half-open resource bound
set_max_packet_length(≤ 4 KiB) MUST be set before the first read (R-S7), and every inbound handshake step MUST be read under a finite timeout (per-step, or one whole-handshake deadline). Verified: the framing codec returns Ok(None) on a partial/dribbled frame (bytes_codec.rs) — it never completes and never errors — so the timeout is the sole bound on a peer that opens a connection and stalls (the inherited code wrapped only its single next(): server.rs / client.rs, CONNECT_TIMEOUT/READ_TIMEOUT = 18 s; four steps need four bounded waits, or one deadline). A timeout, a peer EOF (Stream::next() → None, tcp.rs), an oversize frame (bytes_codec.rs "Too big packet"), or a parse_from_bytes::<Cpace> decode error MUST abort fail-closed. The timeout (stall bound), the ≤ 4 KiB cap (size bound, R-S7), and the per-source limiter (CPU-flood shedding, R-S10) together bound half-open exhaustion to ≤ 4 KiB + one task per connection until the deadline; no per-connection state beyond the in-flight handshake is created.
R-P14cMUST Only key-confirmation failure feeds the limiter; replay needs no state; nothing on the wire to downgrade
Of all pre-key aborts, only an R-P3 key-confirmation tag mismatch MUST increment the per-source limiter (R-S10) — it is the sole event that is an online password guess; decode / length / out-of-order / duplicate / decompress / identity / AD-mismatch / timeout / EOF aborts MUST NOT increment it, or a malformed-frame flood would trip the owner's own block (R-S10's cardinal principle; the limiter is checked before the scalar-mult). Whole-handshake replay is harmless and MUST NOT be defended by sid-tracking state: each side contributes a fresh 16-byte sid nonce and a fresh ephemeral scalar per connection (R-P8), so a replayed sid_a/Ya cannot yield a tag valid under the responder's fresh sid_b/Yb (the mac_key/ISK diverge) and aborts at R-P3. sid freshness is enforced unilaterally and locally by CSPRNG sampling; a "seen-sid" cache is forbidden — unnecessary, and itself a memory-DoS / stateful-config surface (R-S12). The Cpace oneof carries no version / suite / capability field — those live only in the never-sent CI (R-P1, R-P11) — so there is no on-wire field to downgrade, and an unknown/unset oneof case is a decode abort, not a fallback.

10.4 Concrete construction — the byte-level pins (implementable without a cryptographer)

§10.1–§10.3 match draft-21 and reproduce the published ristretto255 vectors, but a faithful, interoperable implementation needs the literal bytes fixed here. CI MUST gate on the draft's published ristretto255 vector — the G_Coffee25519 group in testvectors.json (the JSON's codename for ristretto255), which pins every intermediate (derived generator g = 222b6b19…, symmetric-mode ISK_SY = 544199d7…) and agrees with the draft body's worked example — pinned to the draft-irtf-cfrg-cpace-21 git tag or vendored into the repo so a later CFRG revision (or the eventual RFC) cannot silently shift the expected bytes out from under the KAT. Do not substitute the testvectors.md ristretto255 entry: it is the same vector under a different example CI and so yields different bytes (a6fc82c3…/980dcc5a…). Add the fork's own CI/anchor KAT (the pinned-bytes box below) and the R-P3 self-consistency/negative KAT (R-A10).

ConstantValue
Suite / ISK / MAC DSIb"CPaceRistretto255" · b"CPaceRistretto255_ISK" · b"CPaceMac"
Hash · block · gen-hash lenSHA-512 · s_in_bytes = 128 · generator hash = 64 bytes (not 32)
Groupristretto255 (prime order ℓ = 2252 + 27742317777372353535851937790883648493); 32-byte element encoding
Length prefixlv_cat uses LEB128, and it prefixes the zero-pad field too
Session keysHKDF-SHA-512, IKM = ISK (64 B), salt = the fixed non-secret b"rustdesk-fork/CPACE-RISTR255-SHA512/v1/hkdf", info = b"rustdesk-fork/CPace/secretbox/c2s" & …/s2c, 32 B each
CI (shared, identical both sides, never sent)lv_cat(b"rustdesk-fork/CPACE-RISTR255-SHA512/v1", port) — fork-tag · suite · version · port only. No role here: the draft requires both parties to hold the same CI (draft §"Protocol inputs"), so a per-side role cannot live in it. Pin the port byte-encoding to one form — fixed 2-byte big-endian (network order) (it must be byte-identical on both peers, so a single pinned encoding is required, not a choice) and feed the pinned compile-time direct-access port (21118, R-F4) — not the live socket; add a fork KAT (no draft vector covers CI). For the §17 direct port 21118 the pinned value is CI = 26727573746465736b2d666f726b2f43504143452d52495354523235352d5348413531322f763102527e — the 38-byte tag, then 02 527e (LEB128(2)‖be16(21118)).
ADa / ADb (per-side, sent in clear, authenticated)the fixed-role tags — ADa = b"viewer" (initiator), ADb = b"server" (responder = controlled side). Per-side and folded into both the transcript and the R-P3 tag, so each confirmation tag is sender-bound and a loopback/reflection fails (R-P5). The published KAT vectors use ADa=b"ADa"/ADb=b"ADb"; the fork's runtime values are "viewer"/"server".
# PRS = NFC(password), no case-fold (NFC-only subset of RFC 8265 OpaqueString, R-P1) — fed directly
gen_str = generator_string(DSI, PRS, CI, sid, 128)        # PRS & DSI lv-prefixed inside; zpad fills the block
g       = RistrettoPoint::from_uniform_bytes(SHA512(gen_str))   # 64-byte hash → ristretto map (dalek)
ya      = Scalar::from_bytes_mod_order_wide(&csprng_64())  # == Scalar::random; NEVER Scalar::from_bits
Ya      = (ya * g).compress()                             # 32 bytes, on the wire
P       = CompressedRistretto(Yb).decompress()?           # abort on decode failure …
K       = ya * P;  if K.is_identity() { abort }           # … AND on identity (all-zeros DECODES ok!)
ISK     = SHA512( lv_cat(DSI‖b"_ISK", sid, K) ‖ transcript_oc(Ya,ADa,Yb,ADb) )
                                                          # transcript appended RAW (not re-lv-wrapped) → ISK_SY; fork KAT below = 0421d692…
k_c2s   = HKDF-SHA512(salt, ISK, ".../c2s", 32)           # per-direction secretbox keys (R-P2)
k_s2c   = HKDF-SHA512(salt, ISK, ".../s2c", 32)
mac_key = SHA512( b"CPaceMac" ‖ sid ‖ ISK )               # direct hash, bare concat — NOT lv_cat, NOT HKDF
Ta      = HMAC-SHA512(mac_key, lv_cat(Ya, ADa))           # verify peer's tag in CONSTANT TIME
Three traps a vector-blind implementer hits

(1) Scalar sampling: use Scalar::from_bytes_mod_order_wide (what Scalar::random calls) — a 64-byte wide reduction with no bit-masking. MUST NOT use the deprecated, feature-gated Scalar::from_bits: it masks only the top bit (&0x7F, leaving the scalar unreduced) and is Edwards-only — it will not work with RistrettoPoint::mul. (The draft's alternative clamping sampler clears the top 4 bits with &0x0F; the wide-reduction path needs no clamp — so &0x0F is not the code path here.) (2) ISK layout: the transcript is appended raw after lv_cat(DSI_ISK, sid, K); re-wrapping it as a fourth lv_cat element silently yields a different ISK (on the published-vector inputs, 49ddbd… instead of the correct 544199d7…) and fails the vector. (3) Identity: the all-zeros encoding decodes successfully to the identity, so a decode-only check is insufficient — you MUST test K.is_identity() after the multiply (R-P7).

The fork KAT — anchor A: 16-byte sid (draft-reproducible), port 21118

The §11 review and CI gate against these. KAT-fixed inputs (chosen so the anchor is reproducible from the published draft vector): PRS = OpaqueString("Password"), sid = 7e4b4791d6a8ef019b936c79fb7f2c57 — the draft vector's 16-byte sid, reused so this anchor reproduces from the published vector; a live session always uses a 32-byte sid = sid_a‖sid_b (R-P1), pinned as anchor B below and gated by R-A10 — ya/yb = the draft-21 published scalars, ADa = b"viewer", ADb = b"server", port 21118. In production PRS is the permanent password and sid/scalars are fresh per connection — these bytes pin the construction, not a live session. The port is a pinned compile-time constant (21118, R-F4), so CI — and therefore g and everything below — is fixed: these are the values, unconditionally. (Rebuilding with a different port constant is a build-time change that regenerates them; there is no runtime port mode.)

The fork KAT — anchor B: 32-byte production sid (R-A10)

The production handshake carries a 32-byte sid = sid_a‖sid_b (R-P1/R-P14), which anchor A's 16-byte draft sid never exercises; this anchor pins the 32-byte path and CI MUST gate on it too. KAT-fixed inputs: PRS = OpaqueString("Password"), sid = a1·16 ‖ b2·16 (example halves sid_a/sid_b), ya/yb = the same draft-21 scalars, ADa = b"viewer", ADb = b"server", port 21118. Independently re-derived byte-for-byte from the draft-21 construction — the generator and ISK length-prefix sid, and mac_key = H("CPaceMac"‖sid‖ISK) stays unambiguous under the fixed 64-byte ISK trailer at either sid length (§10.3, R-A10).
