11. Cryptographic verification requirements

Honest caveat — stated every time

The in-tree CPace implementation (§10.1) has had an AI-conducted single-model review (2026-07-02, findings resolved — docs/CRYPTO-AUDIT-2026-07-02.md): the construction was reproduced byte-for-byte against the published CFRG vector by a separately implemented stack, and the parts no vector covers — the R-P14a state machine, the two-key secretbox rewrite, the constant-time/identity paths, and the R-P3 confirmation-MAC composition (whose self-consistency KAT cannot catch a self-consistently-wrong composition) — were analysed for protocol/nonce-misuse. That review was not organizationally independent and is not a professional external cryptographic audit; it does not satisfy R-V3 or equal the decades-deep, multi-party third-party scrutiny SSH's channel crypto has. Maturity is therefore the single axis on which SSH still leads on the wire — the off-wire content-parsing residual of the viewer role is the other (§1 / Appendix C #2b), present whenever a box views and absent when it acts purely as --server.

R-V1MUST Adversarial multi-lens review
The handshake design + implementation undergo structured review attacking from independent angles (first-contact MITM, replay, downgrade, timing, nonce/transcript reuse; the single-authentication shape — one password-keyed channel with no second verification to bind or relay around, which forecloses the compound-/tunneled-authentication class (PEAP-MSCHAPv2 / Triple-Handshake) by construction; the five-type coverage, the set_raw path, curve25519-dalek usage). Findings resolved or risk-accepted in writing.
R-V2MUST Spec conformance & implementation audit
Checked against the chosen CPace draft and its published test vectors (group, encoding, generator derivation, ISK derivation, KDF labels — the published vectors stop at ISK; no vector covers the R-P3 key-confirmation MAC, so that step is checked by a self-consistency + negative KAT instead, R-A10). The in-tree implementation and its curve25519-dalek dependency are read for correct usage and rev-pinned; the implementation's un-audited status is disclosed in the release notes.
R-V3MUST Assurance basis: full byte-level spec + published vectors + adversarial tests
The PAKE is a standardized construction (draft-21) specified here to the byte (§10.4), gated on the draft's published vectors and the R-P3 self-consistency/negative KAT (R-A10), and exercised by the active-attacker / wire-capture tests (R-A8/A9). That — not an external sign-off — is the day-to-day CI assurance basis: a competent engineer can implement and verify the vector-covered construction core (encoding, generator derivation, ISK) against the cited vectors and code without a cryptographer. But that does not extend to the whole of §10: the R-P14a state machine, the two-key secretbox rewrite (R-P2/R-P10), the constant-time/identity guarantees, and the R-P3 confirmation-MAC composition (no published vector) require expert review regardless of their small line count. Because this code guards an internet-exposed, root-capable service and replaces the two most serious inherited findings (Appendix C #1–#2), an independent expert audit of the §10.4 construction, the state machine, the constant-time paths, and the KDF/secretbox parameters MUST be obtained before exposed operation and published — necessary, not merely tractable-because-small. The vectors + KATs + adversarial tests above remain the day-to-day CI assurance basis (and what makes the audit checkable); until the audit is obtained, the §11 "not independently audited" disclosure stands and the box MUST be treated as pre-audit. Status: an AI-conducted single-model review was published on 2026-07-02 (docs/CRYPTO-AUDIT-2026-07-02.md, findings resolved), but it is not organizationally independent and does not satisfy this requirement. The independent external expert audit remains outstanding and exposed production operation remains pre-audit. What remains forbidden is the inverse: shipping a "clean bill of health" the tests do not actually back — every security claim here is reproducible against the vectors and the file+symbol citations.

12. Build, release & version pinning

R-B1MUST Build & publish the full client matrix
From the one forked codebase, the build MUST produce one package per platform — Android aarch64 (.apk), Windows x86_64 (.exe + .msi), and Debian x86_64 (.deb) — each a single binary usable as viewer and, via --server, controlled side (R-R2b); the internet-exposed box (§17) runs that same binary as a headless --service. It MUST run to completion off GitHub-hosted runners (locally or a self-hosted runner; R-B2); macOS/iOS are dropped (R-R2).
R-B2MUST No code-signing; the build cannot live on GitHub-hosted CI
Upstream's release model signs artifacts with secrets injected into GitHub Actions — unusable here on two counts. (1) GitHub-hosted runners are too unreliable to be the build path: the heavy RustDesk matrix (vcpkg built from source, multi-GB toolchains, long Windows/Android jobs) fails on the order of one run in five, so you cannot depend on getting a green build out of it — flutter-build.yml MUST be runnable to completion off GitHub-hosted runners (locally or a self-hosted runner; R-B1). (2) Artifacts ship unsigned: code-signing is a CA/registry dependency (the model R-P5 already rejects), it adds nothing once the binary's hash is verified directly, and with the build off GitHub there are no CI secrets to hold a signing key anyway. Exception — Android: the OS refuses to install an APK with no signature, so it uses a self-generated local key (apksigner/keytool — no CA, no Play Store), a forced local keypair, not CA code-signing — Android refuses to install an unsigned APK (the v2 scheme is mandatory since Android 11), so this signature is not optional. Pin it: one stable RSA-4096 keystore (SHA256withRSA; validity ≥ 10000 days; fixed alias), generated once and reused for every release — Android ties app identity to the signing key, so a stable key gives clean in-place upgrades whereas a changed/absent key forces uninstall+reinstall (wiping app data). The keystore is a secret: kept out of the repo and the build image, fed to build-android.sh only at sign time (R-B11) — mounted read-only, never baked into an image layer, with its password supplied via file/agent, not env or argv (both leak via /proc and container inspect). Because one stable key signs every release for clean in-place upgrades, its theft lets an attacker ship malicious upgrades and rotation forces a data-wiping reinstall, so the fork MUST follow the fail-closed key-loss / compromise procedure in docs/ANDROID-SIGNING-RECOVERY.md and hold independently recoverable encrypted copies offline (ideally HSM-backed). Loss without suspected compromise may restore only a verified copy matching the committed certificate pin; suspected compromise retires the old package identity and requires a new package name, new key and pin, authenticated operator notice, a clean build, and a data-wiping uninstall/reinstall. No pin bypass or ad hoc lineage is a recovery path. The signature is Android's install gate, not the trust anchor — the pinned SHA-256 still is. Integrity from build to install is a pinned SHA-256 per artifact, with the complete manifest digest, full source commit, and fork version authenticated over the operator's existing trusted out-of-band channel before strict local verification under docs/RELEASE-VERIFICATION.md — a checksum downloaded beside an artifact is not a second channel — over the reproducible build from the committed lockfiles (R-R1). Tamper-evidence without a signature. Determinism and source authority are prerequisites for the recorded-SHA bar: upstream baked a wall-clock, local-timezone BUILD_DATE into every binary and generated ./src/version.rs inside the source tree, so two builds of identical source could produce different bytes while a privileged verifier could mutate the snapshot later consumed by a non-root target build. The fork MUST derive VERSION from Cargo's CARGO_PKG_VERSION, derive release BUILD_DATE from the explicit canonical SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH, reject every malformed or out-of-range explicit epoch, and emit the generated Rust only beneath Cargo's private OUT_DIR. The required fork identity MUST be one canonical newline-terminated FORK_VERSION whose numeric base equals CARGO_PKG_VERSION, and the build and --fork-version runtime surface MUST have no missing, malformed, mismatched, or package-version fallback. Verification phases that perform only Cargo compilation MUST mount source read-only, src/version.rs MUST remain absent and unignored, and target checks MUST NOT gain root merely to tolerate generated source ownership. For each independent build pass, the release orchestrator MUST give every target builder an absent, non-symlink output leaf; the target builder alone owns its creation. Windows MUST publish its complete multi-artifact leaf atomically with no clobber. Mutable target lifecycle state MUST be pass-private and disjoint from that output leaf; in particular, Windows VM harness state cannot equal, contain, or be nested beneath OUT_DIR. CI MUST assert reproducibility by a double build: build the same exact committed source twice from independent generated state and require byte-identical SHA-256. Without that assertion the recorded-SHA bar is unfalsifiable.
R-B3MUST Publish AGPLv3 source per binary
Each release links the exact source revision (both repos + submodule commit) it was built from.
R-B4MUST Smoke-test every platform; assume nothing builds until watched
Per platform, connect + authenticate + drive each session type must pass before tagging. (Upstream's fork pipeline emits nothing until upload-artifact is wired and the build is watched to green — real work, the more so on the flaky runners R-B2 keeps the build off of.) Plus a socket-surface smoke-test on the running --service: ss -lntup + strace -f -e trace=bind,listen,connect MUST show exactly one v4 TCP listener on the direct port, zero UDP listeners, and no unsolicited outbound connect — the build-time empirical confirmation that the binary binds what Appendix B claims (R-D3/R-D5/R-D6), with R-A4 the permanent runtime backstop. Readiness is a proved state transition, never elapsed-time inference. The Linux runtime harness MUST capture each child PID's /proc start identity once immediately after spawn and pass that same retained pair through every readiness, event, duration, and shutdown operation; recapturing authority in a later helper is forbidden. Every operation uses one fixed, non-ambient monotonic deadline, descriptor-pins its log and typed probe, fails if the retained process exits, becomes a zombie, or is replaced, and waits only until the complete required state is proved. Listening readiness requires the exact child to own the sole loopback TCP listener, zero UDP sockets, the runtime socket-surface record, and both UID-scoped Unix listener kernel inodes. The mode-0700 non-symlink parent and mode-0600 non-symlink ipc/ipc_password pathnames are metadata preconditions, not authority: each exact pathname must resolve to one listening stream entry in procfs and that kernel inode must occur in the retained child's descriptor table before and after the typed transaction. Typed readiness uses a dedicated read-only response containing individual password/listener/owner booleans, one absolute Tokio deadline across connect/send/receive, SO_PEERCRED PID equality with the retained child, and start-identity checks before request and after response; an outer process timeout bounds synchronous failure, and any missing or mismatched fact fails closed. Parked readiness proves no permanent password, an unbound direct listener, both exact-process IPC listeners, and the parked transition record. Signals use a pidfd opened and identity-checked before delivery, with no raw-PID signal fallback. Event, tunnel-target, packet-capture, and graceful-shutdown waits use their retained process or exact record, and packet capture is interrupted and reaped before inspection. The real 60-second limiter-decay observation is the only fixed semantic interval and MUST continuously revalidate the retained child; startup, event, capture, and teardown delays are forbidden. Runtime stages mount the source snapshot read-only; only the compilation stage receives a writable bind, preserves the complete unfiltered build transcript, and records the real container exit status. All isolated stages run with strict shell status, retain their complete transcript and status independently, use non-pipeline output assertions, and may not filter a probe while it executes. Self-test cleanup records device/inode authority before removing only exact known entries and preserves anything changed or late. A password CLI is invoked exactly once after typed readiness; its watchdog is derived from and exceeds the protocol's exported 600-second admitted-operation recovery bound and has a finite forced-kill ceiling, so the harness cannot create a contradictory unknown outcome.
R-B6MUST Drop the Sciter UI
The repo carries two UI stacks (Flutter + legacy Sciter). Sciter is why Rust is pinned to 1.75 (it breaks on the 1.78 i128-layout change — flutter-build.yml), why a 32-bit i686-pc-windows-msvc job exists, and the source of a large HTML/JS (res/inline-sciter.py, src/ui/*.tis) surface. Building Flutter-only drops the sciter-rs fork, the i686 job, and the .tis tree — and unblocks a modern Rust toolchain, removing a whole class of build/maintenance and attack surface. Decision: the fork nonetheless keeps Rust 1.75 (edition 2021) for now — the deps are pinned, so a bump buys no security and would force re-validating the entire lock; moving to a newer stable is optional later work, gated on a green --locked build + audit (R-R3).

(Version pinning: R-B5a/R-B5b in §3.1 — build from the committed Cargo.lock with the pinned toolchain; track/mirror the 21 rustdesk-org source URLs represented by 27 lockfile records, with the exact in-tree PTY provenance bound by R-S11t.)

R-B7MUST Reproduce upstream 1.4.7's official build first; the fork is an auditable delta on it
Before any fork change, reproduce RustDesk's official 1.4.7 build for each target — the upstream build.py / flutter-build.yml steps, run with the §3.1 toolchain (Rust 1.75, Flutter 3.24.5, NDK r28c, vcpkg 120deac3, LLVM 15) and the committed lockfiles (R-R1) — so the artifacts differ from upstream's by exactly two things and no others: no code-signing, and the build runs off GitHub-hosted runners (R-B2). One faithful-reproduction subtlety the delta accounting MUST include: upstream's generate-bridge job runs flutter_rust_bridge_codegen — pinned separately at FRB 1.80.1 (bridge.yml) — to emit the uncommitted generated_bridge.dart/bridge_generated.rs that every build job needs; the fork MUST reproduce it as a pinned, ./online-fetched step (§12.1, R-B12), or the "exactly two deltas" claim is false. The build process is inherited, not reinvented — identical to how RustDesk builds 0c86d46 (submodule df6badc) officially, minus those two. Establishing this baseline parity first makes every later difference a known consequence of a §7–§10 requirement, never a build accident, and is the entry point the §15 acceptance is measured from.
R-B8MUST Build hosts: all three targets on the one Linux x86_64 box — Debian & Android in Docker, Windows in a KVM VM
Windows cannot be cross-built from Linux (MSVC + WiX are Windows-only, §12.2), so it needs a genuine Windows environment — but that environment can be a virtual machine on the same box. So the Debian x86_64 .deb and Android aarch64 .apk build in Docker on the Linux x86_64 host (Android cross-compiles from Linux, as upstream's own CI does on ubuntu-24.04), and the Windows x86_64 .exe/.msi builds in an ephemeral KVM Windows 11 guest on that same bare-metal host (§12.2; a dedicated bare-metal Windows 11 machine is an equivalent option). All three thus run on one Linux x86_64 host with no GitHub-hosted runner in the path (R-B2), each as a disposable instance of a pinned, immutable template (Docker image or golden VM image) provisioned to the pinned toolchain (R-B5a) and nothing more, and torn down when the build completes (§12.1).
R-B9MUST One build script per target — environment-validated, fail-loud, no fallbacks, one mode
The build is driven by checked-in scripts, one per target (build-debian.sh, build-android.sh, build-windows.ps1), held to the same engineering bar as the rest of this spec — the "one option, the good option" discipline applied to the build:
  • Validate the environment, then abort. Assert the exact pinned versions — not mere presence — of Rust / Flutter / NDK / the vcpkg baseline / LLVM / JDK, plus the OS + arch, the required system libraries, and the submodule + lockfile state, before compiling a byte.
  • Fail loud; no fallbacks. set -euo pipefail (PowerShell $ErrorActionPreference = 'Stop'); every check dies with a precise message; no "install latest if missing", "retry another way", or silent default — a single mode of operation, the correct one.
  • Pin from one manifest. Every version comes from a single committed pins file; no command resolves "latest" or a moving channel (R-B5a, R-R1).
  • Compile every shipped Rust target before artifact construction. Release source verification MUST generate the real Flutter Rust bridge and run the pinned, network-isolated aarch64-linux-android library check with the exact APK toolchain, lockfile, release profile, and flutter feature. Host-only analysis or Gradle cache tests are not Android cfg proof. The APK build still performs the exact cargo-ndk release build independently.
  • No second build scaffold. The repository carries no root-level Dockerfile/entrypoint.sh builder and no translated upstream README build path. Debian/Android Docker builds are the digest-pinned scripts/Dockerfile.* images created by scripts/online-fetch.sh; Windows builds are the pinned VM flow. A live-fetch root Dockerfile with rustup, raw Sciter downloads, unchecked CMake downloads, or ad-hoc vcpkg bootstrap is not a supported fallback.
  • Generated-state ownership is one retained-authority operation. A root-running verifier may create ignored Cargo or Flutter state only inside one of the two private exact-commit repositories. Before every writable consumer and again afterward, the orchestrator MUST reject descendant mounts, require Linux fs.protected_hardlinks=1, and invoke the digest-pinned Debian image with no network, no pull, a read-only container root, no-new-privileges, recursive bind inclusion disabled, and exactly DAC_READ_SEARCH plus CHOWN. The committed helper is opened once when the workspace is created; every host or container closure, normalization, preflight, and deletion invocation reads only that descriptor and re-authenticates the complete bounded bytes in memory before execution. The finite whole-tree authority admits at most 524,288 descendants and directory depth 128. Its 256-descriptor reserve explicitly covers one root authority, that maximum active depth, at most 64 pre-existing descriptors, at most eight transient helper descriptors, and contingency; the helper rejects a deeper tree or excess inherited descriptor state before mutation. The helper MUST set and re-prove an exact soft RLIMIT_NOFILE of 524,544 before enumerating inherited descriptors, reject a lower hard limit, and the container MUST have exact soft and hard limits of 524,544. Release preflight proves both host and pinned-container capacity before the online snapshot or any build begins. One helper first retains every directory and unique non-directory inode descriptor, exact directory inventory, edge, mount identity, file type, owner, group, mode, link count, and complete internal hardlink count; it re-proves that complete recorded metadata before mutation and rejects special objects, mount crossings, external hardlinks, changed inventories, or changed authorities. Through only those retained descriptors it transfers ownership, strips setuid, setgid, sticky, and group/world-write bits, restores owner access, returns the root to the invoking UID/GID at mode 0700, and re-proves every retained authority and postcondition. The host then runs git clean -ffdx, requires the equivalent git clean -nffdx dry run to be empty, and re-proves detached HEAD, index, and tracked bytes. Pathname walks after the helper are not ownership authority.
  • Release snapshots are independent private repositories. Pass A and pass B each use git clone --no-hardlinks --no-checkout --reject-shallow, detach at the exact pinned commit, remove every remote, and retain a private .git object database. Replacement refs, grafts, object alternates, shallow declarations or state, sparse state, index masking, object corruption detectable by strict full git fsck, descendant mounts, and inode links escaping the repository are rejected before a snapshot is admitted. The release build creates no Git worktree registration and cleanup never parses, prunes, adopts, or removes the invoking repository's worktree registry.
  • Private-tree cleanup is one terminal, descriptor-authorized deletion. The workspace and admitted snapshot identities are recorded before consumers run. Production cleanup opens the exact committed helper, proves pathname/descriptor identity and digest, and retains that descriptor before deletion. Every release invocation reads at most 1 MiB directly from that descriptor, hashes the complete bytes in memory against the committed digest, rejects an oversized or mismatched stream, and only then compiles and dispatches it; privileged execution resolves no mutable helper pathname. A no-pull, no-network, read-only-root container with only DAC_OVERRIDE and FOWNER receives the workspace through a nonrecursive bind. Before the online snapshot or any build, an exact disposable removal exercises that same image, bind, descriptor, limit, capability set, root-owned mode-0000 file and directory, current-user sticky directory entry, content deletion, empty-root transition, and absence proof. Before deleting a production edge, the helper re-proves the exact mode-0700 workspace root, filesystem and mount boundary, finite entry/depth/descriptor budget, special-object exclusion, and complete non-directory hardlink closure. It then removes only descriptor-relative authenticated edges, requires every retained non-directory link count to reach zero, and proves the original root remains empty with unchanged identity, owner, group, mode, and mount authority. It does not chown, chmod, or otherwise normalize the online snapshot or workspace as deletion preparation. The host re-authenticates the still-open helper bytes, re-acquires the exact empty root and protected parent, refuses any late content instead of traversing it, removes only that root through its retained parent descriptor, requires the original root link count to reach zero, synchronizes the parent, proves pathname absence, and closes the helper authority. If production cleanup lacks the exact pinned image or any other authority, it preserves the workspace and fails; the recursive host remover is fixture-only. Missing probes, capacity or capability failures, acquisition failures, replaced edges, external links, mounts, nonempty state, or failed descriptor close preserve uncertainty and abort with complete cleanup diagnostics. The invoking UID remains the cooperating release authority and MUST keep every admitted private tree and fixture quiescent; deliberately concurrent same-UID namespace mutation is outside the transaction and MUST NOT be described as contained.
  • Stateful verifier commands own one authenticated transient cgroup scope. Before spawn, the verifier installs temporary SIGHUP, SIGINT, and SIGTERM handlers and gates target execution behind a trusted helper. A 256-bit random scope name and independent description are authenticated through the current user's protected systemd bus, exact transient-unit properties, invocation ID, control-group path, gated helper membership, and descriptor-walked cgroup-v2 cgroup.events/cgroup.kill authority. The synchronous scope launcher is never classified absent by a quiet-time heuristic: pre-authentication failure waits for either completed launcher failure or the exact nonce-described transient unit, and any unresolved live admission aborts. Only after scope authentication does the parent send the bounded exact target environment and descriptor-number allowlist over the gate's sole Unix SOCK_SEQPACKET channel and transfer exactly those descriptors with SCM_RIGHTS; the gate rejects truncated, extra, malformed, duplicate, or out-of-range authority, collision-isolates received descriptors, restores only the requested inheritable numbers, closes the channel, and executes the target. Target exit is observed by pidfd. Timeout sends SIGTERM to the exact unit, then writes 1 to the retained cgroup.kill descriptor if needed; normal exit with populated descendants is failure. Completion requires recursive populated 0, target and launcher reap, exact unit collection, and cgroup-path absence before reserved release state is compared and prior signal state is restored. This proves emptiness of the authenticated scope, including ordinary setsid and double-fork descendants. It does not claim containment of hostile same-UID code that migrates to a writable sibling cgroup, creates another user unit, or delegates work to Docker or another daemon; repository verifier fixtures are cooperative commands and MUST NOT use those escape channels.
  • Cleanup never converts uncertainty into success. Every verifier and release-workspace exit or signal trap is installed before allocation, blocks repeated managed signals during cleanup, preserves the original failure and every cleanup failure, and defers success until cgroup collection, publication reconciliation, descriptor-bound workspace deletion, and final source-state proof succeed. The managed-unit finalizer MUST propagate forced-kill, launcher-reap, descriptor-close, unit-collection, and cgroup-path failures as one complete failure set. A mount, external hardlink, changed identity or inventory, uncertain cleanup boundary, missing cleanup probe, cgroup/unit ambiguity, publication ambiguity, or Windows ownership uncertainty preserves unresolved state and aborts; ambient sudo, ignored cleanup errors, cross-snapshot normalization, or generated-state reuse is forbidden.
  • Final-set installation is same-parent, failure-atomic, and restartable. The canonical dist is never privileged-normalized, repaired, or made writable by verification. One non-file-producing lock on the exact repository common-directory inode excludes cooperating release orchestrators. The publication parent MUST be an exact canonical directory owned by the invoking UID and primary GID, grant owner read/write/traverse, deny group/world write, and have no extended POSIX ACL. Publication is supported only on Linux ext4 when descriptor-bound fstatfs magic and the exact runtime mount's /proc/self/mountinfo filesystem type agree, descriptor-bound FS_IOC_GETFSUUID returns one nonzero 16-byte external filesystem UUID, and directory fsync, O_TMPFILE, renameat2, and persistent opaque object handles are available. The complete ext4 UUID and every persistent object handle bind restart authority; folded f_fsid and runtime mount IDs bind only the live process and MUST NOT be persisted as restart identity. Every untrusted file edge is first opened with O_PATH|O_NOFOLLOW, proved regular, then reopened through its retained /proc/self/fd/N authority with O_NONBLOCK and re-proved, so a FIFO or special-file substitution cannot block before type rejection. The exact five source files are copied into unnamed files, synchronized, linked through exact retained descriptors, fixed at current-UID/current-GID mode 0444 with one link, and assembled beneath one same-parent mode-0700 payload. Exact content, inventory, security metadata, source commit, fork version, epoch, and manifest digest are proved before the payload root is explicitly finalized and synchronized at mode 0555.
  • The publication journal authorizes one exact recovery action. An unnamed synchronized mode-0400 journal is linked into the same parent in initializing state before payload creation. After a same-parent empty mode-0700 payload is created and parent-synchronized, a durable staging record binds its persistent handle before any source byte is copied; only a fully synchronized, finalized, manifest-proved payload advances to prepared, then to durable rollback or cleanup authorization. The canonical v3 schema binds the destination, token, complete ext4 UUID, parent handle, payload name and handle, optional prior handle, source metadata, and manifest digest. Recovery first requires every canonical record, next-record, and payload name to carry the active transaction token. It deletes an initializing record only when no payload exists; an observed payload has no recorded identity and is preserved as ambiguous. A staging record safely rolls back only its bound partial payload. A prepared first installation uses RENAME_NOREPLACE; replacement uses one same-parent RENAME_EXCHANGE. The parent is synchronized before post-exchange classification can authorize cleanup. Recovery compares current persistent handles and the full ext4 UUID to the record, re-proves the complete release set, durably records rollback or cleanup before partial deletion is legal, and removes the journal only after the authorized payload is absent and the surviving destination has been re-proved. Public verification is non-repairing and rejects any reserved .dist-release-* state both before and after exact destination proof. A recovery scan with no journal synchronizes and rescans that complete namespace. Unknown names, wrong-token canonical names, malformed or multiple records, unsupported filesystems, missing prior state, substituted objects, changed security metadata, or any unclassifiable exchange outcome are preserved and rejected. Logical process-restart fixtures cover first and replacement publication at staging, prepared, durable rollback, exchange, durable cleanup, and payload removal; separate fixtures cover the unbound post-creation payload, wrong-token canonical payload and next-record names, no-clobber race, partial deletion, malformed state, and special-file substitution. They do not simulate physical power loss, whose guarantee depends on the required kernel/filesystem persistence semantics. Processes under the invoking UID must cooperate with the publication lock and namespace; the invoking UID, root, kernel, storage device, and ext4 implementation are trusted publication authorities.
  • Self-verifying + idempotent. Re-running is safe; on success the script verifies the artifact (it exists, is the right type, and matches its recorded SHA-256, R-B2) and prints that — it never reports success it has not proven (the full per-script behavioral contract is §12.3).
  • Fixture Git authority is isolated from the outer release pass. Transaction and reset fixtures initialize independent private repositories or independent no-hardlink clones and can mutate only their descriptor-owned scratch tree. Exact-checkout validation is branch-neutral and takes an independently supplied commit for detached fixture/release repositories; only the real release wrapper requires attached master. The production dirty-source proof creates its own mode-0700 no-hardlink, complete-history clone, attaches master at the exact expected commit, proves that baseline clean, introduces one exclusive untracked probe, and invokes that clone's committed release wrapper. Its recorded root is mount-closed, inode-link-closed, descriptor-removed, and proved absent. No fixture inspects or mutates the outer repository's worktree registry.
  • Verifier fixture state has one descriptor-owned allocation and cleanup boundary. The verifier acquires one canonical current-principal mode-0700 scratch root component-by-component with O_NOFOLLOW, retaining its parent/root descriptors, device/inode identities, mount ID, and exact edge. Every child is randomly named and created, traversed, normalized, and removed descriptor-relative; parent edges, complete inventories, mount identity, filesystem identity, and hardlink closure are re-proved before mutation or deletion. Constructor and child-acquisition failure fixtures prove descriptor-inventory equality and preserve an unowned ambiguous edge until independent re-acquisition. Managed consumers inherit only their exact allowlisted descriptors and normally address fixture state through unresolved /proc/self/fd/* paths. The Debian/Android contract fixture and closure API canonical-tree test remain race-detecting compatibility calls under the same quiescent invoking-UID boundary. A live replacement fixture proves a renamed child still receives writes through its descriptor while the replacement remains untouched. Before/after state includes reserved workspace identities, canonical dist, and every name beginning .dist-release-.
  • Canonical publication snapshots include observable security metadata and a complete resource matrix. A fresh isolated Python worker receives the already-open repository descriptor, performs no-follow descriptor-relative traversal, and returns one bounded result. The parent authenticates the worker by pidfd, enforces a monotonic deadline and aggregate output bound, sends exact pidfd SIGKILL on failure, and never interprets partial output. Independent constants, enforcement predicates, source mutations, and behavioral fixtures bound total tree entries, depth, content bytes, per-inode xattr-name bytes, per-inode xattr count, per-value xattr bytes, aggregate xattr bytes, aggregate xattr count, repository inventory entries, repository inventory name bytes, canonical namespace entries, serialized worker result, aggregate worker output, and elapsed time. Canonical trees reject symlinks and special entries. For every admitted inode the worker records ordinary metadata including nanosecond ctime, statx masks/attributes/mount ID, mount flags, visible xattr names and value digests, explicit POSIX ACL/default-ACL and security.capability probes, FS_IOC_GETFLAGS, and FS_IOC_FSGETXATTR. It compares before/after metadata, two directory inventories, exact file size/EOF/content digest, and the final parent edge. These checks detect races observable to the verifier UID; they are not a coherent filesystem transaction against a privileged writer, inaccessible trusted.* xattrs may be omitted by Linux, and SIGKILL cannot make uninterruptible kernel I/O disappear immediately.
  • Restart proof re-establishes exact file authority without repair. Record-bound recovery requires the release root itself at exact mode 0555 and every artifact plus SHA256SUMS to be a current-UID/current-GID regular file at exact mode 0444, link count one, exact filesystem/mount authority, no xattrs, no unsupported inode flags, stable metadata, exact EOF, and manifest-matching SHA-256. Verification never changes a mode or other metadata. First-install and replacement fixtures cover every durable stop point; partial rollback and cleanup resume only after their journal state is durable. Root-mode, entry-mode, hardlink, xattr, missing-prior, content-equal object substitution, incomplete payload, malformed reserved-name, multiple-record, and oversized-record corruption must preserve the journal and reject recovery until the exact recorded authority is restored.
R-B10MUST One networked step: online-fetch.sh → a git-ignored, SHA-256-verified ./online
The repository is build-oriented and offline-by-construction. A single checked-in scripts/online-fetch.sh is the only script permitted to touch the network; it materializes every resource the repo does not embed — cargo vendor of the committed lockfile (including all 37 git-sourced records), the pub/Gradle caches, vcpkg @120deac3 and its asset cache, the NDK r28c zip, the Flutter SDK, LLVM 15.0.6, the Android SDK (cmdline-tools / platform-34 / build-tools-34.0.0), the pinned base-image digests, and the Windows installers/ISOs — into ./online/, which is git-ignored. Every artifact is checked against its pinned SHA-256 in the single scripts/pins.env manifest (the machine-readable §3.2); any mismatch MUST abort fail-closed. This reconciles R-R1's "pinning ≠ vendoring" with the offline build: the bulky pinned world is cached, not committed — re-creatable from pins.env and re-verifiable, never trusted from the network at build time. The build scripts (R-B8/B9, §12.1–§12.2) then run with the network namespace removed (--network=none) and MUST refuse to run — never silently fetch — if ./online is incomplete or any SHA fails. The isolation MUST be of the compile container itself, not merely "no published ports": its own network namespace removed, no Docker socket bind-mounted in, and the warm caches mounted read-only — with a canary build.rs in CI that attempts an outbound connect and MUST fail the compile stage, so "no fetch at compile time" is proven, not trusted. Run order: cleanup.sh --build-host-network (preflight scrub of any prior harness-created VM/network state) → host-provision.sh (once) → online-fetch.sh (once, or on a pins.env change) → build-* (per build, offline) → cleanup.sh.
R-B10 Android Gradle execution-cache authority

The verified warm Gradle home remains an immutable input and MUST NOT be used as writable execution state or copied with source modes intact. The offline Android container runs as the invoking non-root UID and MUST reject links, special files, multiply linked files, foreign ownership, mode drift, mount crossings, source mutation, a pre-existing destination, and every seed-provided user-home or distribution init.gradle, init.gradle.kts, or init.d script authority. It projects every admitted regular file into one fresh private /tmp/gradle-home with owner-only writable modes, verifies the complete projected inventory, content digests, and source stability, and leaves the warm seed unchanged. A single tracked init script is then installed as the projected home's sole init authority and MUST set and prove Gradle's actual StartParameter.offline state before settings, plugin, or dependency resolution. The project property org.gradle.offline is not an offline-mode control and MUST NOT be treated as one. --network=none remains the independent hard isolation boundary. A pinned-image gate MUST first authenticate the complete canonical online closure, then mount only the exact test authorities and execute the exact pinned Gradle distribution as non-root to prove enabled, absent, and malformed internal-flag behavior; the online-input snapshot mutation suite MUST also be a release gate.

R-B11MUST Minimal host provisioning; no system libvirt network, no host DNS/DHCP listener
scripts/host-provision.sh installs only the host-level runtimes the ephemeral envs need. For Docker that means the container engine with no published ports. For the Windows VM that means direct QEMU/KVM components only: qemu-system-x86, qemu-utils, OVMF firmware, swtpm if TPM emulation is used, and the ISO/disk helper tools the scripts call. It MUST NOT install or start libvirt-daemon-system merely to build Windows; MUST NOT define, start, or autostart the libvirt default network; MUST NOT create virbr0; MUST NOT start libvirt's dnsmasq; and MUST NOT change host-wide IP forwarding. The observed bad state from the old build-machine provisioning was exactly this class of side effect: dnsmasq listening on 192.168.122.1:53/tcp and 192.168.122.1:53/udp, dnsmasq DHCP listening on 0.0.0.0%virbr0:67/udp, a host-owned virbr0 address 192.168.122.1/24, and host IP forwarding enabled. None of that is required to compile RustDesk for Windows: the golden provisioner needs the guest to make outbound HTTPS connections once, and the per-build VM needs no network at all. QEMU user-mode networking gives the guest internal DHCP/DNS/NAT inside the QEMU process without a host dnsmasq, host bridge, or host port 53/67 listener; the build must use that or --network none, not a host bridge. This requirement does not ban VM control ports as such: a headless Windows install may need VNC, and a manual provision/debug path may need SSH into the guest. Those are permitted only as host-local control channels — for example 127.0.0.1:5901 for QEMU VNC, 127.0.0.1:2222 forwarded to guest :22, or a Unix monitor socket. The forbidden property is a non-loopback host bind, not the existence of a local VNC/SSH control path.
R-B11aMUST Cleanup is the inverse of provisioning and must remove prior harness-created VM networking
scripts/cleanup.sh is the explicit opposite of host-provision.sh. It MUST remove every harness-created VM artifact and only those artifacts: QEMU pidfiles/processes it launched, Unix monitor sockets, swtpm state/sockets, qcow2 overlays, generated ISOs, transient domains if a session libvirt path is still used, and any loopback-only debug forwards. For host packages, it removes only packages recorded in the persistent .provisioned manifest as installed-by-us; it MUST NOT remove or downgrade packages that pre-existed. But because an older harness version did install system libvirt, cleanup also has a security duty: if the manifest says the harness installed libvirt-daemon-system, cleanup MUST stop and undefine the libvirt default network, remove its autostart, and remove the harness-installed package so virbr0 and libvirt dnsmasq disappear. If libvirt pre-existed and therefore cannot be removed safely, cleanup MUST fail closed with an explicit finding instead of pretending the host is clean. A post-cleanup and post-provision audit MUST assert: no virbr0; no listener on 192.168.122.1:53/tcp or :53/udp; no 0.0.0.0%virbr0:67/udp; no new non-loopback TCP or UDP listener of any kind; no host-wide ip_forward change attributable to the harness. The only acceptable build-control listeners are loopback (127.0.0.1) or Unix-domain sockets, and even those are temporary.
R-B12MUST Pin provenance, not just a hash — close the bootstrap TOFU and the codegen/distfile gaps
R-B10 verifies every ./online artifact against a SHA-256 in pins.env, but a hash is only as trustworthy as its origin. The fork MUST establish each first pin by an audited, dual-sourced bootstrap — cross-checking the publisher's own published hash/signature (Rustup, Flutter, LLVM, the NDK, and the Windows toolchain all publish them) against a second path — and record per-pin provenance, so a compromised mirror/CDN at first-fetch cannot silently poison the anchor (the distinction is pinned vs pinned-and-provenance-verified). Three inputs R-B10's enumeration did not originally reach MUST stay under the same regime: (a) the vcpkg distfiles — retained codec overlays consume deterministic captured inputs from ./online with exact SHA512 values; AV1/libaom is no longer a vcpkg dependency (Appendix C #88), and libvpx's retained patched overlay is governed by R-B13/Appendix C #129; (b) the flutter_rust_bridge_codegen binary (R-B7) and the bindgen libclang + the cc compiler versions in the image — codegen-determinism inputs the R-B2 double-build depends on (bindgen output varies with libclang, pinned via LLVM 15.0.6 in R-B5a; name them so an image drift is caught); and (c) the Windows toolchain installers, which are partly evergreen (the VS Build Tools bootstrapper and the Win11 ISO are not stably SHA-addressable) — for these the fork MUST pin the captured offline layout by SHA-256 and document which inputs are publisher-hash-verified vs evergreen-best-effort. Finally, the 38 Cargo git records + 8 pub git records and the hbb_common submodule are anchored only by git's SHA-1 commit hash (no content checksum, unlike the SHA-256-pinned crates.io/pub deps) — a residual bounded by git's collision-detection and the R-B5b diff-audit that MUST be documented as accepted; and any Dart ref: HEAD fork pin MUST be rewritten to its resolved commit so a re-resolve cannot float.
R-B13MUST Retain the patched libvpx v1.15.2 overlay as one closed offline input identity
The retained libvpx overlay is version 1.15.2, port revision 1, with exact upstream fix commit d5f35ac8d93cba7f7a3f7ddb8f9dc8bd28f785e1. The source archive MUST match SHA-512 824fe8719e4115ec359ae0642f5e1cea051d458f09eb8c24d60858cf082f66e411215e23228173ab154044bafbdfbb2d93b589bb726f55b233939b91f928aae0; the patch MUST match SHA-512 2980e0504e207047d55e6c98dcc55c2a3c06315b4ec04d59c42d786657e03ba0e1c73a0718ac6635990aac25fc642b204a1d56e13501ce2bd9625996ad0310d8. scripts/pins.env, the overlay port, the source and patch files in ./online/vcpkg-distfiles, and the complete Windows acquisition manifest MUST agree; the 32-entry Windows tools manifest has SHA-256 c888ee51f816d8fb811ff5e62971b973449d0dadfef683d4cae83119f0e83a8e and individually SHA-512-pins 25 MSYS2 runtime packages, the MinGW x64 pkgconf package, NASM, CMake, Ninja, the two 7-Zip inputs, and PowerShell Core 7.2.24. The overlay accepts only captured file:// source and patch URLs, every required vcpkg tool archive is preloaded into the verified download cache, and the guest blocks origin access: no network fallback is legal. Linux x64, Android arm64, and Windows cache/install reuse MUST be keyed by the baseline, source ref and digest, fix commit and digest, and complete overlay bytes; a missing or mismatched key forces replacement. Every offline Windows build MUST disable binary caching, remove stale installed/build/package libvpx state, rebuild revision 1, and verify the resulting keyed installation before Rust compilation; the golden image is toolchain input, never compiled-libvpx authority. This closes CVE-2026-1861/CVE-2026-2447, two identifiers tracking the same VP9 encoder write_superframe_index heap-write issue. It does not close Appendix C #2b: the in-process VP8/VP9 decoder residual remains accepted and SHOULD be sandboxed.

12.1 The containerized build flow — Debian x86_64 and Android aarch64, in turn

This is the concrete realization of R-B7–R-B9 for the two artifacts that build on the Linux x86_64 host in Docker — the Debian x86_64 .deb and the Android aarch64 .apk, built in turn; the Windows .exe/.msi follows the same discipline in a KVM virtual machine on the same box (§12.2). Both Docker builds run inside a digest-pinned image — the image is the versioned build environment R-B8 demands ("provisioned to the pinned toolchain and nothing more"). This does not contradict §17's "no Docker": that rule governs the controlled host's runtime, never the build. Every toolchain version, vcpkg triplet, feature flag and build command below is taken verbatim from upstream's 1.4.7 build (R-B7, R-B5a) — the fork makes no independent version choices; its only deltas are to pin what upstream uses, run off GitHub on one box, drop code-signing (R-B2), build offline (R-B5b), bind loopback-only (R-D3), and drop the hwcodec/vram features — software codec only (R-R2b).

Build environments are ephemeral instances of an immutable, pinned template

Every target follows the same disposable-instance discipline ("cattle, not pets"). The persistent, version-controlled artifact is the template — the Docker image pinned by digest (Debian/Android) or the golden Windows VM image (§12.2). Each build spins up a fresh, throwaway instance of that template (a docker run --rm container; a direct QEMU/KVM process or transient qemu:///session domain over a copy-on-write qcow2 overlay, with no system libvirt network), runs the validated flow, copies out the artifact and its SHA-256, and is then stopped and deleted — no instance survives the task, and no state carries between builds. That is what makes R-B9's "one mode, no drift" structural rather than aspirational: every build starts from the byte-identical pinned baseline, so the recorded SHA-256 (R-B2) is reproducible, and nothing mutable accumulates on the host.

Why Docker is the faithful path, not a deviation (R-B7)

Upstream already builds the .deb inside a container: flutter-build.yml's build-rustdesk-linux runs every step through rustdesk-org/run-on-arch-action in an ubuntu18.04 container — a deliberately old glibc baseline that keeps the .deb portable. The Android job cross-compiles natively on ubuntu-24.04. So a pinned ubuntu:18.04 image for the .deb and a pinned ubuntu:24.04 image for the .apk reproduce upstream's own environments off GitHub-hosted runners — the "exactly two deltas (no signing, no GitHub runner) and no others" bar of R-B7.

The shared two-stage shape. Each script MUST split a network-enabled fetch stage from a network-isolated compile stage, so "no build.rs / Gradle / vcpkg fetch at compile time" (R-B5b) is proven structurally, not trusted:

  1. Validate (R-B9) — assert the exact pinned versions, the base-image digest, and the submodule + lockfile state; abort on any mismatch before a byte compiles.
  2. Fetch (network on) — cargo vendor the lockfile (including the 28 rustdesk-org records from 21 source URLs, R-B5b), build/restore the vcpkg deps from the pinned baseline, warm the Flutter/pub/Gradle caches.
  3. Compile (--network=none) — build --locked --offline; with the network namespace gone, any forgotten fetch fails loudly here instead of silently succeeding.
  4. Package + self-verify (R-B2, R-B9) — produce the artifact, record its SHA-256, re-check it; never claim success unproven.

No new exposure — loopback only. No build stage opens a non-loopback listener: dockerd is reached over its unix socket with no published ports, the network-on fetch stage is outbound only, and the Windows VM (§12.2) uses QEMU user-mode guest networking for outbound provisioning and --network none for the per-build VM. VM control can still require inbound host sockets: VNC is often the only way to inspect a stuck installer, and an SSH host-forward may be the simplest manual guest-control path. That is acceptable only when the socket is host-local: VNC on 127.0.0.1:5901, SSH forwarding on 127.0.0.1:2222 to guest :22, or a Unix-domain monitor/socket. Binding those same services to 0.0.0.0, 10.0.0.200, 192.168.122.1, virbr0, or any other non-loopback address turns a local control mechanism into host attack surface and is forbidden. The build adds nothing to the host's audited public ports (R-D3) and is safe to run on a no-firewall DMZ host only if this invariant is true.

Debian x86_64 — build-debian.sh

# Dockerfile — base pinned by DIGEST (upstream's glibc baseline), not the floating tag
FROM ubuntu:18.04@sha256:<pin>
# provision the EXACT pinned toolchain, nothing more (R-B5a, R-B8):
#   apt build deps (build-essential, cmake, libgtk-3-dev, libxcb*-dev, libasound2-dev, pkg-config, …)
#   Rust 1.75 x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu  · Flutter 3.24.5 (checkout the 3.24.5 ref, not 'stable')
#   vcpkg @ 120deac3 + res/vcpkg overlay ports

# build-debian.sh, run inside the image:
# --- FETCH (network on) ---
cargo vendor --locked vendor/                          # incl. 27 rustdesk-org records / 21 source URLs (R-B5b)
flutter_rust_bridge_codegen --rust-input src/flutter_ffi.rs …   # REQUIRED upstream step (generate-bridge): emits the UNCOMMITTED bridge; pin FRB 1.80.1 (§3.2/R-B12); codegen bin fetched into ./online, SHA-pinned
$VCPKG_ROOT/vcpkg install --triplet x64-linux          # libvpx,libyuv,opus,… (pin libyuv distfile by SHA512 — R-B12)

# --- COMPILE (run this stage with --network=none) ---
cargo build --locked --offline --lib --release \
      --features flutter,unix-file-copy-paste   # the Rust core — upstream's set minus hwcodec (software codec only, R-R2b)
DEB_ARCH=amd64 python3 ./build.py --flutter --skip-cargo # flutter build linux + dpkg-deb (build_flutter_deb)

# --- PACKAGE + VERIFY (R-B2) ---
mv rustdesk-*.deb rustdesk-x86_64.deb
sha256sum rustdesk-x86_64.deb | tee rustdesk-x86_64.deb.sha256  # target-local proof; build-release.sh creates the sole final manifest

Faithful to upstream's build-rustdesk-linux (cargo build --locked --lib --features flutter,unix-file-copy-paste --release → python3./build.py --flutter --skip-cargo → build_flutter_deb → dpkg-deb), minus signing, the GitHub runner, and the dropped hwcodec feature (R-R2b). The systemd unit + postinst ship in the .deb unchanged (R-D1, §17).

Android aarch64 — build-android.sh

# Dockerfile — base = upstream's Android runner OS, pinned by DIGEST
FROM ubuntu:24.04@sha256:<pin>
# provision the pinned toolchain (R-B5a):
#   JDK (pinned) · Android SDK cmdline-tools (numbered, NOT ';latest') · one platform
#   build-tools <= 34.0.0 (apksigner trap, below) · NDK r28c = 28.2.13676358 (VENDOR zip + verify SHA — r28c is delisted)
#   Flutter 3.24.5 · Rust 1.75 + target aarch64-linux-android · cargo-ndk 3.1.2 (--locked)
#   nasm + yasm (libvpx SIMD asm — build fails without them) · vcpkg @ 120deac3
yes | sdkmanager --licenses                            # non-interactive accept (re-check on a version bump)

# build-android.sh, run inside the image:
# --- FETCH (network on) ---
export ANDROID_NDK_HOME=…  VCPKG_ROOT=…
./flutter/build_android_deps.sh arm64-v8a              # vcpkg install --triplet arm64-android (oboe,libvpx,libyuv,opus,libjpeg-turbo)
cargo vendor --locked vendor/
flutter pub get                                         # warm pub + Gradle caches now; offline later

# --- COMPILE (run this stage with --network=none) ---
./flutter/ndk_arm64.sh                                  # cargo ndk --platform 21 --target aarch64-linux-android build --locked --release --features flutter (hwcodec dropped — software codec only, R-R2b)
cp $ANDROID_NDK_HOME/…/aarch64-linux-android/libc++_shared.so flutter/android/app/src/main/jniLibs/arm64-v8a/
flutter build apk --release --target-platform android-arm64 --split-per-abi

# --- SIGN OUTSIDE THE IMAGE (keystore bind-mounted at sign time ONLY), then VERIFY (R-B2) ---
zipalign -v 4 app-arm64-v8a-release-unsigned.apk aligned.apk      # align BEFORE signing
apksigner sign --ks $KEYSTORE --out rustdesk-arm64.apk aligned.apk
sha256sum rustdesk-arm64.apk | tee rustdesk-arm64.apk.sha256  # target-local proof; build-release.sh creates the sole final manifest

Faithful to upstream's build-rustdesk-android (build_android_deps.sh arm64-v8a → cargo install cargo-ndk --version … --locked → ndk_arm64.sh → flutter build apk --target-platform android-arm64 --split-per-abi → apksigner), minus the GitHub runner. The key is the fork's self-generated local key (no CA, no Play Store) — R-B2.

Versions come from upstream — we only pin and record

No independent version choices: the Android SDK / build-tools, NDK r28c, Flutter, cargo-ndk and the vcpkg triplet are exactly upstream's 1.4.7 values (R-B5a, R-B7) — we commit them and build --locked. Release integrity requires two independent cold builds from the same exact commit and immutable pinned input snapshot, followed by byte-identical signed-APK SHA-256 comparison (R-B2). The BUILD_DATE wall-clock timestamp MUST be neutralized via the pinned SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH; generated Cargo version code belongs only in OUT_DIR; and Cargo, Flutter, Gradle, generated bridges, package staging, and artifact outputs MUST NOT be shared between the two passes. One inherited caveat worth respecting when you pin: apksigner from build-tools ≥ 35 re-pads ZIP alignment, so pin the build-tools version upstream's 1.4.7 build used (or pass --alignment-preserved), and keep zipalign before signing.

The integrity bar for this flow

The release target is same exact commit + same immutable pinned input closure + two independent cold build roots → byte-identical SHA-256 for Debian, the signed Android APK, and both Windows artifacts. The two passes share only content-verified read-only dependency/toolchain inputs; they do not share compiler outputs, generated bridges, package staging, or mutable source state. The consolidated nine-line SHA256SUMS is created only after all four A/B comparisons pass and is re-verified on the controlled host over the trusted channel.

12.2 Windows x86_64 — the ephemeral KVM Windows 11 VM

Windows is the one target that cannot be cross-built from Linux: flutter build windows runs only on a Windows host (the desktop embedder needs Visual Studio's MSVC "Desktop development with C++"), the vcpkg x64-windows-static codecs need cl.exe, and the installer needs WiX — all Windows-only. So the .exe/.msi build in a Windows 11 guest, realized here as an ephemeral KVM virtual machine on the same bare-metal Linux x86_64 box (any x86_64 host with hardware-virtualization / KVM support) — so all three targets live on one machine with no second physical host and no GitHub-hosted runner. (Where the build shares the §17 host, all build tooling must obey the same no-firewall/DMZ model as the runtime: no non-loopback listener, no host bridge DNS/DHCP, no assumption that a router or LAN segment is trusted, and no "temporary during build" exception.)

Observed build-machine network regression — not a Windows-build requirement

The live build host showed libvirt's default network, not a RustDesk runtime service: dnsmasq on 192.168.122.1:53/tcp and 192.168.122.1:53/udp, DHCP on 0.0.0.0%virbr0:67/udp, and virbr0 owning 192.168.122.1/24. Those listeners are bad even though they are not 0.0.0.0. First principles: any non-loopback IP assigned to this host is a local destination in the kernel; interface names are not an internet security boundary; and a compromised router is not just "the public internet through DNAT" but the host's Layer-2 neighbor/default gateway, able to send Ethernet frames directly to the host's MAC with destination 192.168.122.1 or to alter LAN routing/NAT. Even if a daemon such as libvirt's dnsmasq attempts receive-interface filtering, this build must not depend on daemon-specific filtering to make an unwanted service safe. The only host-side bind that is inherently outside the LAN/router threat model is loopback (127.0.0.1) or a Unix-domain socket. The observed QEMU debug listeners 127.0.0.1:2222 (optional guest SSH forward) and 127.0.0.1:5901 (VNC) are acceptable only because they are loopback-only and temporary; the canonical per-build path does not require SSH and uses no guest network at all. The unrelated intentional service observed on 0.0.0.0:8472/tcp is outside the Windows builder and not a precedent for build tooling.

VNC/SSH control ports are allowed only as local ports

The Windows build may need a way for the Linux host to control or inspect the guest. That requirement is real: VNC can be critical during unattended installation, and SSH can be useful for a manual/debug provision path. The safe implementation is local-only: -vnc 127.0.0.1:1 (host 127.0.0.1:5901), hostfwd=tcp:127.0.0.1:2222-:22 if guest SSH is deliberately enabled, and a Unix-domain QEMU monitor socket. These are host-to-guest control channels, not services offered to the LAN. The unsafe implementation is the same service bound to any non-loopback address: 0.0.0.0, 10.0.0.200, 192.168.122.1, virbr0, or another physical/virtual interface. In other words: the port is not the defect; the bind address is. Loopback VNC/SSH may be necessary; non-loopback VNC/SSH is forbidden.

The template — built once. Provision a Windows 11 guest with the pinned toolchain and nothing more (R-B5a): VS 2022 Build Tools + "Desktop development with C++", Windows 11 SDK, LLVM 15.0.6, Flutter 3.24.5, Rust 1.75 (x86_64-pc-windows-msvc), vcpkg @120deac3 (x64-windows-static), WiX, and the unattended build/logon task that runs the checked-in Windows build script — then freeze it read-only as the golden backing image. The template provision step is the only Windows VM step allowed to have guest outbound networking, and that networking MUST be QEMU user-mode networking (-netdev user / SLIRP, or libvirt type='user' on a session connection only) with no host bridge, no TAP, no virbr0, no host dnsmasq, no public host forwarding, and no host-wide forwarding. The per-build VM MUST have no NIC at all (--network none / no -netdev) because it receives source and offline dependencies as virtual media and writes artifacts to an output disk. Windows 11's installer requirements are implementation details: OVMF for UEFI, optional swtpm if the chosen flow uses a TPM rather than an unattended installer bypass, and an inbox-supported NIC such as e1000e for the provisioning-only user-mode network. This golden image is the Windows analog of a digest-pinned Docker image — version-stamped, and the reproducibility anchor. Two integrity steps are mandatory: the golden image MUST be verified by the SHA-256 of its qcow2 before each build (a version-stamp string is forgeable; a content hash is not), and the Windows toolchain installers that seed the image are partly evergreen, so the fork MUST apply R-B12's captured-layout pinning rather than assume a stable upstream URL. If SSH is ever enabled for manual diagnosis, its host key MUST be pinned and the host forward MUST be 127.0.0.1-only; SSH is not a required part of the canonical build flow.

# HOST PREFLIGHT: fail closed if an old VM harness left host networking behind
assert_no_listener 192.168.122.1 53 tcp udp
assert_no_listener '0.0.0.0%virbr0' 67 udp
assert_no_interface virbr0

# TEMPLATE (built once): golden Win11 image, pinned toolchain, frozen read-only
golden=win11-rustdesk-golden.qcow2           # the pinned "image" == read-only backing file
# PROVISION ONLY: guest outbound via SLIRP; no hostfwd by default
# If manual/debug guest SSH is required, replace the next line with:
#   -netdev user,id=net0,hostfwd=tcp:127.0.0.1:2222-:22
# Never hostfwd to 0.0.0.0, 10.0.0.200, 192.168.122.1, or any non-loopback address.
qemu-system-x86_64 ... \
  -drive file="$golden",if=none,id=disk0,format=qcow2 ... \
  -netdev user,id=net0 \
  -device e1000e,netdev=net0 \
  -vnc 127.0.0.1:1 \
  -monitor unix:"$state/monitor.sock",server,nowait

# PER BUILD: a disposable instance with NO NETWORK — the exact analog of `docker run --rm --network=none`
qemu-img create -f qcow2 -b "$golden" -F qcow2 build.qcow2      # copy-on-write overlay, throwaway
qemu-system-x86_64 ... \
  -drive file=build.qcow2,if=none,id=disk0,format=qcow2 ... \
  -drive file=win-build-src.iso,media=cdrom,readonly=on ... \
  -drive file=win-build-offline.iso,media=cdrom,readonly=on ... \
  -drive file=win-build-output.img,format=raw ... \
  -net none \
  -vnc 127.0.0.1:1 \
  -monitor unix:"$state/monitor.sock",server,nowait

# The guest logon task runs build-windows.ps1 from the BUILD medium and writes .exe/.msi plus its exit marker to OUTPUT.
extract_output_disk_readonly win-build-output.img ./dist
sha256sum dist/rustdesk-setup.exe | tee dist/rustdesk-setup.exe.sha256
sha256sum dist/rustdesk.msi | tee dist/rustdesk.msi.sha256    # target-local proof; build-release.sh creates the sole final nine-line SHA256SUMS

# TEARDOWN: stop + delete — no instance survives; the golden image is untouched
qemu_monitor_system_powerdown_or_kill
rm -f build.qcow2 win-build-src.iso win-build-offline.iso win-build-output.img
assert_no_new_non_loopback_listener

build-windows-vm.sh is the Linux-side wrapper that creates the disposable overlay/media/output disk, starts the no-network Windows VM, extracts the output disk read-only, verifies SHA256SUMS, and tears the instance down. Inside the guest, build-windows.ps1 mirrors upstream's build-for-windows-flutter — $ErrorActionPreference = 'Stop', assert the pinned versions, then vcpkg install --triplet x64-windows-static from the prewarmed/offline cache → build.py --portable --flutter (cargo features flutter — hwcodec/vram dropped, software codec only, R-R2b; runs flutter build windows --release) → WiX .msi → record the SHA-256 (R-B9). No cross-compile, no code-signing, no per-build network, and no host listener except temporary loopback/Unix diagnostics — integrity is the recorded SHA-256, verified on the target over the trusted channel (R-B2).

12.3 The script contract — idempotent, defensive, fail-closed

Every script of §12 (R-B9/B10/B11/R-B11a) obeys one behavioral contract, so the harness is predictable regardless of the host's prior state. Each is a convergent, idempotent step: run it when the system already matches pins.env and it is a no-op (it verifies, says "already satisfied," exits 0); run it on a fresh host and it establishes only what is absent. One rule makes that safe.

Defensive means: complain loudly and die — not "deal with it"

A script may create what is missing; it MUST NOT mutate what is present-but-wrong to force a match. A wrong-version tool, a SHA that doesn't match the pin, a conflicting artifact it did not create — each is an anomaly, and the correct response is a precise, non-zero, loud abort naming expected vs found — never a silent upgrade, downgrade, overwrite, retry, or skip. Reconciling a genuine change (a bumped pin) is an explicit operator action — cleanup.sh the stale state, then re-run — not something a script does behind your back. Every component distrusts both the environment and the outputs of its sibling scripts and re-verifies them before acting (set -euo pipefail / $ErrorActionPreference = 'Stop'; every value from pins.env; success proven, never assumed). "Establish-the-absent" is the only mutation any script performs.

ScriptNo-op when already satisfiedEstablishes (absent only)Dies loudly when
host-provision.shevery host runtime present at the pinned version, and the host-network audit is cleaninstalls an absent runtime; records it in .provisioned; installs QEMU/KVM pieces only for Windows, never system libvirt networkinga runtime is present at the wrong version (it never up/downgrades it); OS/arch unsupported; pins.env invalid; virbr0, libvirt dnsmasq, host port 53/67, or any new non-loopback listener exists
online-fetch.shevery ./online artifact present and its SHA-256 matches pins.envfetches an absent artifact, then verifies its SHA-256a present artifact's SHA ≠ pin ("run cleanup.sh, then re-fetch"); a freshly-fetched SHA mismatch; pins.env invalid
build-debian.sh · build-android.sh— always rebuilds in a fresh ephemeral instance (deterministic, so re-running is safe and yields the same bytes)the artifact + its recorded SHA-256./online incomplete/unverified; a host runtime missing or wrong-version; the in-image toolchain ≠ pin; any compile or verify failure
provision-windows-vm.shthe golden Win11 image is present, matches its recorded content SHA-256, and no stale build-host networking existsbuilds the golden image from the ./online installers using guest outbound-only QEMU user networking; creates no host bridge, no host DNS/DHCP, no non-loopback listenerthe golden image is present but stale/mismatched (remove it, re-run); a required ./online Windows artifact is missing; provisioning would require system libvirt default networking or any public/non-loopback host listener
build-windows-vm.sh · build-windows.ps1— fresh no-network overlay each run (§12.2)the .exe/.msi + SHA-256the golden image is absent or mismatched; the in-VM toolchain ≠ pin; the VM has any NIC during per-build; build or verify failure; teardown leaves any non-loopback listener
cleanup.shnothing the harness created remains and the host-network audit is cleanremoves only harness-created instances/images and, explicitly, .provisioned-recorded host packages; reverses old harness-created libvirt default networking if the manifest proves the harness installed ithost-package removal is requested but .provisioned is absent (it refuses rather than guess); libvirt/virbr0/dnsmasq remains but pre-existed and cannot be removed safely; any cleanup would touch unrecorded host state

Net: re-running the whole chain on an already-set-up box does nothing and says so; re-running after a pin bump fails loudly at the first stale artifact and tells you to cleanup.sh first; running it on a foreign or half-broken host stops at the first anomaly with an exact expected-vs-found diagnosis. No step ever "fixes" the host out from under you — and the one mutation it does make (establishing the absent) is recorded so cleanup.sh can undo exactly it (R-B11/R-B11a).

Automation is script-owned: the workflow directory has zero enabled definitions, seven inert .disabled reference definitions, one documentation file, and eight regular files total. Each row has a disposition; the build and supply-chain rows are actioned in §16, the deployment ones in §17.

Native C/C++ codec surface: vcpkg builds libvpx, libyuv, opus, and libjpeg-turbo. The binary is a full viewer, so native media code is present; but the --server role never decodes peer video (it encodes its own screen) — its only inbound C-decode is opus + zstd + clipboard-image, entirely post-PAKE from the authenticated peer (§2). Encode/decode are software-only by compile-out: hwcodec/vram and ffmpeg's amf/nvcodec/qsv are dropped on every build; AV1/libaom is runtime-quarantined and removed as a dependency; VP8/VP9 remain the supported software video codecs. That surface is reached only when a box views — the universal residual the fork SHOULD sandbox (Appendix C #2b). Audit caveat: these are vcpkg C libraries, not crates — cargo-audit/RUSTSEC (R-A7, Appendix D) cannot see their CVEs, so R-B5a pinning buys reproducibility, not CVE-freedom against this surfacevcpkg.json, libs/scrap, hwcodec--server never decodes; SHOULD-sandbox; PIN (≠ CVE-free)
Memory-safety debt: 850 lexical unsafe { blocks across 251 tracked Rust files, with at least one match in 73 files (FFI to the C libs, screen capture, input injection, IPC). This inventory is a lexical count, explicitly not an AST proof.tree-wideTRACK; review any pre-PAKE-reachable ones
Release profile is panic=abort + no overflow-checks (integer overflow wraps silently); the AddrMangle::decode underflow on crafted bytes is mediator-only — unreachable on the direct-only path, and with overflow-checks off it yields a garbage address, not a crash (audited: no other pre-auth integer/panic/OOB defect)Cargo.toml; hbb_common/src/lib.rssaturating arithmetic; overflow-checks in CI
Bundled default rendezvous public key + servers (the rendezvous trust anchor) — vestigial in a serverless deployment but compiled inconfig.rsPIN/replace own values (R-X4)
Rendezvous/relay client (27 fns) — live by default, connects to rs-ny.rustdesk.com and runs pre-PAKE signaling; the source stays (removing it is a non-goal, §14) but every shipped binary compiles it outrendezvous_mediator.rs; config.rsCOMPILE OUT (R-D4)
hbb_common is the one git submodule, and we change it (the PAKE spans it)libs/hbb_commonABSORB in-tree (§16)
Android client declares 12 runtime permissions (manifest :5-16): accessibility input-injection, SYSTEM_ALERT_WINDOW, MANAGE_EXTERNAL_STORAGE, legacy READ/WRITE_EXTERNAL_STORAGE, RECORD_AUDIO, boot-persistence, …AndroidManifest.xmlDROP legacy storage; keep dual-role minimum (R-X6)
Windows build-machine host-network expansion: the prior harness installed system libvirt and started its default NAT network, creating virbr0, 192.168.122.1, libvirt dnsmasq DNS on :53/tcp/:53/udp, DHCP on :67/udp, and host forwarding. This is not a RustDesk binary exposure; it is a build-host exposure caused by the Windows VM setup. Non-loopback interface-specific listeners are still bad on a no-firewall DMZ host because a hostile router/L2 neighbor can target host-owned private addresses directlyscripts/host-provision.sh; scripts/cleanup.sh; scripts/provision-windows-vm.sh; scripts/build-windows-vm.shREMOVE FROM HARNESS — no libvirt-daemon-system, no default network, no virbr0, no host DNS/DHCP; cleanup reverses old harness-created state; QEMU user-mode outbound only for golden provisioning and no NIC for per-build (R-B11/R-B11a/§12.2)

14. Non-goals

  • The rendezvous/relay servers (hbbs/hbbr) — serverless by design. (Self-hosting that pair is the upstream-sanctioned road to sovereign-and-encrypted, but it adds internet-facing ports and key-managed services and surrenders the "connect by address, nothing pre-configured" property the PAKE fork keeps.)
  • A new GUI / protocol redesign — keep the engine; change the handshake, the invariants, and excise the dangerous paths, nothing gratuitous.
  • Defending a compromised endpoint or a password-knowing adversary.
  • Host network segmentation — the fork must not worsen pivot risk but does not implement the host firewall/VLAN.

15. Acceptance criteria

  1. The unconditional PAKE (§10) is the only connection path — the responder's accept choke point and, when a box views, the initiator's connect path (the unkeyed direct-IP early-returns deleted, R-S13); the secure parameter, the plaintext path, the trust escape-hatch, and the fingerprint ceremony are deleted; CI proves their absence (R-A6).
  2. All six transports are keyed and all five types + auxiliary channels covered (all five reachable; the terminal pinned ON by the pinned policy, R-X8); the reconnect, switch-sides, trusted-device, and post-key password-challenge paths are deleted — authorization collapses onto the single CPace KEYED edge (R-S2), not re-gated — proven by R-A1/A2/A6/A8.
  3. The port-forward set_raw escape is sealed and wire-capture proves the RDP tunnel is ciphertext (R-A9); no transcript yields offline-crackable material (R-S6).
  4. Pre-auth frame length is capped (R-S7); file writes use no-follow opens, with file transfer a single full-filesystem mode and no confinement toggle (R-S8); the controlled-side security policy — permanent-password-only, password approve-mode, the capability set, egress-empty — is a compile-time table the Config::get_option funnel returns and the is_option_can_save guard rejects, not a permissive default a peer, the rendezvous server, or a local write can change (R-S16), with the one-time-password path excised so the permanent password is the sole credential and sole PAKE PRS (R-S9, R-X7).
  5. The updater, plugin loader, ConfigureUpdate body, key/server overrides, and the os_login→PAM desktop-session-start are excised; the terminal is pinned ON by the immutable enable-terminal=Y pin — full control for the authenticated owner is the single mode, while the second-credential PAM/LogonUserW path stays excised; CI proves both (§8, R-A6, R-X8, R-X14); the trust anchor equals the pinned value (R-A4).
  6. Crypto verification R-V1–V2 complete, findings resolved; the AI-conducted single-model review published (docs/CRYPTO-AUDIT-2026-07-02.md, 2026-07-02, findings resolved) is recorded without claiming independent assurance; R-V3's external expert audit remains outstanding and release notes state that boundary exactly.
  7. Unsigned (SHA-256-pinned), source-published, smoke-tested binaries for Android, Windows, and Linux — one package per platform, each a single binary usable as viewer or, via --server, the exposed box (R-R2b) — produced off GitHub-hosted runners by env-validating per-host scripts (Debian + Android on the Linux box, Windows on a Windows 11 machine; R-B8/B9), having first reproduced upstream 1.4.7's official build (R-B7), --locked from the pinned toolchain (R-B5a), Sciter dropped (R-B6).
  8. The deployed surface is understood port-by-port: the shipped binary, run as --server, exposes exactly one inbound v4 TCP port and zero UDP, makes no unsolicited egress, has the rendezvous mediator and LAN discovery absent and the terminal pinned ON by policy as the authenticated owner's full control — on every shipped target (R-D3–R-D7, R-X5, R-X8); a startup self-check asserts the live socket surface (R-A4), so the box is defensible even with no firewall — the firewall is a redundant tripwire, not the gate.
  9. The exposed box runs the one binary as --server (R-R2b): §1's "one strict mode" is structural by deletion — the mediator, relay, KCP, LAN discovery, updater and plugin loader are gone from the tree (sound symbol-greps) — while the viewer/decoder code that necessarily remains (the binary is a full viewer) is unreachable from the --server entry (R-A6 call-graph) and held off the wire by R-A4's live socket-surface assertion. The native-decoder residual is universal and documented (Appendix C #2b), the fork SHOULD-sandboxing it.
  10. Sovereign by construction (§18): every shipped binary — on all three platforms — makes zero connections to any global resource and carries no auto-update, version-check, or self-install path; CI proves the §18 token set compiled-absent (sound grep) or call-graph-unreachable (the dual-use STUN/NAT probes) per target (R-SV10), the runtime self-check covers the --server process (zero UDP, one TCP listener), and the build smoke-test (R-B4) confirms no unsolicited outbound connect. The rendezvous/relay/STUN stack, the updater, the api.rustdesk.com version-check, the Telegram push, the audit/account POSTs and the iOS Firebase plist are compiled out, not config-pinned (R-SV1–R-SV9). The only surviving network capability is the direct, PAKE-gated peer connection by IP — connect-by-ID is removed on every platform, so R-F2/R-F3 hold for peers that present a reachable address; the relay-only NAT-traversal case is a deliberate, accepted drop (R-SV5, R-F3).
  11. Apple source-conformance (R-R2): iOS and macOS are not built, but a cargo check --target {x86_64,aarch64}-apple-darwin + aarch64-apple-ios pass proves the philosophies hold on the Apple code paths — the shared wire/auth/config/fs core inherited with no Apple #[cfg], and the Apple platform surfaces (the osascript root updater/install and DMG fetch-and-run, the two in-process root-exec primitives, the authenticated desktop _url IPC, the deep-link incl. the iOS transport and the Rust ?key= adoption, the key/get_key() override, the sandbox-off/persistent-TCC entitlements, and the §18 egress) excised or hardened in source — so any future Apple build on a real Mac is the hardened fork, never the un-hardened upstream.
The one-sentence bar

Connecting by address is encrypted and mutually password-authenticated across every transport, type, and content channel, with no plaintext escape and no fetch-and-run or override path left in the building; the program refuses to run if any of that is untrue; and a build exists for every target device — making it, on the wire, as defensible as SSH.

16. Repository structure (the monorepo)

The fork changes a small, bounded part of RustDesk: the rustdesk application, the hbb_common protocol library, and the exact pinned portable-pty child boundary required by R-S11t (Appendix A). It does not copy the dependency universe into the repo. The monorepo holds only code we fork or author plus that one provenance-recorded PTY source; every other dependency stays an unmodified upstream artifact, pinned by the committed lockfiles.

Pinning ≠ vendoring

The reproducible pin is already done, upstream: the committed Cargo.lock (909 package records — all 37 git-sourced records pinned to exact commits), flutter/pubspec.lock (199 package records), and vcpkg.json (registry baseline 120deac3) fix every transitive version. A --locked build is reproducible without vendoring a single third-party line; the one in-tree portable-pty exception is exact pinned source ownership required by R-S11t. We add only the one toolchain pin upstream omits: a committed rust-toolchain.toml.

rustdesk_fork/                  # ONE repo — fork of rustdesk/rustdesk; only what we change lives here
├─ Cargo.toml  Cargo.lock        # 909 package records; 37 git records / 27 source URLs
├─ rust-toolchain.toml           # NEW — pin Rust in-tree (bumped past 1.75 once Sciter is gone)
├─ src/                          # CHANGED: PAKE choke point, excisions, defaults
├─ flutter/                      # CHANGED: deep-link excision (R-X6); pubspec.lock has 199 records
├─ libs/
│  ├─ hbb_common/                # ABSORBED submodule (in-tree): PAKE/secretbox/fs/codec live here
