R-S11v/R-S11e-36 then corrected the remaining broker design by deleting machine-wide basename termination entirely and making each privacy instance own one exact job/process/PID/window. R-S11w/R-S11e-37 corrects the remaining consent-state heuristic: exact-name ToolHelp results are candidates only, and UAC state requires the current process session plus the handle-pinned full image path of the no-reparse System32 consent.exe. The obsolete termination helper, server startup sweep, portable taskkill, generic PID selector, and unused LogonUI heuristic are absent. verify.sh and the semantic mutation verifier bind the final authority model rather than the superseded native-by-basename intermediate design.
39Windows MSI runtime-generated executable cleanup completion authority — the MSI RemoveRuntimeGeneratedFiles deferred non-impersonated custom action was scheduled on uninstall/upgrade to remove RuntimeBroker_rustdesk.exe from the installed Program Files directory, but it discarded the deletion result, treated an empty or root install folder as a successful skip, and declared the WiX action Return="ignore". A privileged cleanup action that owns an installed executable payload must either prove the payload is absent/deleted or fail closed; silent continuation can leave stale executable state behind after an update or uninstall.FIX R-S11d-4 makes runtime-generated executable cleanup checked. Empty install-folder data and root install-folder targets are fatal packaging errors; the broker cleanup requires DeleteRuntimeGeneratedFile(...) to succeed, where absent files remain a successful no-op and directories/reparse-target hazards remain refused by the existing handle-based no-follow deletion path; and RemoveRuntimeGeneratedFiles is declared Return="check". The scheduled no-op CustomActionHello sample action is deleted from the MSI surface. verify.sh asserts the checked delete result, checked WiX return policy, absence of the old ignored return, and absence of the sample custom action.
43Windows RDP viewer credential handling and command provenance — the viewer-side RDP tunnel originally launched cmdkey and mstsc through ambient executable search, exposed a saved password in cmdkey /pass: argv, and left the seeded credential behind. The intermediate native-API design removed argv/environment disclosure but still mutated the account-global TERMSRV/localhost Credential Manager entry under only a process-local lease. A crash, concurrent RustDesk process, or independent same-user credential update could therefore strand, overwrite, or restore stale account state; snapshots also retained another credential value in process memory. This is viewer-side same-user credential corruption/exposure, not a service/SYSTEM escalation path, but it is not an acceptable durable-secret model.FIX R-S11d-8 deletes RDP credential pre-seeding rather than attempting another lease. The RDP dialog stores only the remote port; rdp_username and rdp_password are retired options removed on every peer-config load/store, with legacy files rewritten without them. No username or password enters the tunnel supervisor, process environment, command line, or Windows Credential Manager. The helper resolves mstsc.exe through the checked GetSystemDirectoryW-based trusted-tool resolver and launches it with exactly the ephemeral loopback endpoint /v:localhost:<port> and /prompt. Authentication and any credential retention are left to the trusted Windows RDP client UI and OS policy. verify.sh asserts the exact endpoint/prompt argument set, trusted executable resolution, retired-option scrubbing and rewrite tests, absence of credential UI/transport, and absence of cmdkey, bare mstsc, Credential Manager APIs, TERMSRV/localhost, password argv, and credential leases.
44Windows terminal default-shell command provenance — the Windows terminal helper selected its default shell from a current-directory pwsh.exe candidate, then from Windows PowerShell, then from the caller's COMSPEC environment variable or a bare cmd.exe fallback. The terminal feature intentionally grants an authenticated terminal user a shell, and installed-service mode runs the helper/shell as the current logon user rather than SYSTEM. The defect was narrower but still real: a remote-triggered terminal-open path must not choose the shell executable from caller current-directory or environment state.FIX R-S11d-9 makes Windows terminal default-shell selection absolute-only and fail-closed. The helper resolves System32 with GetSystemDirectoryW, tries only C:\Program Files\PowerShell\7\pwsh.exe, C:\Program Files\PowerShell\6\pwsh.exe, System32\WindowsPowerShell\v1.0\powershell.exe, and System32\cmd.exe, and returns an error if no trusted shell is present. Both the helper ConPTY path and the direct terminal path propagate that error instead of launching a bare fallback. verify.sh asserts fallible shell resolution, the trusted candidate set, absence of COMSPEC/bare pwsh.exe/bare cmd.exe fallback, fail-closed propagation, and this requirements/ledger disposition.
45Windows portable RuntimeBroker cleanup command provenance — the Windows portable launcher killed every process named RuntimeBroker_rustdesk.exe with a PATH-selected taskkill before copying a broker payload. The arguments were fixed and the cleanup was local portable-launch behavior, but a deliberately elevated launcher would apply that token to every basename match and ambient executable lookup was an additional provenance defect.FIX R-S11d-10 originally bound taskkill.exe to System32 as an intermediate command-provenance correction. R-S11v/R-S11e-36 supersedes that design by deleting portable broker copying and cleanup entirely: the portable wrapper does not own installed-service privacy mode, and a privacy instance now owns only the exact broker it creates in a kill-on-close job. No taskkill, broker-copy compatibility path, or basename cleanup remains, and the source gate forbids reintroduction.
46Windows unsupported 32-bit WMIC process-probe deletion — inherited non-x64 Windows process helpers kept a wmic.exe fallback for command-line process probes because 32-bit Windows builds could not reliably read process command lines through the structured process snapshot path. This fork's release matrix ships Windows x86_64 only; retaining an unsupported compatibility branch that starts an external process-query tool is a wrong provenance surface, even if it is not in the live release artifact.FIX R-S11d-11 deletes the unsupported WMIC branch instead of hardening it. The Windows non-64-bit detours in check_process and the shared platform process helpers are gone; get_pids_with_args_by_wmic, get_pids_with_first_arg_by_wmic, get_pids_with_first_arg_check_session, their parsers, and their parser tests are removed. Non-mobile process probes use the structured sysinfo enumeration path only. verify.sh rejects any reintroduced WMIC helper, by_wmic helper, or Windows non-64-bit process-probe cfg detour under src/common.rs / src/platform, and asserts this requirements/ledger disposition.
47Service-owned voice-call input IPC mutation — MainIpcRequest::SetVoiceCallInput was a typed main-channel operation, but it was still admitted unconditionally by the desktop IPC mutation policy. On a service-owned receiver this let an ordinary local main-IPC peer mutate service-owned runtime audio-selection state through the same generic channel whose credential and policy writes had already been receiver-scoped. This is not a remote protocol path and not a root shell by itself; it is the remaining service-owned main-channel state mutation that was not tied to receiver ownership.FIX R-S11c-14 gates MainIpcRequest::SetVoiceCallInput through MainIpcAuthority::allows_main_channel_voice_call_input_write, which admits user-owned receivers only. Service-owned receivers now reject the operation on Linux, macOS, and Windows main IPC; the typed operation remains available for user-owned desktop receivers. verify.sh asserts the authority helper, the receiver-gated handler arm, and absence of an unconditional service-owned write path.
48Local credential-bearing store file hardening — per-peer config load used a direct confy::load_path(Self::path(id)) call rather than the fork's hardened config wrapper, and address-book/group stores wrote raw encrypted compressed payloads with direct File::create plus ignored write_all errors. Those stores carry connect-relevant local material such as per-peer saved credentials and address-book/group access tokens. The address-book/group files are raw encrypted byte payloads, not TOML; changing them to the TOML config writer would silently change their on-disk format.FIX R-S11b-4d routes PeerConfig::load through a typed hardened loader so Windows ACL preparation, corrupt-config preservation, and recovery-file hardening apply, and peer enumeration removes a peer file only after a successful load of that exact enumerated file as a semantically default PeerConfig, never after loading a different canonicalized peer path, never after transient unreadable/corrupt/default status, and never when saved options or connect-equivalent credentials are present. Address-book and group persistence keep the existing encrypted raw-byte format, but write through a raw temp-and-replace helper that prepares the Windows config ACL, writes owner-only 0600 files on Unix, uses MoveFileExW with replace-existing/write-through semantics on Windows, hardens the final Windows file, and logs store/load/remove failures. Present-but-unreadable or corrupt TOML/raw encrypted payloads are preserved as sibling recovery files instead of being deleted; recovery files are immediately rehardened through the same helper, using O_NOFOLLOW plus descriptor fchmod(0600) on Unix and the protected config DACL on Windows. verify.sh asserts the typed peer-config load status, exact-path loaded-and-semantically-default empty-peer cleanup, raw-byte helper shape, Windows ACL preparation, Unix owner-only mode, replace-existing/write-through primitive, corrupt-payload preservation and hardening, absence of direct File::create(Self::path())/write_all(...).ok() in these stores, and the raw/TOML recovery permission, symlink-rejection, replacement, transient-load, RDP-password, and alias-path cleanup-policy regression tests.
49Windows privacy broker and user shortcut process provenance — privacy mode launched RuntimeBroker_rustdesk.exe with CreateProcessAsUserW using a null lpApplicationName and a path-only mutable command line, leaving Windows module-name parsing to the command-line string. Separately, the user-facing "create shortcut" action generated a VBScript containing the peer id/executable path and launched cscript to create a Desktop shortcut. The privacy broker path can be remote-triggered after authentication through privacy mode; the shortcut path is same-user UI convenience, but it still should not create script literals or depend on script-host execution for a native shell-link operation.FIX R-S11d-12 binds the privacy broker executable as the explicit lpApplicationName, passes a null command line, verifies the broker and injection DLL are files, and supplies the broker directory as the current directory. The user shortcut path now validates the direct connect id, resolves the current executable and Desktop known folder, and creates the shortcut through native COM IShellLinkW/IPersistFile rather than per-shortcut VBScript or cscript. verify.sh asserts explicit broker application-name launch, absence of the old command-line module parsing variable, native ShellLink shortcut creation, id validation, Desktop known-folder use, absence of script-backed shortcut code in create_shortcut, and this requirements/ledger disposition.
50Windows service and session-token process launch provenance — the installed LocalSystem service and root/session handoff helpers launched --server, --tray, connection-manager, and whiteboard children through a single mutable command-line string while passing null lpApplicationName to CreateProcessAsUserW. That left executable identity to Windows command-line module parsing on the service-owned privilege transition and on token-switched user-session launches.FIX R-S11d-13 makes the process-launch API carry the executable path separately from argv text and carries an explicit current directory derived from that executable. Token-switched launches now require an absolute existing executable file, pass that path as the explicit lpApplicationName, pass the executable parent as lpCurrentDirectory, build argv with Windows command-line quoting, reject NUL-bearing application/current-directory/argv/environment data, and use the command line only as argument text. The obsolete preformatted command-string wrapper is gone. verify.sh asserts the explicit application-name/current-directory FFI shape, absence of null-application CreateProcessAsUserW and fixed MAX_PATH command buffers, absolute-file validation, quoted argv construction, the service-owned --server launch through the new helper, user-session launch reuse of the same helper, and this requirements/ledger disposition.
51Windows service/session token source provenance — the token helper for service-owned and token-switched process launches selected authority by enumerating processes and accepting the first same-session basename match: explorer.exe for user launches, winlogon.exe for service-owned launches, with a sihost.exe username fallback. The selected process was opened with PROCESS_ALL_ACCESS and its token with TOKEN_ALL_ACCESS. A process basename is not an authority proof for a LocalSystem service deciding which token to hand to CreateProcessAsUserW.FIX R-S11d-14 replaces user-token process hunting with WTSQueryUserToken for the target session. The service-owned SYSTEM path accepts only same-session winlogon.exe after validating its full image path against %SystemRoot%\System32\winlogon.exe, validating the token session, and validating the token user as LocalSystem. The launcher requests minimum process/token rights for query and CreateProcessAsUserW rather than all-access handles, deletes the Explorer/sihost fallback path, and removes the old Explorer-name error suppression. verify.sh asserts the WTS user-token source, trusted winlogon image/SID/session validation, minimum handle rights, absence of basename user-token fallbacks, absence of launcher all-access token opens, and this requirements/ledger disposition.
59Desktop service lifecycle completion authority — the explicit desktop service CLI, platform wrappers, and Debian package lifecycle had several ways to report success without proving the service action completed. core_main ignored install_service()/uninstall_service() returns; Linux service install imported active-user whole-config files into root service state before start, install/uninstall discarded systemctl enable/disable, and uninstall treated stop failure as success; Debian maintainer scripts masked deb-systemd-invoke/deb-systemd-helper stop/start/reload/disable/purge failures with || true; build.py could hide Debian staging/control-script copy and dpkg-deb failures; macOS service install/uninstall treated every successful osascript spawn as the same branch regardless of nonzero exit, checked only one plist postcondition, discarded local launchctl status, and the privileged uninstall script masked daemon unload failure with || true before deleting plists.FIX R-S11c-16 and R-S11c-10j make service lifecycle completion status-authoritative. The CLI exits nonzero when install/uninstall wrappers return failure. Linux no longer imports active-user Config/Config2 files into root service state during install; service-owned unattended password provisioning uses raw _service_password with socket-bound polkit authorization, while generic _service carries no password body, and systemctl enable, start, disable, and stop failures are fatal wrapper failures with logged status. Debian maintainer scripts use deb-systemd-helper for enable/disable/purge state and deb-systemd-invoke only for unit stop/start, while manager reload is the checked fixed command /bin/systemctl --system daemon-reload; none of those failures are masked: upgrades stop an existing old unit before transition, configure checks enable/manager-reload/start, removal/deconfigure checks stop/disable/manager-reload before deleting entry points/unit files, and purge checks helper-state plus the stock /root/.config/RustDesk service-config tree removal. Debian packaging starts from clean tmpdeb staging, uses checked control-script copies, splits dpkg-deb from cleanup, and the release build extracts the emitted .deb to compare preinst/postinst/prerm/postrm with source and run the same maintainer-script lifecycle validator before hashing artifacts, rejecting masked lifecycle failures, invalid helper actions, or missing manager reloads. macOS routes AppleScript and launchctl calls through checked status handling, requires both daemon and agent plist postconditions, propagates synchronous uninstall result, removes/reloads the current-session LaunchAgent label with verification, and the privileged scripts verify daemon unload/load state and plist removal instead of masking launchctl errors. verify.sh asserts the cross-desktop CLI, Linux, Debian-package, and macOS invariants; build-debian.sh validates built Debian control scripts; apple-conform-check.sh mirrors the macOS source-conformance half.
60Windows MSI service-state and SAS policy persistence — installer state was derived from user config and multiple installer/runtime paths wrote machine SAS policy without transactional ownership.FIX WiX declaratively owns the LocalSystem service and restart policy; config-derived and hand-written service actions are deleted. Installer writes and uninstall deletes for SoftwareSASGeneration are absent. Runtime opens the administrator policy with KEY_READ, permits only service-enabled values 1 and 3, rejects all other/absent/read-error states, and never sets, deletes, restores, or otherwise mutates the value.
61Linux polkit policy/package assurance — Linux installed-service password changes use raw _service_password plus socket-bound polkit authorization, but source inspection alone does not prove that every Debian package ships exactly the intended administrator-authentication policy or rejects a permissive repo-owned .rules override. Distro-local administrator policy overrides are outside app-side LPE, but the fork's package must not introduce permissive service-owned credential policy drift.FIX R-S11e/R-S11i bind authorization to the raw socket's SO_PEERCRED PID, uid, and process start time, invoke trusted absolute /usr/bin/pkcheck with --action-id, --process, and --allow-user-interaction, bound and reap that process, and let only the root service commit after exact parent/replica proof. The assurance gate parses res/com.carriez.RustDesk.policy as XML and requires exactly one action, com.carriez.RustDesk.set-unattended-password, with allow_any, allow_inactive, and allow_active all set to auth_admin; it rejects yes, auth_self, and keep-style authorization values, rejects repo-shipped matching .rules overrides, asserts that all Debian build paths stage the policy into usr/share/polkit-1/actions, and rejects the obsolete executable usr/share/rustdesk/files/polkit stub. build-debian.sh validates each emitted .deb before hashing it: the package must contain the policy at usr/share/polkit-1/actions/com.carriez.RustDesk.policy as a root-owned regular file that is not group/world writable, its XML semantics and bytes must match the source policy, and no legacy executable polkit stub may be packaged. verify.sh and build-debian.sh call the same policy checker.
62Windows helper launch environment authority — token-switched helper launches carry CM and whiteboard launch-token proof material in environment variables. If the target user's inherited environment already contains a same-name RustDesk launch-proof variable, appending the launcher-owned value produces an ambiguous environment block whose effective value depends on environment-block lookup behavior rather than the service's generated authority. This is helper launch ambiguity and denial rather than a proven SYSTEM shell, but the authority boundary is still wrong: launch proof material belongs to the launcher, not ambient user profile state.FIX R-S11c-15 makes the Windows LaunchProcessWin environment merge explicit. The C++ path parses environment keys, removes inherited same-name entries with case-insensitive comparison before appending launcher-owned variables, and sorts the final environment block before CreateProcessAsUserW, matching the Win32 environment-block contract. verify.sh asserts the key parser, case-insensitive key comparison, base-entry removal for extra variables, final sort, and that the merge helper stays outside C linkage.
65Windows Amyuni SetupAPI install reboot-required completion — the active Amyuni virtual-display install path already rejected reboot-required completion from the checked deviceinstaller64.exe helper before using the driver, but the direct SetupAPI fallback called win_device::install_driver(), received the same reboot_required authority signal, and ignored it before reporting the driver install usable.FIX R-S11d-25 makes the direct SetupAPI install fallback follow the helper-path install policy: after win_device::install_driver(), reboot_required is fatal because the driver cannot be treated as immediately usable. Remove/cleanup reboot-required remains accepted under the cleanup policy; this fix is scoped to install/update before monitor plug-in proceeds. verify.sh asserts the direct SetupAPI install call, the reboot_required branch, fatal install reboot-required error, absence of the old discarded install result shape, and this requirements/ledger disposition.
66Windows app-name identity contract — signed custom-client "app-name" and MSI --app-name are system identifiers, not arbitrary display strings: the same value is used as an executable/path component, URI-scheme prefix, service name, HKCR/HKLM registry key component, firewall rule label, and shortcut path component. In current master this was not a local caller-writable LPE because the live sources are signed custom-client config and build-time MSI packaging, but unsafe signer/build input could still cross into privileged service/protocol/path sinks without an identifier grammar.FIX R-S11d-26 defines app-name as a constrained ASCII system identifier: 1-64 characters, first character letter, last character letter or digit, interior characters letters/digits/hyphen only. Signed custom-client parsing rejects non-string or invalid "app-name" before applying any payload settings, and MSI preprocessing rejects invalid --app-name before resource/package generation. verify.sh asserts the Rust validator, that the signed-config APP_NAME write is guarded by it, the MSI regex/front-door check, and this requirements/ledger disposition.
67Windows custom-client public staging deletion — a dormant Windows custom-client update helper copied custom.txt from a public-style staging directory under RustDeskCustomClientStaging into the current executable directory and then loaded it as runtime custom-client configuration. The cluster had no live caller in current source, generated bindings, scripts, packaging, tests, or docs, and signed custom-client parsing now rejects invalid identity fields before applying payload settings. Still, a public staging-to-install-directory copy has no supported authority model in this fork: custom-client identity and policy come from signed runtime configuration or build/package inputs, not from a mutable public filesystem mailbox.FIX R-S11d-27 deletes the Windows public staging helper rather than hardening or leaving it as dormant compatibility code. get_public_base_dir, get_custom_client_staging_dir, remove_custom_client_staging_dir, prepare_custom_client_update, the RustDeskCustomClientStaging path, and the executable-directory custom.txt copy/load path are absent from src/platform/windows.rs. verify.sh fails if those symbols, the staging directory name, or the executable-dir copy sink reappear, and gates this requirements/ledger disposition.
68Windows dormant diagnostic message-box deletion — a legacy Rust diagnostic macro had a Windows-only branch that routed text into a platform message_box helper. The macro had no callers, and the helper was not a cross-platform trait requirement, exported FFI surface, or Flutter bridge API. The dormant helper still contained environment-controlled behavior: NO_DIALOG suppressed the dialog, PRINT_OUT printed text, WRITE_TO_FILE wrote to an environment-selected path, and the non-dialog path wrote text to the clipboard while discarding errors. This was not a live privilege escalation because the macro/helper were unreferenced, but privileged builds should not retain dormant environment-selected file/clipboard side-effect code.FIX R-S11d-28 deletes the unused my_println! macro and the Windows message_box helper. The peer protocol/UI MessageBox flow is unaffected. verify.sh fails if my_println!, macro_rules! my_println, the Windows message_box helper, NO_DIALOG, PRINT_OUT, WRITE_TO_FILE, or the old diagnostic message strings reappear, and gates this requirements/ledger disposition.
69Windows service-adjacent path known-folder authority — Windows service-adjacent code derived profile and root-recording paths from the process SystemDrive environment. These paths were not a proven unprivileged-to-SYSTEM primitive, but protected system/profile roots in a privileged-capable process must come from Windows known-folder APIs rather than mutable environment text.FIX R-S11d-29 removes SystemDrive from the Windows profile and root-recording path surface. Active-user home fallback resolves FOLDERID_UserProfiles, rejects usernames that are not a single safe path component, and joins under that known folder. Root recording resolves FOLDERID_ProgramData and fails closed if it is unavailable. verify.sh rejects reintroduced SystemDrive use and asserts the known-folder, username-component, and root-recording invariants.
70macOS runtime service ACL inspection provenance — the macOS _service IPC installed-app/helper trust check and the service-owned LaunchAgent password-snapshot plist trust check rejected extended ACLs by spawning absolute /bin/ls -lde and parsing its formatted output. This was not a demonstrated command-injection primitive because the command path was absolute and the checked path was argv-passed, but it was still the wrong authority model for root-side runtime trust: filesystem ACL state must come from the kernel/libc ACL interface, not from an external formatter process.FIX R-S11c-17 makes runtime ACL inspection native and shared. src/ipc/auth.rs now owns a single macos_path_has_no_extended_acl helper that builds a checked C path, calls acl_get_link_np(..., ACL_TYPE_EXTENDED), validates the returned ACL with acl_valid_link_np, rejects any first entry returned by acl_get_entry, and frees the ACL through a drop guard. NUL paths, ACL retrieval failure, and ACL validation failure reject the path. src/ipc.rs uses that shared helper for the LaunchAgent plist parent/file trust check instead of carrying a second ls parser. verify.sh and apple-conform-check.sh assert the native ACL API shape and reject MACOS_LS / Command::new(MACOS_LS) in the Rust runtime trust path. The separate installer shell ACL surface is closed by R-S11c-18.
72Windows privacy broker served-session authority — the topmost-window privacy implementation launched RuntimeBroker_rustdesk.exe by taking a user token from WTSGetActiveConsoleSessionId(). That is the physical-console session, not necessarily the Windows session currently being served by the installed RustDesk service when RDP/ICA session sharing is enabled. The service-owned server already runs inside the selected served session, so the privacy broker must follow that receiver session rather than re-selecting a different interactive session at broker-launch time.FIX R-S11d-31 removes the physical-console lookup from the privacy broker. The broker launch now resolves the current process session id of the service-owned --server process handling the authenticated Remote session, fails closed if that session id is unavailable or invalid, obtains the user token for that served session, and launches the broker there. verify.sh rejects WTSGetActiveConsoleSessionId in the privacy broker source and asserts the served-session helper, token error shape, explicit broker executable launch, null command line, current directory, and file-existence checks.
73macOS privileged installer ACL enforcement provenance — after R-S11c-17 moved runtime ACL trust to native libc ACL APIs, the admin-authorized macOS installer still proved helper ACL-free state by piping absolute /bin/ls -lde formatter output into /usr/bin/awk. This was not a command-injection primitive because paths were quoted and the tools were absolute, but a privileged installer should not make service-helper authority depend on parsing a human-readable listing format.FIX R-S11c-18 removes the installer ls -lde / awk ACL parser. The bundled helper source is verified as a non-symlink executable with the pinned helper code-signing requirement, then the deployed /Library/PrivilegedHelperTools directory and com.carriez.rustdesk_service helper are made ACL-free with checked /bin/chmod -N postconditions before root:wheel, mode, executable-bit, byte-compare, and code-signing checks. verify.sh and apple-conform-check.sh require that deployed-helper ACL postcondition and fail if installer ACL authority reintroduces /bin/ls -lde or the old NR > 1 parser.
74macOS LaunchAgent live argv authority — the service-owned password runtime snapshot path already required launchd to report the requesting pid under the expected root-owned LaunchAgent plist, and it parsed that plist to require the exact ProgramArguments array. The live peer-process proof, however, accepted any argv whose second and third entries were --server and --service-owned-server, leaving extra live arguments outside the proof even though they were not part of the trusted LaunchAgent job shape. This was not a demonstrated local-to-root path because installed-app/helper identity and root-owned launchd plist proof were already load-bearing, but privileged credential delivery should not mix an exact plist proof with a prefix-shaped live argv proof.FIX R-S11c-19 makes the live LaunchAgent process proof exact-length. src/ipc.rs routes the macOS snapshot peer check through macos_service_owned_server_live_argv_is_expected, which admits only a three-entry process command vector with --server followed by --service-owned-server; extra live arguments fail before the LaunchDaemon returns a root credential snapshot. The existing root-owned LaunchAgent plist parser remains exact over Label, ProgramArguments, RunAtLoad, and KeepAlive. R-S11e-9 subsequently binds the installed-app/helper identity proof to the socket audit token. Regression tests reject extra live argv and extra plist arguments, and verify.sh/apple-conform-check.sh gate the exact live argv helper, its wiring, and both extra-argument tests.
75Unix terminal default-shell command provenance — the non-Windows terminal service selected the executable for an authenticated remote terminal by first reading the process SHELL environment variable, then falling back to fixed shell names and finally returning /bin/sh even when no trusted shell had been proven. In the shipped Linux systemd unit this was not a demonstrated unprivileged-to-root primitive because the service unit does not pass SHELL and an authenticated terminal intentionally grants the owner the service user's shell, but root/service-adjacent command provenance must not depend on ambient environment or an unverified fallback.FIX R-S11c-20 removes SHELL from Unix terminal shell selection. src/server/terminal_service.rs now chooses only fixed absolute Unix shell candidates, canonicalizes the selected path, requires both the candidate/canonical parent directory and the executable to be root-owned and not group/world-writable, requires the executable bit, and fails terminal open if no trusted candidate exists. Linux candidates cover /bin and /usr/bin bash/zsh/sh, macOS uses the system zsh/bash/sh paths, and Android uses /system/bin/sh. Regression tests reject relative and parent-traversal shell paths and prove a trusted absolute candidate in the Linux verifier environment; verify.sh and apple-conform-check.sh reject any reintroduced SHELL, bare shell, or unconditional /bin/sh success fallback.
76Linux pkcheck executable and lifetime provenance — the root service's administrator-authorization helper is itself authority-bearing and must be a fixed trusted executable whose blocking lifetime cannot outlive timeout or shutdown.FIX R-S11e-1/R-S11i bind raw _service_password authorization to the exact socket-derived pid,start-time,uid subject, fixed action, and packaged auth_admin policy. Only fixed absolute PKCHECK_PATH is accepted after clean-path, canonical-parent, root-owner, non-writable, regular-file, and executable checks. The child is polled under a 120-second bound; only a successful exit authorizes, every nonzero exit denies, and timeout, shutdown, or status-query failure kills and reaps it. Missing or untrusted helper state fails closed.
77macOS service server authentication — neither the generic control socket nor raw password socket may derive privileged-helper identity from a pathname or later PID observation.FIX R-S11e-2/R-S11i snapshot uid, effective-pid metadata, and LOCAL_PEERTOKEN from the connected socket before traffic. Security.framework resolves and strictly validates the audit-token-bound live code against the pinned privileged-helper requirement; the resolved path must be the root:wheel, non-writable, executable, non-symlink, ACL-free PrivilegedHelperTools installation. Generic _service carries only no-secret readiness/runtime-snapshot controls; mutation uses raw _service_password after the same proof. No unauthenticated, PID-only, path-only, or subprocess-code-signing fallback exists.
78Linux helper canonical target provenance — Linux root/service-adjacent helper resolvers had removed shell/PATH lookup, but still checked fs::metadata() through fixed candidate paths and returned the original path string to Command::new. If a fixed helper candidate resolved through a symlink chain into a directory writable by an unprivileged local user, a privileged process could verify one target and execute a swapped target. The meaningful impact is installed Linux service mode for helpers such as sudo/env in the root-to-user server launch and any shared loginctl or notification helper used from a privileged process.FIX R-S11e-3 gives Linux fixed-helper resolvers the same authority shape as the trusted pkcheck and terminal-shell paths. App-side helpers in src/platform/linux.rs and shared helpers in libs/hbb_common/src/platform/linux.rs now reject relative or parent-traversal candidates, require the candidate parent directory to be root-owned and not group/world-writable, canonicalize the helper, require the canonical path and canonical parent to remain clean absolute root-owned non-writable state, require the canonical executable to be a root-owned regular file with no group/world write bits and at least one executable bit, and return the canonical PathBuf that is passed to Command::new. Regression tests cover bad path shapes and metadata policy; verify.sh gates canonicalization, candidate/canonical parent checks, executable-bit checks, canonical return wiring, and this requirements/ledger disposition.
80macOS service accept-loop blocking proof ownership — code-signing, filesystem, ACL, helper, and installed-app proof may block, while generic _service and password _service_password admission must remain separately bounded and may not detach proof work after timeout or cancellation.FIX R-S11e-4/R-S11i capture uid, effective-pid metadata, and LOCAL_PEERTOKEN immediately from the accepted socket and reserve independent fixed proof capacity for generic service and password service traffic. Security.framework proof runs on a dedicated exactly owned OS thread, returns through a one-shot result, and is synchronously joined. Timeout, cancellation, panic, lost result, or dropped join ownership is process-fatal; no detach-capable blocking pool owns these proofs. Authorization completes before the generic frame or raw password header/body is read. The no-secret user-paced Authorization Services prompt remains outside the bounded proof worker and between a bounded right-readiness exchange and a fresh raw transport deadline.
81Linux service-owned password receiver proof — polkit authorization alone does not prove that the receiver of the proposed credential is the service-owned child selected by the root service.FIX The root service sends the password only on raw _password after authenticating the service-owned replica from SO_PEERCRED uid/pid, current executable, exact --server --service-owned-server argv, service-parent environment, and live ancestry to the root service. The child independently authenticates the root --service parent before reading the raw body. The same operation ID/value is retained through Prepared/Pending and nonsecret status recovery; missing or stale parent/replica proof fails before password disclosure, and no ordinary main-IPC password write exists.
82Linux service-password endpoint authentication — a pathname-selected local listener must not receive a service-owned password merely because it occupies the expected Unix socket path.FIX R-S11e-6/R-S11i place the password only on raw _service_password. Before sending the canonical header/body, the client proves from SO_PEERCRED that the receiver is uid 0, the current trusted executable, exact --service role, and rooted in non-writable trusted executable/parent metadata. The root listener independently snapshots and proves the active/root caller before body read. Missing, non-root, wrong-executable, wrong-role, writable-path, or socket-squatting receivers receive no password bytes. Generic _service has no password request.
83Voice-call close/reset authority — R-S19 already confined voice-call connect and peer-audio playback to Remote/ViewCamera sessions, but the peer close message and the connection-manager Data::CloseVoiceCall path still called close_voice_call() without first proving that this connection was a Remote/ViewCamera voice-call owner. Separately, ordinary connection teardown reset the global voice-call input device for any session type. A FileTransfer or Terminal peer could not start a call or inject audio, but it could still drive cross-session voice-call UI/input reset state outside its AuthConnType.FIX R-S19 now treats voice-call close/reset as the same typed capability as voice-call start/playback. src/server/connection.rs routes voice-call start, accept/refuse, peer close, CM close, and teardown through connection-owned voice state: can_drive_voice_call() admits only Remote/ViewCamera; close_voice_call() returns false unless the connection is Remote/ViewCamera and has pending or active voice-call state; CM close notifies the peer only after that state closes; and the global input-device reset on teardown runs only for an active voice-call owner. verify.sh gates the helper, Remote/ViewCamera authority, result-bearing close path, state-owned close condition, CM result check, and teardown reset guard.
84Linux FUSE file-content response provenance — Linux file-clipboard FUSE mounted one global response queue per side. FUSE file nodes carried the source conn_id and sent FileContentsRequest back to that connection, but incoming FileContentsResponse messages were queued as bare clipboard messages and matched only by the 32-bit stream_id. A second authenticated file-clipboard-capable session could fill the bounded queue, and a guessed or colliding stream id could satisfy another connection's local paste read. This was not unauthenticated, not remote-to-root, and not unbounded memory growth; it was a cross-session capability/provenance bug in the Linux file-clipboard path.FIX R-S19 now routes Linux FUSE file-content responses through bounded active routes keyed by the connection and fresh read stream that originated the request. FuseServer::read_node generates a fresh stream_id, registers a (conn_id, stream_id) route, and only then sends FileContentsRequest; src/clipboard_file.rs passes the serving conn_id into handle_file_content_response; and the handler dispatches FuseFileContentResponse { conn_id, clip } only to the matching active route. Responses that do not match the fresh active key are dropped before they can occupy another read path. Regression coverage proves bounded route admission, wrong-connection and wrong-stream rejection, stale route removal, duplicate active-route rejection, and a real read_node request/response path where wrong-session replies are ignored before the correct response supplies the bytes; verify.sh gates the keyed router type, caller wiring, fresh per-read stream ids, active-route registration, bounded nonblocking admission, stale global-queue absence, and regression tests.
85Windows MSI deferred install-root provenance — the MSI package rooted App.InstallFolder privately under ProgramFiles6432Folder and removed the browse/public install-folder UI, but deferred no-impersonation runtime-file cleanup still trusted its CustomActionData install-root shape inside the LocalSystem DLL. RemoveRuntimeGeneratedFiles only rejected empty/root strings before deleting the fixed broker name. The package did not expose a normal low-privilege way to supply malformed data, but privileged cleanup must validate the exact root authority it consumes.FIX R-S11d-33 makes the remaining install-root consumer fail closed. CustomActions.cpp normalizes the deferred install folder with GetFullPathNameW, rejects empty/relative/root/path-too-long values, requires an immediate child of FOLDERID_ProgramFiles or FOLDERID_ProgramFilesX86, rejects reparse-backed Program Files/install directories, and supplies only that normalized root to exact runtime-broker cleanup. Service state is declarative and Amyuni cleanup is a self-contained SetupAPI commit action under R-S11f/R-S11e-20; neither consumes custom-action command/root data. verify.sh gates the private Program Files root, native install-folder validation, normalized runtime cleanup, and absence of unrelated install-root consumers.
86Permanent-password PRS read-state authority — the direct listener and CPace auth boundary already failed closed when the stored permanent-password PRS was missing or undecryptable, but the old string accessor collapsed all unavailable states into an empty string. That made an operator-cleared credential, an empty half-state, and an undecryptable machine-UUID-sealed password_prs indistinguishable to status/reporting code, and allowed the direct listener park to look like ordinary no-password state rather than a durable at-rest credential read failure.FIX R-S9 now carries permanent-password PRS reads as typed authority: PermanentPasswordPrsRead::Available, Empty, or UndecryptableStorage. Config::read_permanent_password_prs() is the status source; get_permanent_password_prs() only string-adapts unavailable states for existing CPace callers, where empty still means fail closed before keying. has_permanent_password() keys on PRS availability, not stale config.password. The service-owned runtime snapshot helper preserves the typed status, and direct_server logs undecryptable stored PRS distinctly while keeping the listener parked/dropped until a valid password is provisioned. No fallback is admitted from stale runtime memory, config.password, preset/hard settings, or prior service snapshots. Regression tests cover decryptable, empty, half-state, and undecryptable storage, and verify.sh gates the typed enum, undecryptable branch, server status helper, direct-listener log, and absence of a silent unwrap_or_default() collapse in the PRS string accessor.
87AV1/libaom runtime quarantine — current public aom advisories are not a proven viewer-decoder-only issue; they localize to libaom encoder/control surfaces, while the old fork still exposed AV1 as a selectable, advertiseable, negotiable runtime codec. A password-correct hostile peer could still try to send Av1s frames, and stale local preferences/UI/server config could still steer the app toward AV1 unless the policy was enforced at every codec boundary.FIX Appendix C #2b remains open for the other native viewer decode paths, but AV1/libaom itself is no longer a runtime codec. scrap::codec advertises no AV1 encode/decode capability, ignores stale AV1 preferences by falling back to the normal software policy, creates an invalid decoder for CodecFormat::AV1, and fails inbound Av1s before any native decoder path. The receive loop treats AV1 as locally unsupported before creating a decoder/recorder worker. The server encoder config maps stale AV1 requests to VP9, the Flutter desktop/mobile/toolbar codec UI removes AV1, the FFI codec menu JSON omits the av1 key, codec-preference no longer accepts av1, and the old test_av1/av1-test startup benchmark path is deleted. verify.sh gates the policy function, capability tests, decoder-disabled test, UI/session/config absence, server VP9 fallback, benchmark/probe deletion, and this requirements/status/watch disposition.
88AV1/libaom dependency removal — runtime quarantine was necessary but incomplete while the native library, FFI bindings, overlay port, and offline build paths still carried aom. A future refactor, build Dockerfile, or stale build script could have reintroduced a linked libaom path without changing user-visible UI.EXCISE AV1 remains only a protocol/wire enum. The aom entries are deleted from vcpkg.json; res/vcpkg/aom, libs/scrap/src/common/aom.rs, and aom_ffi.h are deleted; libs/scrap/build.rs no longer generates aom_ffi.rs; EncoderCfg has no AV1/libaom variant; Linux/Android/Windows/Apple/dev-check scripts, README build commands, build Dockerfiles, and tracked build scaffolds do not install, stub, or reference aom; and scripts/native-codec-watch.sh now watches only the retained vcpkg package set while recording aom as retired. verify.sh fails if a source module, FFI binding, bindgen package, overlay path, manifest entry, build Dockerfile, build scaffold, or ledger record reintroduces libaom.
90User-owned permanent-password receiver authentication — a same-UID socket or same-session pipe squatter must not receive a proposed password or impersonate the daemon.FIX User-owned mutation uses only raw _password. Unix proves matching uid, current executable, and exact user-owned --server role on both sides before body transfer. Windows proves the first-instance server's exact executable, finite role, generation, and process token before sending, and the server proves the client before reading the body. Installed-service routing remains preferred when present. Recovery reuses the same UUID/value and may query only nonsecret status on ordinary IPC after admission; no ordinary main-IPC password request remains.
91macOS service-owned password Authorization Services right ordering — macOS service-owned password changes used the custom com.carriez.RustDesk.set-unattended-password right, but the UI created the external authorization form before the trusted helper had necessarily normalized that right. On a fresh authdb, the UI-side existence check could fail before the helper received the request that would create the right. With a stale or weaker preexisting right, the authorization prompt could be acquired against that stale definition before daemon-side verification updated the database. This was a first-use/policy-definition correctness gap, not a proven password-write bypass from source alone, because the daemon still verified the external form noninteractively before writing the root credential.FIX R-S11e-8 makes the trusted root helper own right normalization before the UI prompts. After authenticated connect_service, the macOS client sends a no-secret MacosServiceOwnedPasswordRightReadyRequest over _service; the helper calls the existing right setup path and returns MacosServiceOwnedPasswordRightReadyResult(bool). The client calls service_owned_unattended_password_authorization() only after that readiness result is true and sends the password only after authorization succeeds. The native creator no longer accepts existence-only state: MacCreateServiceOwnedUnattendedPasswordAuthorizationExternalForm requires RustDeskSetUnattendedPasswordRightMatchesExpected, which reads the Authorization Services right definition and validates class=user, group=admin, shared=false, allow-root=false, authenticate-user=true, session-owner=false, extract-password=false, and timeout=0. verify.sh and apple-conform-check.sh gate the service-only readiness message, readiness-before-authorization ordering, exact native right-definition validation, absence of the old existence-only helper, and this requirements/ledger disposition.
92macOS service audit-token peer code identity — code authority for a connected Unix-domain peer is the socket-bound audit token; pid and path are metadata and secondary location checks.FIX R-S11e-9/R-S11i capture getpeereid uid, LOCAL_PEEREPID metadata, and LOCAL_PEERTOKEN immediately. Security.framework resolves live peer code from kSecGuestAttributeAudit, strictly validates pinned app/helper requirements, and uses SecCodeCopyPath only for installed-location, owner, mode, symlink, and ACL proof. The same snapshot model backs generic `_service`, raw `_service_password`, client-side helper proof, receiver admission, and the service-owned LaunchAgent runtime-snapshot requester. Exactly owned proof threads are synchronously joined and fail the process on lost ownership or deadline. No PID-only, path-only, or subprocess-code-signing fallback exists.
93macOS residual process launch provenance — the connected-session wake path still spawned /usr/bin/caffeinate -u -t 5 after CPace keying, and the macOS root-to-user-bootstrap helper built launchctl asuser <uid> /usr/bin/env KEY=VALUE ... <current_exe>. Neither path gave a peer arbitrary executable or shell text, and the installed macOS service-owned server is the LaunchAgent user process rather than the root LaunchDaemon. The residual was correctness/provenance: a remote-authenticated connection should not create a helper process just to declare user activity, and a root-capable bootstrap launcher should not carry generic expansion or credential-transition ambiguity.FIX R-S11e-10 first replaced the wake subprocess with the native IOKit MacDeclareRemoteUserActivity bridge and removed the /usr/bin/env argv bridge. Later contract review in R-S11x/R-S11e-38 corrected the remaining overclaim: launchctl asuser changes bootstrap/audit context but does not change UID/GID, so an environment-key allowlist did not make it a root-to-user credential transition. The generic launcher is now deleted entirely; an unexpected root server fails before starting CM/whiteboard, and the per-user LaunchAgent owns the user-context server. The macOS build links IOKit explicitly, and source/Apple/mutation gates bind both subprocess deletions, the fail-closed topology, and this superseding disposition.
94Windows service-owned password receiver and namespace proof — an elevated requester and a LocalSystem receiver are insufficient unless the exact process roles, active principal, retained pipe namespace, and operation delivery are bound together.FIX Mutation terminates at the stable LocalSystem SCM service on raw _service_password. The first-instance, local-only, max-instances-one message pipe is retained and serially reused for process life. The client proves the fixed service image, LocalSystem token, exact service role, and process generation before sending. The server preauthorizes exact active-principal RustDesk roles before header wait, impersonates the header sender before body read, revalidates executable/generation/process token/last-message token and a session-before/between/after two-token principal snapshot before admission, re-reads the kernel DACL with GetSecurityInfo, and requires an operation-bound status ACK before disconnect. The service-owned child receives only a generation-bound read-only replica over _service_credential; it is not the durable writer.
95Desktop URL IPC raw deep-link material handoff — the desktop uni-links listener's non-Flutter forwarding branch sent uri.toString() into bind.sendUrlScheme and then Rust _url IPC when a connection-manager or secondary process forwarded an OS-delivered rustdesk:// link to the main process. The parser later stripped ?password=, ?key=, and relay syntax before connect, but the raw URL had already crossed local IPC, contradicting R-X6's rule that embedded credential/trust-anchor material is never carried through handoff. This was local same-user IPC disclosure and authority hygiene, not remote reachability or root privilege escalation.FIX R-X6/R-S11c-9 now canonicalizes the desktop URL IPC payload before forwarding. listenUniLinks(handleByFlutter: false) calls urlLinkToForwardUrl, which reuses urlLinkToCmdArgs, rejects config/password write authorities and relay syntax, accepts only the supported connect authorities, and renders a fresh address-only rustdesk://<authority>/<id> URL for bind.sendUrlScheme. The raw uri.toString() forwarding shape is absent, so desktop _url IPC never carries embedded password/key/relay query material. verify.sh gates the helper, listener wiring, address-only renderer, and raw-forwarding absence.
97macOS clipboard-file paste no-follow finalize — the macOS CLIPRDR file-paste path already sanitized hostile-peer FILEDESCRIPTOR names and bound FileContentsResponse frames to the originating connection, but its local paste worker still created directories/files, progress xattrs, cancellation cleanup, final rename, and final metadata by re-resolving paths under the Finder-observed paste directory. The normal installed macOS server is a user LaunchAgent, so this was not a hidden remote-to-root path; it was a peer-controlled filesystem-write correctness gap adjacent to R-S8/R-A5 where local symlink/path races could be followed by the paste worker instead of being rejected.FIX R-S11e-12 makes macOS clipboard-file paste use handle-relative no-follow filesystem operations. paste_task.rs opens the paste target directory with an openat(O_DIRECTORY|O_NOFOLLOW) component walk, keeps that directory handle as the paste anchor, creates missing peer-requested parent directories through mkdirat plus no-follow re-open, reserves zero-size and .rddownload files with openat(O_CREAT|O_EXCL|O_NOFOLLOW), stores only relative download state for later cleanup, updates/removes Finder progress xattrs with fsetxattr/fremovexattr on the open file descriptor, unlinks cancelled temp files with unlinkat, and finalizes completed downloads with renameatx_np(..., RENAME_EXCL) while retrying numbered final names on real name collisions. The old path-based File::create, create_dir_all, std::fs::rename, path xattr, path metadata reopen, path remove, and masked initial update_next(0).ok() shapes are absent. verify.sh and apple-conform-check.sh gate the fd-relative primitives, no-follow/exclusive flags, requirements/ledger disposition, and absence of the deleted path-based write fallback.
98macOS clipboard-file paste placeholder temp authority — the macOS file-paste placeholder URL path still created pasteboard placeholder files directly as global /tmp/.rustdesk_* names, counted all matching names in /tmp, and cleaned the source placeholder by path. The installed macOS controlled side normally runs as the console user's LaunchAgent, so this was not a hidden remote-to-root path; it was local same-user pasteboard/temp authority ambiguity adjacent to R-S11e-12, where another local process could pre-shape or flood the global placeholder namespace and where the cleanup authority did not match the creation authority.FIX R-S11e-13 makes pasteboard placeholder state private to the pasteboard context. pasteboard_context.rs creates a unique rustdesk-clipboard-<euid>-<uuid> directory under the current user's temporary directory with mkdir(0700), opens it with O_DIRECTORY|O_NOFOLLOW|O_CLOEXEC, normalizes/verifies owner-only mode and euid ownership through fchmod/fstat, and keeps the directory handle as the placeholder authority. item_data_provider.rs creates placeholder files only through that handle using openat(O_CREAT|O_EXCL|O_NOFOLLOW|O_CLOEXEC) mode 0600; placeholder counting is scoped to the private directory and fails closed on read errors; source cleanup uses unlinkat through the same handle; and the observer callback captures the private authority instead of relying on a global function pointer. The old global /tmp/.rustdesk_* creation/counting model, source-placeholder path cleanup, and path-create fallback are absent. verify.sh and apple-conform-check.sh retain the touched Apple clipboard sources and gate these invariants.
99Linux root/headless FileTransfer owner authority — follow-up review confirmed that, where the Linux controlled --server or its connection manager runs as root/service-owned in root, login-screen, or headless service modes, authenticated FileTransfer filesystem operations execute with that process authority. This is not an ordinary local-to-root IPC bug or remote unauthenticated escalation: the peer must complete CPace first, local CM file authority is bound to the server-validated FileTransfer connection token, and R-S8 already defines FileTransfer as the authenticated owner's single unconfined full-filesystem mode. Denying, demoting, or directory-confining this path would contradict R-S8/R-F1/R-F2 unless those requirements are deliberately changed.ACCEPTED / GATED R-S11e-14 records the intended authority model rather than adding a runtime restriction. The code keeps FileTransfer admitted after the local file-transfer permission gate, classifies it as AuthConnType::FileTransfer, grants CM file authority only for server-validated FileTransfer sessions, and confines FileTransfer to clipboard+file by clearing input, block-input, privacy, restart, recording, and host-audio capability. Normal active-desktop Linux service mode still launches the serving child as the active user; root/headless service modes keep FileTransfer at the serving process credential by design. verify.sh gates the R-S8 no-confinement requirement, this ledger row, the FileTransfer login shape, CM file-authority type binding, Unix headless username/refusal guards, and the FileTransfer capability confinement set.
101macOS hardened-runtime JIT entitlement minimization — macOS source conformance kept a single DebugProfile.entitlements file for both Debug and Profile and also granted com.apple.security.cs.allow-jit to the shipped Release.entitlements. Apple defines that hardened-runtime entitlement as permission to create writable/executable MAP_JIT memory, while Flutter Profile and Release builds are AOT. Carrying the JIT exception outside Debug was therefore an unnecessary executable-memory entitlement on retained macOS source.FIX R-S14 splits the macOS entitlements by build configuration. Debug.entitlements is the only file with com.apple.security.cs.allow-jit; Profile.entitlements keeps the profiler network-server entitlement but no JIT exception; Release.entitlements keeps only App Sandbox off, audio input, and network client. The Xcode project points each configuration to its own entitlement file, the old combined DebugProfile.entitlements is deleted, and apple-conform-check.sh exact-matches all three macOS entitlement maps plus the empty iOS entitlement map.
102Android service-destroy capture-resource teardown — the first Android F5 fix stopped and cleared MediaProjection on service destruction, but MainService.onDestroy() still did not run the same capture-resource cleanup as explicit app Stop. A system/OEM service-destroy path could therefore skip explicit ImageReader.close(), Surface.release(), and retained VirtualDisplay.release(), relying on process death or projection invalidation rather than the foreground service owning every capture resource it created.FIX R-S14/R-D7a now routes both MainService.onDestroy() and explicit destroy() through releaseCaptureResources(). That sink calls stopCapture() to close the ImageReader, release/null the Surface, stop raw video/audio state, then releases the retained reusable VirtualDisplay and stops/clears MediaProjection. stopCapture() nulls the released Surface, making the shared teardown idempotent when explicit Stop later reaches onDestroy(). verify.sh gates the shared lifecycle sink, both callers, the virtual-display/projection release chain, the nulled surface, and the retained START_NOT_STICKY restart barrier.
103Linux Flutter runner Rust core library load provenance — the Linux Flutter runner used dlopen("librustdesk.so") to load the Rust core. A no-slash dlopen target is resolved by the dynamic loader's library search order, including LD_LIBRARY_PATH for non-secure-execution processes, before the bundled RUNPATH. The default systemd unit did not set LD_LIBRARY_PATH, so this was not promoted to a proven default local-user-to-root path, but the installed root-capable runner must bind its core library to the Debian bundle it is executing rather than ambient loader state.FIX R-S11c-10s makes the runner load $ORIGIN/lib/librustdesk.so, matching the installed bundle layout and the existing $ORIGIN/lib RPATH. It uses immediate local binding, exits nonzero when the bundled core or rustdesk_core_main is missing, and deletes the legacy load-failure helper that probed PATH for package managers. verify.sh gates the executable-relative core path, fail-closed startup, retained bundle RPATH, absence of the bare dlopen("librustdesk.so") shape, and absence of the package-manager/PATH advice path.
104Android final APK manifest authority — the source manifest hardening was insufficient because Android's manifest merger can add library declarations to the single manifest packaged in the APK. The existing signed artifact demonstrated the gap: file_picker merged READ_EXTERNAL_STORAGE, AndroidX ProfileInstaller merged an exported ProfileInstallReceiver protected by android.permission.DUMP, and two RustDesk-internal no-filter components relied on implicit default non-export rather than explicit policy. This was not a proven ordinary-app-to-root path, but it contradicted the fork's Android authority model: only the launcher/deep-link activity should be externally launchable, forbidden permissions must not reappear through library merge, and shipped package authority is the final APK manifest, not source XML alone.FIX R-X6/R-S14 now makes the Android manifest policy artifact-backed. The app manifest declares tools:node="remove" for merged READ_EXTERNAL_STORAGE, WRITE_EXTERNAL_STORAGE, SYSTEM_ALERT_WINDOW, and androidx.profileinstaller.ProfileInstallReceiver; PermissionRequestTransparentActivity and MainService are explicitly android:exported="false". scripts/build-android.sh runs scripts/verify-android-apk-manifest.py after apksigner verify and before hashing the signed APK. That verifier parses the final APK with the pinned SDK aapt2 dump xmltree, requires package/minSdk/targetSdk and allowBackup=false, rejects forbidden or unknown final permissions, requires the exact component inventory, allows only MainActivity exported with launcher and rustdesk deep-link filters, requires every service/receiver/provider/internal activity to be explicitly non-exported, and fails if ProfileInstaller or any unexpected component returns. verify.sh gates the source removal markers, explicit internal export flags, helper syntax, build-script invocation, and component/permission policy strings.
105Android legacy JAR-signature META-INF authority — the Android release APK still declared minSdk 22, so Android 5.x/6.x installs relied on v1/JAR signing while the APK necessarily carried runtime Java resources under META-INF/, including R8-shrunk kotlinx.coroutines service-loader entries. apksigner verify --verbose warned that those entries were not protected by the JAR signature; local mutation confirmed an API 22/23 verification range still accepted a modified META-INF/services entry, while API 24+ rejected the same APK through v2/v3 whole-APK verification. Removing the service-loader files would change coroutine runtime behavior, so the wrong compatibility promise was the API 22/23 support floor.FIX R-X6/R-S14 retires the v1/JAR authority path. Android support now starts at minSdk 24; the release signer passes --min-sdk-version "$ANDROID_MIN_SDK", explicitly disables --v1-signing-enabled, explicitly enables v2 and v3 signing, and verifies with apksigner verify -Werr --min-sdk-version "$ANDROID_MIN_SDK" --verbose. The build asserts v1 is false, v2 and v3 are true, and no not protected by signature warning is present before hashing or double-build comparison. scripts/verify-android-apk-manifest.py requires the final manifest minSdkVersion to be 24, and verify.sh gates the pin, Gradle value, manifest verifier, signing flags, warning-as-error verifier, and this disposition. Runtime META-INF resources remain only on platforms where v2/v3 protect the APK as a whole; Android 5.x/6.x compatibility is intentionally dropped.
106Linux pkcheck request-time peer identity binding — Linux service-owned password changes use race-resistant pkcheck --process pid,start-time,uid, and all three subject fields must remain bound to the caller of the raw _service_password socket rather than split observations made after accept. The older split shape was not promoted to a proven default local-to-root path because it still required the active-user/root uid gate, same-executable proof, and polkit auth_admin; it was nevertheless the wrong authority shape for a privileged mechanism authorizing an untrusted IPC subject.FIX R-S11e-15/R-S11i derive the pkcheck subject from the same live raw-socket peer identity proof used by admission. linux_polkit_subject_for_peer revalidates the SO_PEERCRED pid/uid, current executable, live /proc uid, and process start time used for pid,start-time,uid. The old direct handler-side linux_proc_start_time(peer_pid) assembly is absent. verify.sh gates the live-identity subject, start-time accessor, requirements/ledger disposition, and absence of the split-observation regression.
107Windows CM non-file clipboard helper authority — in installed/root Windows server mode, the clipboard service connected to the authenticated _cm endpoint and sent a bare Data::ClipboardNonFile(None). Endpoint proof established that the caller was the launched RustDesk server/helper process, but it did not prove that any active Remote connection currently held clipboard-read authority. A helper peer with process-level CM proof could therefore ask CM to read the interactive user's text/image clipboard through check_clipboard_cm() without carrying the per-connection conn_id, AuthConnType, or cm_auth_token that protects CM file operations.FIX R-S11c-22 makes non-file clipboard reads a typed connection-bound CM capability. The Windows clipboard service now chooses a subscribed connection's CmClipboardAuthority lease and sends Data::AuthorizedClipboardNonFile { id, conn_type, cm_auth_token }; Data::ClipboardNonFile remains response-only and a bare None request is rejected. CM validates the tuple through the main server with ValidateCmConnection before calling check_clipboard_cm(), and the server registry records a live cm_clipboard bit that is Remote-only, requires the same can_sub_clipboard_service() capability used for subscription, and is refreshed when peer clipboard/keyboard disables change. FileTransfer/ViewCamera/Terminal/PortForward, stale-token, wrong-type, no-subscriber, disabled, and endpoint-proof-only requests do not read the desktop clipboard. verify.sh gates the authorized request variant, Remote-only clipboard authority, subscriber lease, live registry bit, validation-before-read ordering, bare-request rejection, and absence of ClipboardNonFile(None) sends.
108Linux Debian package tree authority — Debian package construction is a privilege boundary because the archive installs a root service executable, service unit, polkit policy, configuration helpers, and maintainer scripts. Plain dpkg-deb -b preserved staging uid/gid, so a direct non-root build could install builder-owned service payloads. Mode-preserving control-file copies and ordinary payload copies also made the package depend on checkout and process modes: a private release snapshot created under umask 077 could stage maintainer scripts as 0700 and public package data as 0600/0700. Git records only the executable bit, and dpkg-deb --root-owner-group changes archive ownership rather than file permissions, so neither mechanism establishes a usable or canonical installed tree. Multiple constructors and reuse of an uncleared Flutter output could also admit divergent or stale payloads. Finally, the editable /etc/rustdesk/startwm.sh and xorg.conf files lacked Debian conffile declarations, so local administrator changes had ordinary-file upgrade/removal semantics.FIX R-S11c-10t gives Debian packaging one supported Flutter constructor and one exact finalization boundary. The parser-only --package and non-Flutter cargo-bundle/repackaging paths are absent. The Linux Flutter output is removed before each build; the emitted bundle must have exact data/lib/rustdesk structure, the exact pinned runtime-library set, fixed package resources, and no unlisted data path outside Flutter's generated flutter_assets subtree. The producer and independent artifact verifier each enumerate that runtime-library set explicitly. Ignored Flutter plugin registrants and generated CMake metadata are regenerated build mechanics, not source authority; dependency or plugin-output drift must fail against the actual staged bundle and emitted .deb. Maintainer scripts are byte-copied from an exact four-file source inventory rather than mode-preserved. The exact control inventory is control, conffiles, md5sums, and the four scripts; conffiles declares both editable /etc/rustdesk files, while md5sums exactly covers every non-conffile data file. The finalizer rejects unexpected entries, nested control directories, links, special files, and hardlinked regular files, then requires every directory to be 0755, the runner and startwm.sh to be 0755, every other data file and ordinary control file to be 0644, and all maintainer scripts to be 0755. One checked argv helper runs dpkg-deb --root-owner-group -b. Its AST gate admits only that exact archiver and the existing exact PE canonicalizer subprocess, resolves concatenated, joined, and interpolated constant strings before rejecting alternate dpkg-deb commands, pins the complete top-level import inventory, rejects every decorator and unsupported process-launch member including re-exported subprocess paths, and requires each package authority to be one synchronous top-level function whose loaded name and code origin match the checked definition. It rejects process/module/package callable aliases, direct stores, dynamic namespace/evaluation APIs, sys.modules, and function/module/frame namespace reach, pins the sole shell-wrapper and FFI-helper bodies, requires the exact four-function shell-call ownership inventory and exact 21-operation Flutter pre-finalization program, requires staging/finalization/archive as contiguous reachable direct operations, and permits only the exact cleanup/versioned-rename/chdir publication tail after archiving. Comments, hidden or dead alternate shell calls, explicit early return/raise/termination, intervening post-finalizer mutation, and historical command spellings are not proof. The independent artifact verifier checks every tar member's numeric root ownership, canonical root/root owner names, exact raw regular-file or directory typeflag, mode, and link absence, plus exact data/control/conffile/md5 inventories. It reads each raw tar header and requires UTF-8 names, the canonical ./ prefix, exactly one trailing slash for directories and none for regular files, empty regular/directory link names, zero-only padding in the name, linkname, uname, gname, and prefix fields after the first NUL, no extended path metadata, no parser-normalized alias, and one unique normalized name. Its source fixture is assembled from tracked inputs, succeeds without generated plugin metadata, and is invariant under stale or malformed ignored metadata. Its production fixture invokes the real finalizer under umask 077 with private inputs. One-sided producer library removal or renaming, finalizer-level missing or extra plugin libraries, and archive-level missing, extra, substituted, or non-ELF plugin libraries are explicit negatives. Handcrafted malformed packages and direct parser mutations prove rejection of wrong control/data modes, extra, missing, nested, or special members, regular-file stowaways, links, alternate regular/contiguous/sparse raw types, noncanonical raw names and textual padding, non-ELF or wrong-identity runtime objects, malformed/duplicate/missing/extra md5 entries, malformed conffiles, and invalid, empty, multiple, or legacy ELF search paths. Source fixtures reject hardlinked and individually symlinked maintainer scripts, a symlinked script directory, stale data, decorated constructor replacement, direct OS-process imports, byte-valued spawn/exec and re-exported subprocess launches, literal/concatenated/joined/interpolated-f-string/percent-formatted/aliased archive commands, early termination, direct or dynamic authority rebinding, shell/helper/constructor-program drift, intervening package mutation, and reordered package operations. scripts/build-debian.sh validates the emitted archive and locale-independent exact maintainer-script metadata before hashing it; verify.sh runs the source, production-behavior, and mutation gates.
109Windows Flutter runner Rust core DLL load provenance — the Windows Flutter runner loaded the Rust core with bare LoadLibraryA("librustdesk.dll") before Rust-side service, IPC, or policy checks ran. Windows normally searches the application directory first, so this was not promoted to a proven default standard-user-to-SYSTEM path for the installed Program Files service bundle, but a root-capable service executable must not delegate its core module selection to the ambient DLL search order if the bundled DLL is missing, moved, or the process search path is changed.FIX R-S11c-23 makes the Windows runner bind the Rust core to the executable bundle. wWinMain now resolves the current executable path with GetModuleFileNameW(nullptr, ...), constructs the sibling absolute librustdesk.dll path, and loads it with LoadLibraryExW using LOAD_LIBRARY_SEARCH_DLL_LOAD_DIR, LOAD_LIBRARY_SEARCH_APPLICATION_DIR, and LOAD_LIBRARY_SEARCH_SYSTEM32. Startup fails closed if the executable directory cannot be resolved or the bundled core cannot be loaded. verify.sh gates the executable-relative path, restricted load flags, absence of the bare DLL load, and this disposition.
110Linux XDO libxdo dynamic-library provenance — the patched libxdo-sys wrapper kept RustDesk runnable on systems without libxdo, but its Linux input backend tried bare names libxdo.so.4, libxdo.so.3, and libxdo.so through libloading::Library::new. In active-desktop service mode that backend normally runs as the active user, but in root/headless service-owned server mode it is reachable from the root --server input path. The default systemd unit does not set attacker-controlled loader variables and default system library directories should not be user-writable, so this was not promoted to a proven default local-user-to-root exploit; it was still the wrong authority shape for service-reachable native code loading.FIX R-S11c-10u makes the XDO backend load only fixed absolute system-library candidates for versioned libxdo.so.4 or libxdo.so.3. Before calling Library::open, the resolver requires the candidate path, candidate directory chain, canonical target path, canonical target directory chain, and target file to be absolute, root-owned, and not group/world-writable; the target must be a regular file. Protected distro symlinks are accepted only when their canonical target is also protected, while /tmp, /usr/local, relative paths, unversioned libxdo.so, writable parents, writable files, shell helpers, ldconfig, pkg-config, and ambient name search are absent. The library is opened with immediate local binding, and missing or untrusted libxdo leaves XDO functions disabled rather than falling back. verify.sh runs the focused libxdo-sys trust tests and source-gates the fixed candidates, ownership/mode predicates, protected symlink handling, absence of the old bare-name loader, and this disposition.
111Windows service-owned server child executable provenance — a service-owned --server --service-owned-server child must be the fixed Program Files service executable installed by the MSI, not whichever image happens to be running as the service.FIX R-S11d-37 makes the child image a fixed-root runtime invariant. The service opens fixed and running directories/executables no-follow, rejects reparse-backed objects, proves Win32 handle identity, and launches only the fixed image with exact role and explicit current directory. Entry refuses the marker unless LocalSystem and the same fixed-root proof succeed. The raw `_service_password` client proves the fixed LocalSystem service image and exact service role before sending; `_service_credential` replica traffic is bound to the exact retained child generation. No password-bearing main receiver remains.
112Desktop Dart FFI Rust core library provenance — after the native Linux and Windows Flutter runners were hardened to preload the Rust core from executable-relative bundle paths, flutter/lib/models/native_model.dart still reopened the FFI binding with bare DynamicLibrary.open("librustdesk.so") on Linux and bare DynamicLibrary.open("librustdesk.dll") on Windows. The Dart FFI layer is normally reached after the runner's safe preload, and this was not promoted to a proven default local-to-root path, but a privileged-capable desktop process must not leave its second Rust-core handle selection to platform library search semantics.FIX R-S11c-24 makes desktop Dart FFI follow the same bundle authority as the native runners. Linux resolves Platform.resolvedExecutable, takes its parent directory, and opens lib/librustdesk.so; Windows opens sibling librustdesk.dll. Android keeps the packaged librustdesk.so loader name, and macOS keeps DynamicLibrary.process() to avoid duplicate Rust global state. verify.sh gates the executable-dir helper, resolved-executable use, Linux/Windows executable-relative paths, Android/macOS exceptions, init helper use, and absence of bare desktop librustdesk opens.
113Windows inactive RustDesk IDD loader excision — after the active Windows virtual-display implementation was pinned to Amyuni and --install-idd was made reject-only, the inactive RustDesk IDD implementation still remained in source, workspace metadata, the lockfile, Windows build scripts, and Flutter peer/UI compatibility code.
