The libs/virtual_display and libs/virtual_display/dylib crates are removed from the workspace and lockfile, the Windows build no longer builds or copies dylib_virtual_display.dll, virtual_display_manager owns the small MonitorMode shape and dispatches only to Amyuni, and the client/UI recognizes only amyuni_idd for virtual-display controls. verify.sh gates absence of the deleted crate directory, dependency/workspace/lockfile/build artifacts, legacy RustDesk IDD platform-addition/UI names, and this requirements/ledger disposition.
114Obsolete generated Docker build helper — build.py::generate_build_script_for_docker() wrote, chmodded, and executed fixed /tmp/build.sh, and cloned Flutter Rust Bridge into fixed /tmp/flutter_rust_bridge. An elevated or shared-host invocation would have followed or acted on attacker-controlled public temporary names. The function was not reachable from the parser, had no caller or supported workflow, and was therefore not promoted to a shipped-runtime local privilege escalation. It also downloaded an obsolete Flutter archive and shallow-cloned mutable Flutter Rust Bridge and vcpkg repositories outside the pinned offline build inputs.FIX R-S11c-10v deletes the generated Docker build helper rather than preserving an unreachable, non-reproducible bootstrap mode behind safer temporary-file mechanics. build.py no longer contains the helper, fixed public temporary paths, obsolete Flutter archive, mutable Flutter Rust Bridge clone, or mutable vcpkg clone. Supported builds continue through the repository's pinned shell-script facilities. verify.sh gates the deleted helper and bootstrap inputs plus this requirements/ledger disposition.
115Verifier private scratch workspace authority — the supported host-side scripts/verify.sh, also run as the first scripts/verify-release.sh source gate, redirected checker output through 65 predictable /tmp/rd_verify_*.$$, /tmp/r_s11b3_*.$$, and /tmp/r_s11c23_hits.txt names. Shell redirection follows a preexisting symlink before the checked command executes, so an elevated verifier invocation on a shared host could truncate or write an attacker-selected file. No repository caller elevates the verifier, so this was not promoted to a default build-host or shipped-runtime local privilege escalation.FIX R-S11c-10w gives the verifier one private scratch authority. It creates /tmp/rustdesk-verify.XXXXXXXXXX with checked mktemp -d under umask 077, marks the resulting path variable readonly, verifies current-UID ownership and mode 0700, and redirects every generated checker output beneath that directory. A single EXIT handler preserves the verifier result, treats cleanup failure as failure, and works with nonzero HUP/INT/TERM exits; the old fixed/PID-suffixed names and scattered per-file cleanup branches are absent. A separate structural verifier proves the exact full-line create, cleanup, signal, and metadata blocks without placing its assertion literals in the input being checked; its mutation-negative self-test removes every required real line in memory and requires rejection. It also rejects fixed-string self-inspection, old scratch prefixes, and unquoted, single-quoted, or double-quoted direct public-/tmp redirections. The Apple checker is tracked separately.
116Apple checker private host scratch authority — the supported host-side scripts/apple-conform-check.sh, also invoked by scripts/verify-release.sh, redirected seven fixed/PID-suffixed diagnostics and its per-target cross-check log through public /tmp. An elevated invocation on a shared host could follow attacker-created symlinks during shell redirection. No tracked caller elevates either checker, so this was not promoted to a default build-host or shipped-runtime local privilege escalation. It creates /tmp/rustdesk-apple-check.XXXXXXXXXX with checked mktemp -d under umask 077, marks the path variable readonly, verifies by lstat that it is a current-UID mode-0700 directory, and redirects every host diagnostic and target log beneath it. A single EXIT handler preserves prior status, treats cleanup failure as failure, and runs after nonzero HUP/INT/TERM exits. The old public host paths and scattered per-file cleanup branches are absent, the unnecessary container-local rustfmt diagnostic file is replaced by in-memory stderr capture, and required container-private fixture paths remain in their container namespace. Anchored source-shape assertions prevent the gate from satisfying itself and reject old host prefixes or any direct public-temp redirection.
117Windows obsolete updater temporary-file authority — normal Windows GUI startup still called try_remove_temp_update_files() after the supported self-updater had been excised. The function enumerated the process temporary directory and deleted every file older than one hour whose name matched rustdesk-*.msi or rustdesk-*.exe, without proving a producer, owner, installation transaction, or file identity. A privileged-capable process therefore retained ambient filesystem deletion authority over installer-shaped names it did not own. No supported updater producer or consumer remained, and remove_file did not establish a default symlink-to-target arbitrary deletion path, so this was not promoted to a confirmed default local-to-SYSTEM exploit.FIX R-S11d-39 deletes the obsolete authority rather than inventing ownership for a removed feature. Normal startup no longer enumerates or removes temporary installer-shaped files. The uncalled cross-platform download-file-<version> FFI asset-name query, its updater-only Windows MSI registry probe, the dead Flutter updateUrl state, and the four exclusively owned translation keys are deleted with the sweep. Live peer-compatibility wording such as Download new version remains because it is not updater authority. verify.sh gates absence of the sweep, filename query, MSI probe, Dart state, and dead language entries plus this requirements/ledger disposition; dart-verify.sh independently gates the Flutter state and strings.

Honest exceptions are explicit, not accidental. The at-rest PRS is accepted, not fixed, by the §2 threat model: it is a memory-hard Argon2id value, connect-equivalent but not the plaintext password (#14). File transfer retains intended full-filesystem authority while descriptor-based no-follow writes close its TOCTOU class (#10/#99). The password replay ledger is bounded and process-lifetime: at capacity it evicts only the oldest terminal replay result and never live admitted work; process restart may repeat the same idempotent set-to-value operation, so it is not described as durable exactly-once. Windows SendSAS is void, so the service reports dispatch accepted rather than observed secure-desktop activation. Local IPC is structurally gated by closed endpoint protocols, receiver authority, caps/deadlines/budgets, owned task drain, typed finality, and R-A6 reachability checks. Everything else is excised, logic-fixed and tested, explicitly accepted, or covered by pinned advisory gates.

#Finding / exposureDisposition in this fork
118Windows terminal service principal authority — an authenticated remote Terminal login selected its shell principal from uninstall-registry-derived is_installed() state. The fixed-root service-owned --server --service-owned-server child can be positively running as LocalSystem while a stale legacy uninstall key makes that compatibility query false. That selected SelfUser/None; terminal_service then used token presence as its only role discriminator and opened the PTY directly in the current LocalSystem process. Persistent services recorded only is_specified_user: bool, accepted simultaneous same-class attachments, and reacquired entries by bare service ID, so another Windows session/logon principal could pass the boolean check and stale cleanup could remove a replacement entry. Service creation errors were ignored, and the attempted token repair used DuplicateToken, which creates an impersonation token rather than the primary token required by CreateProcessAsUser. The network trigger required the permanent password and an authenticated Terminal session, but this still violated the declared Windows serving-user contract by turning remote Terminal into a LocalSystem shell under supported compatibility metadata state. The same audit found lifecycle faults around that authority: synchronous PTY/helper construction blocked the Tokio connection task; revocation did not cancel an in-flight open; a new session became output-visible before its TerminalOpened response; delayed close responses used terminal-ID absence and could pass after a second open/close generation; output polling drained a concurrently refillable channel without a work or memory ceiling while holding the session mutex; revocation and connection destruction created teardown threads on demand and could lose ownership or block when spawning failed; detached dead helpers remained reconnectable; helper pipe rights used generic write and therefore included pipe-instance authority; one-shot CancelSynchronousIo could miss a subsequent synchronous read and block join; and helper/shell failures were converted into process exit code zero.FIX R-S11c-25 derives terminal authority from the positively proved process role, never installation metadata. A service-owned LocalSystem server derives the served session from its own process token, then must obtain that session user's primary token with WTSQueryUserToken or reject Terminal before authorization succeeds. A direct non-LocalSystem server uses only its process owner; every LocalSystem/service-owned no-token direct PTY is rejected again at the service sink, and the terminal helper refuses LocalSystem execution. The reconnect principal is token session ID + user SID + TOKEN_STATISTICS.AuthenticationId. The persistent entry separately owns the original primary token through a non-Copy RAII handle, never replaces it on reconnect, and revalidates TokenId/ModifiedId before launch. Canonical ts_<UUID> service IDs are bound atomically to an exclusive opaque lease carrying exact entry identity, attachment generation, and authority epoch; every action, output read, persistence mutation, and cleanup uses that lease, simultaneous attachment is rejected, and exact Arc identity prevents registry, session, output, and close-response ABA. Preparation and a current-authority checkpoint precede the checked login-success write; activation immediately revalidates under the service lock before committing persistence and the exact subscriber. A bounded ordered worker performs terminal construction outside the Tokio task and service lock. Every opening owns a shared cancellation capability checked across helper-pipe waits and before exact generation/epoch commit. A committed session remains output-invisible until its exact TerminalOpened response is enqueued under the service lock; natural close response and exact-session removal share that same lock, so the ID cannot be reused between them. Each service and each output poll have explicit session, chunk, and byte ceilings; sorted IDs, a rotating start cursor, and per-session quanta prevent cross-session starvation, while split replay/output tails remain owned for later polls. Orderly termination becomes pending state and emits TerminalClosed only after replay, deferred tails, and the disconnected reader channel are exhausted. Direct PTY launch drops the parent's slave descriptor immediately after spawn and retains only the master, allowing child exit to end the blocking reader and complete that barrier. For an attached session's close response, reader-only completion starts a bounded direct-child status grace and re-queries the retained child on each poll; a real exit status wins, grace expiry is reported as -1, and writer failure remains immediate. Transport-worker completion is nevertheless immediate for reconnect admission and detached cleanup, so a dead persistent session is never republished during that grace. Revocation advances the epoch, marks workers fatal, cancels openings, takes the token, drains exact session owners, removes only the exact registry entry, and admits teardown to a bounded queue served by fixed workers created before terminal admission. Every lease and opening reserves a teardown permit before resource creation and retains it through rollback or complete joins, so admission and pending cleanup cannot grow without bound. Queue insertion shares no lock with cleanup work; session shutdown is signaled outside the session mutex, and Windows holds a shared TerminateJobObject capability that stops the helper tree immediately before deferred cleanup; unexpected termination failure aborts the service so process teardown closes the sole service-owned job handle and activates kill-on-close rather than continuing with a revoked shell. Detached monitoring removes and completes teardown of reader-dead, writer-dead, child-dead, and helper-dead sessions. The helper boundary is restricted to SYSTEM and the exact enabled logon SID: the service holds a duplex server handle needed for SetNamedPipeHandleState, while the user side receives only directional FILE_READ_DATA or FILE_WRITE_DATA plus SYNCHRONIZE, never generic-write/append/create-instance rights. Pipe connect is timeout- and authority-cancellable, switches from PIPE_NOWAIT to synchronous PIPE_WAIT, and shutdown repeatedly applies CancelSynchronousIo to retained worker thread handles until each is observably finished; every framed read checks the shutdown latch before issuing another operation, and unexpected cancellation failure terminates the dedicated helper while both worker handles remain owned. The helper is launched suspended into a kill-on-close job with an isolated token environment and validated profile directory. Its retained process handle supplies the actual Windows exit status; helper output EOF waits through a bounded grace longer than the I/O cancellation deadline for that status, the dedicated helper entrypoint exits with the shell status or a defined nonzero infrastructure-failure status, and query/transport failures are never reported as clean zero. Periodic served-session validation revokes the token and surviving shells on logoff or principal replacement. The invalid DuplicateToken conversion and fallback are deleted because WTSQueryUserToken already returns a primary token; Microsoft separately states that DuplicateToken creates an impersonation token unusable by CreateProcessAsUser, and documents AuthenticationId as the represented logon-session identifier while TokenId/ModifiedId identify the token object and its modification state. Linux's intentional authenticated-owner root terminal (R-X8/R-F1) is unchanged.
119Linux Debian shipped ELF runtime-library provenance — after the runner was bound to $ORIGIN/lib/librustdesk.so and Debian payload ownership was normalized, the shipped Rust core still carried Cargo's inherited release RPATH output: DT_RUNPATH [$ORIGIN/../../../../tmp/tc/rustinstall/lib/rustlib/x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu/lib]. From the installed location /usr/share/rustdesk/lib/librustdesk.so, that resolves under public /tmp/tc. A normal user can create that tree, and glibc uses a shared object's DT_RUNPATH for its direct DT_NEEDED dependencies before default system directories; local runtime proof showed the root-capable core searching the /tmp/tc path for libpulse-simple.so.0. The same package sweep found copied Flutter plugin shared libraries with absolute /src/flutter/linux/flutter/ephemeral build RUNPATHs. The plugin paths were not promoted to the same default local-user-to-root primitive because /src is not normally user-writable, but shipped root-capable ELFs must not contain build-directory runtime search paths at all.FIX R-S11c-10y makes Debian shipped ELF loader metadata, search paths, and identities artifact invariants. Cargo release rpath is pinned off in Cargo.toml, build.py, and scripts/build-debian.sh, so the Rust core no longer receives a Rust sysroot RUNPATH from the container's /tmp toolchain. Flutter plugin targets are built with install RUNPATH and $ORIGIN, so their copied artifacts search only their installed bundle directory for same-directory dependencies. scripts/verify-debian-package-authority.py inspects every regular data member with ELF magic, not only known bundle paths, and requires the runner plus every exact runtime-library path to contain a real runtime ELF rather than text, a relocatable object, or a wrong-architecture binary. Every shipped ELF must have a current 64-bit little-endian x86-64 header; the runner must be ET_EXEC or ET_DYN, while each shared object and any ELF admitted under the variable asset subtree must be ET_DYN. The runner must have one exactly mapped PT_INTERP naming only /lib64/ld-linux-x86-64.so.2; shared objects must have none. Every load segment must be ABI-aligned and never writable plus executable. Every ELF must carry exactly one canonical no-content PT_GNU_STACK: its offset, virtual address, physical address, file size, and memory size are zero; its flags are exactly PF_R|PF_W, without PF_X or extra bits; and its p_align is 0 or 1 for no required alignment, or a positive power of two. This preserves the security property actually consumed from p_flags by the Linux 4.15 baseline loader and glibc loader without inventing one linker-specific alignment: the pinned Dart AOT producer deliberately emits 1, the pinned Flutter engine emits 0, and GNU-linked objects emit 16. The verifier parses exactly one in-bounds loader-visible PT_DYNAMIC containing 16-byte Elf64_Dyn records, requires canonical p_align=8 with ABI-congruent file/virtual addresses, a canonical DT_NULL terminator with zero-only remainder, and consistent mapping by one file-backed PT_LOAD. It maps one nonempty bounded DT_STRTAB/DT_STRSZ through that same program-header authority, requires the ELF string-table first and last NUL bytes, and resolves every recognized loader string tag there rather than trusting potentially divergent section metadata. Every DT_NEEDED value must be a nonempty ASCII dependency basename, an optional sole DT_SONAME must equal the installed basename, and DT_CONFIG, DT_DEPAUDIT, DT_AUDIT, DT_AUXILIARY, and DT_FILTER are forbidden loader controls. Legacy RPATH, multiple tags, malformed strings, and present-but-empty RUNPATH tags are rejected; the runner must have exactly $ORIGIN/lib, libflutter_linux_gtk.so and plugin libraries must have exactly $ORIGIN, and every other ELF, including librustdesk.so, must have no runtime search-path tag. Handcrafted package and direct parser negatives cover wrong ELF class, byte order, machine, type, interpreter count/path/role; missing or duplicate GNU-stack headers, every nonzero no-content field, executable/missing/extra permission bits, and malformed alignment, with positive 0, 1, and 16 producer alignments; writable-executable loads and dynamic-segment count/mapping/alignment/termination; missing, duplicate, zero-sized, unmapped, oversized, non-NUL-bounded, or out-of-bounds dynamic-string state; unsafe dependency/SONAME values and auxiliary loader tags; mandatory non-ELF substitution; public-/tmp, empty, multiple, and legacy search-path tags; and a bad ELF hidden in the variable asset subtree. scripts/build-debian.sh runs this artifact gate before hashing packages.
120Protected service IPC resource boundary — generic service controls and dedicated password endpoints require separate, bounded work ownership; an admitted local peer must not obtain an uncapped frame, persistent connection, unowned blocking task, or secret-body read before endpoint proof.FIX Generic _service uses a capped codec, one request, bounded deadlines, and endpoint-specific concurrency, and contains no password body. Raw _password/_service_password use fixed canonical frames, fixed wiping allocation, proof before secret-body transfer, and independent proof/admission capacities. Linux/macOS own accepted tasks and mutation workers through shutdown. Windows retains and serially reuses its one first-instance message-pipe handle, owns exact listener/client supervisors and cancellation drains, and keeps service control and SAS on separate budgets. Shutdown closes admission and drains accepted work before clearing replay keys/tags; admitted mutations are not aborted. Password values are capped at 4096 bytes and macOS authorization at 1024 bytes. Malformed, timed-out, unauthorized, oversized, and noncanonical operations fail closed.
121Permanent-password provisioning through visible process arguments — the sole CPace owner credential was accepted as rustdesk --password <value>, copied from the OS argument vector, and documented in the Debian deployment guide. On Linux, /proc/<pid>/cmdline exposes the complete live command line under the default procfs visibility policy; the invoking sudo command can retain the value while administrator authentication is pending, and the RustDesk process retained it while polkit or macOS Authorization Services ran. Shell history and same-login process inspection extend the class to every desktop platform. This is CWE-214: an ordinary local observer can learn the actual permanent password, then authenticate through CPace as the owner; on a root/headless Linux controlled side that intentionally grants the documented root-capable Remote, Terminal, and FileTransfer authority.FIX R-S11e-16 removes password-valued process arguments from provisioning. Bare rustdesk --password uses rpassword::prompt_password to read and confirm the value from the controlling terminal with echo disabled. Noninteractive headless automation uses the distinct no-value --password-stdin form, which refuses an interactive stdin, reads one bounded UTF-8 line, and shares the 4096-byte unattended-password ceiling; an explicit empty line remains the deliberate credential-removal operation. Both commands require an exact one-argument command shape. --password <value>, --password-stdin <value>, environment-variable input, and any positional compatibility fallback are rejected before password routing begins. The resulting value follows only the user-owned or privileged raw protocol and receiver authorization of R-S11i; there is no argv, environment, ordinary-main IPC, or local-persistence fallback. The deployment guide uses only the hidden prompt; the smoke harness uses stdin without putting the secret in RustDesk or timeout argv; unit and source gates pin exact command parsing, confirmation, TTY refusal, bounded UTF-8 line input, positional-secret rejection, and absence of password-valued provisioning examples.

#118 lifecycle and helper closure. The lease also carries an authority epoch and stages reconnect persistence until a current-authority checkpoint and successful login-response write. With no option processing between that checked write and activation, the post-write transition validates the checkpoint under the service lock, commits staged state, installs the exact subscriber, and makes output eligible; concurrent revocation fails closed. Existing persistent state survives failed preauthorization, while new reservations roll back. A bounded ordered action worker accepts only nonblocking queue sends, so PTY/helper construction never occupies the Tokio connection task. Every epoch-bound opening owns a shared cancellation capability; revocation signals it before clearing reservations, helper connection polling observes it, and any process already created is under direct-child or kill-on-close-job ownership before later fallible work. Final commit still requires the exact generation, epoch, reservation identity, and uncancelled capability. Input and resize linearize against the exact session Arc and use nonblocking sends. A service-entry watchdog runs after Windows logoff events and periodically even while detached. Revocation advances the epoch, latches worker failure, cancels openings, takes the token, and drains exact session Arcs under the service lock, then conditionally removes only that registry Arc and enqueues tracked teardown without waiting for a session mutex or join. A new logon can therefore reuse the opaque ID without the revoked Arc affecting its replacement. Detached monitoring removes naturally exited direct/helper sessions by exact Arc identity and releases their process, pipe, and job ownership; dead sessions are not reconnectable metadata. Attached connections close on fatal-latch detection, and stale output is sent only while its exact session Arc or verified absence condition remains authoritative. Helper pipes use no overlapped handle: a timeout-and-authority-cancellable joined worker polls a local-only first-instance PIPE_NOWAIT server, verifies the retained helper process PID and token principal, then restores PIPE_WAIT for synchronous I/O. The server handle is duplex so it has the mode-change access required by SetNamedPipeHandleState; the enabled logon SID receives only FILE_WRITE_DATA|SYNCHRONIZE or FILE_READ_DATA|SYNCHRONIZE according to direction, never generic-write/append/pipe-instance authority. Helper shutdown targets both retained I/O thread handles with CancelSynchronousIo before joining them. Token and security buffers are pointer-aligned. CreateEnvironmentBlock(FALSE) and GetUserProfileDirectoryW must both succeed for the same token; no LocalSystem environment or working-directory fallback exists. The helper is created suspended, assigned to a JOB_OBJECT_LIMIT_KILL_ON_JOB_CLOSE job, and resumed only after assignment, so dropping the single RAII owner terminates the helper, ConPTY shell, and descendants. The offline committed-source Windows build runs native terminal runtime tests before artifact compilation, including real blocking round trips for both pipe directions, external opening cancellation, and pending synchronous-read cancellation.

#Finding / exposureDisposition in this fork
122Connection-manager raw authenticated-message authority — the inherited CM file worker serialized arbitrary top-level message_proto::Message values into Data::RawMessage(Vec<u8>); the authenticated Connection accepted those bytes from CM and wrote them directly to the keyed peer stream with send_raw. The worker needed only a finite set of file directory, block, digest, completion, error, and confirmation results, but the abstraction granted authority over every protobuf union accepted by the remote peer, including media/control/clipboard shapes and malformed-message connection termination. Job admission used peer-chosen integer IDs; read and write namespaces could overlap; IDs could be reused after cancellation; write authority was removed before CM acknowledged finalization; digest responses had no independent request generation; one-shot directory and mutation responses had no operation-bound generation. This is a CWE-441 confused deputy and CWE-863 incorrect authorization: CM could select authenticated network behavior outside the file operation the connection delegated. Protocol Buffers explicitly require bytes to be interpreted relative to a selected message type; raw wire bytes are not a capability boundary (protobuf encoding guide). The path was live in desktop and Android controlled-side file operations; iOS retained source coherence but has no controlled server. It was not promoted to a default local-to-root escalation because the bytes were sent outward to an already authenticated file-transfer peer and CM endpoint admission remained separately authenticated, but it violated exact capability confinement and exposed remote-side parsers/state beyond delegated file results.FIX R-S11e-17 deletes Data::RawMessage, every legacy CM file-result variant, raw protobuf directory/result blobs, and raw serialized FS::SendConfirm. CM can return only the closed serde-native CmFileResponseKind DTO set inside a CmFileResponse envelope carrying the exact connection ID and random per-session cm_auth_token. Connection mints monotonic generations for read and write jobs plus bounded one-shot requests; every mutation acknowledgement echoes a typed operation descriptor. It validates exact FileTransfer mode, connection, token, generation, operation kind/path, expected ID/path/file number, metadata limits, and bounded error text. CM job IDs are never reused during a connection. Reads track monotonic current-file progression and an exact digest-confirmation phase. Writes use exclusive active, digest-checking, peer-confirmation, and finalizing phases; blocks and successful completion are rejected while a digest is unresolved, peer errors remain terminally admissible, and final authority remains until typed CM acknowledgement. Stale, cross-kind, and out-of-phase responses do not consume another authority; failed helper-channel enqueues retire the affected authority and return a peer error. Windows virtual-root drive entries are admitted only as canonical uppercase drive descriptors for an authorized / listing; transfer manifests retain strict relative-name validation. Generic CM filesystem authority is FileTransfer-only; Remote clipboard authority remains a distinct Remote-only capability. CM returns structured file metadata, never top-level protobuf bytes, and Connection alone constructs peer-facing protobuf messages. Structured _cm frames have a 128 MiB aggregate decoder ceiling. The only split binary frame is a typed ReadBlock payload: FileTransfer mode, connection, and token are checked first; the decoder is reduced to 256 KiB and read under a five-second deadline before its length header is consumed; exact generation/current-file phase is checked before peer message construction. Linux, Windows, macOS, Android, and retained iOS source share the closed IPC types. Unit tests cover exact session/token/type binding, monotonic read and digest-confirmation phases, exclusive write phases, bounded errors, FileTransfer-only CM file authority, and typed directory/block results; verify.sh and apple-conform-check.sh gate deletion of every raw/legacy variant and the complete authority/state-machine shape.
123Windows named-pipe impersonation restoration — the LocalSystem service impersonates the connected _service client to verify elevation, and the service-owned main server impersonates its connected main-pipe client to verify the LocalSystem commit peer. The inherited guard called RevertToSelf only from Drop and logged failure, after which the operation returned normally. Windows specifies that a failed restoration leaves the thread executing in the client's security context and directs the application to terminate. RustDesk's IPC entry point uses a current-thread Tokio runtime whose spawned connection tasks reuse that OS thread, so log-and-continue could run later privileged service work under the client token. This affected Windows installed-service password and service-owned RDP policy requests plus the LocalSystem service-owned credential commit gate; Linux, macOS, Android, and iOS do not compile this token-impersonation path.FIX R-S11e-18 confines every connected-client impersonation to one dedicated disposable OS thread operating over a duplicated pipe handle retained by its never-impersonated parent. The parent waits for thread termination before observing a result. Normal success and ordinary Result error paths call RevertToSelf before returning from the worker. The release profile remains globally process-fatal on panic, so this disposition makes no panic-containment claim. If restoration fails, the worker records a restoration-failed state and calls ExitThread, returning no authorization result and running no post-failure destructor or runtime work. For the specific restoration-failure case, the service process remains alive with its service-owned child job retained, while no reusable thread continues under the client token. verify.sh requires the single confined impersonation site, duplicated-handle lifetime, restore-before-normal-return ordering, disposable-thread termination on restoration failure, parent wait-before-result, token-requirement vocabulary, SAS generation checks, and absence of the former log-and-continue guard and process abort; the repository Windows build gate compiles the target API shape.
124Windows service-owned child lifetime and SCM completion authority — the LocalSystem service retained only one raw handle to the current service-owned --server process. Session handoff could lose the old tree, unknown port-forward state was treated as idle, IPC traffic could suppress supervision, and SCM stop used the capacity-limited _service bus before claiming SERVICE_STOPPED without proving descendant absence. A stale LocalSystem process tree could therefore survive replacement or stop outside SCM accounting.FIX R-S11e-19/R-S11e-21 make SCM the sole owner of a fresh non-inheritable kill-on-close job, retained process handle, immutable PID/creation-time identity, and served session. Creation-time PROC_THREAD_ATTRIBUTE_JOB_LIST assignment places the child in the job before its initial thread runs. Independent supervision preserves active or unknown port-forward state. Runtime retirement bounds each authenticated shutdown attempt and retains a still-live main child for later retry; exact-job termination is permitted only after the main child has observably exited and only for remaining descendants. Replacement requires zero-active-process proof. SCM stop uses a capacity-independent in-process latch, closes listener admission, drains each accepted service transaction, then requests authenticated shutdown from the exact retained child. An admitted password mutation is never aborted to satisfy a stop deadline; SCM may remain truthfully STOP_PENDING; while active transaction recovery, drain, child shutdown, or job-empty retry continues, checkpoints may advance periodically and do not claim that a count decreased. Stop and preshutdown are prioritized over endpoint admission, and the stop latch is linearized with both service endpoint admissions after receiver authorization and with every service-owned child launch. The shutdown loop retains the sole job handle and retries accounting or descendant termination until the job is empty. Only then is SERVICE_STOPPED emitted: success uses a zero exit code, while a prior loop, cleanup-status, or service-status error uses a service-specific failure code rather than a clean-stopped claim. The native Windows build runs the focused service, SAS, password-finality, and input-lifecycle suites before artifact compilation.
125Windows caller-image installation authority and nontransactional machine-state mutation — the public application --uninstall/--silent-install/helper verbs and in-app install page fed a generated batch/VBS program into an elevated System32 cmd.exe. Uninstall embedded current_exe as the elevated certificate/driver helper; the Flutter runner loaded caller-adjacent librustdesk.dll before Rust dispatched the helper verb. An administrator approving UAC for a copied or attacker-prepared application directory could therefore execute that adjacent DLL elevated and then create a LocalSystem service. The path also replayed registry uninstall strings, killed processes by basename, and independently mutated service, firewall, registry, shortcut, and file state outside a rollback transaction. The MSI duplicated those mutations in custom C++ actions; firewall and service completion could diverge from package success. The direct execution chain requires an administrator/UAC approval and is not a promptless standard-user-to-SYSTEM exploit, but the approved operation conferred authority on caller-controlled application bytes instead of a fixed package.FIX R-S11f/R-S11e-20 deletes the application installer/uninstaller/helper verbs, elevated command-file machinery, in-app install route, basename MSI process killer, and custom SCM/firewall implementations. The fixed rustdesk-setup.exe bootstrapper embeds only a pre-canonicalized rustdesk-installer.msi, stages it under a protected Program Files directory, invokes the System32 msiexec.exe image with explicit arguments, and accepts only 0/3010. It never loads or executes the packaged application to install. WiX ServiceInstall (null StartName for LocalSystem), ServiceControl, and ServiceConfigFailureActions own service state with 5/10/30-second restart backoff; a file-bound inbound TCP/21118 fire:FirewallException owns the sole network rule. Fixed no-input certificate and self-contained SetupAPI Amyuni cleanup are checked commit actions scheduled only after successful explicit uninstall; upgrades preserve that irreversible state. Exact runtime-file cleanup remains a checked deferred package action. The runtime broker refresh is restricted to the verified fixed service image, fixed Program Files destination, and non-reparse System32 source, with byte verification, atomic replacement, and no shell/UAC/process-name kill. The Windows build constructs and validates the canonical MSI before packing only that file into setup, deletes staging in a finally path, and emits artifacts from exact paths.
126Local transaction finality and connection-owned input execution — broad local messages, detached work, and timeout results must not outlive or contradict an admitted credential mutation or connection-owned native input operation.FIX R-S11g/R-S11i keep ordinary main IPC closed and bounded while moving every password body to canonical raw _password/_service_password transport. Password changes use a canonical UUID plus process-random-keyed HMAC-SHA256 value fingerprint, irrevocable Prepared/Pending admission, worker-owned terminal completion, persistence-before-Applied, typed terminal results, and uncertainty recovery under the same ID/value. Recovery reuses the same ID/value for at most 600 seconds; authoritative rejection, internal failure, and shutdown remain terminal. The 64-entry ledger evicts only its oldest terminal replay result, never live or recoverable work, and denied pre-admission authorization consumes no durable slot. It is not crash-durable exactly-once; shutdown drains accepted work before wiping entries, tags, and keys. Windows status delivery is operation-ACK-bound and listener/client threads retain exact blocking ownership. Protected replies and helper launch proofs are deadline-bound. Service control and SAS remain independently budgeted, with SAS bound to the retained child generation. Desktop input has item/byte caps, strict event validation, fail-closed queue errors, atomic cancellation/dispatch admission, fixed supervisor-owned joins, connection-owned reference-counted pressed keys/buttons, unwind-safe cleanup under unwind-enabled builds, and a synchronous macOS queue barrier; Android validates both raw and rewritten keys. Current `.6` source and source-conformance gates do not substitute for the pending native Windows worktree validation or final clean cold release build.
127Viewer-side TCP tunnel mapping authority and lifetime — desktop port-forward/RDP mappings selected remote targets through shared mutable login state, bound reusable loopback listeners, detached relays from mapping removal, awaited setup inside the listener loop, ignored control EOF during setup, and accumulated local application bytes in an unbounded pre-login vector. A local process could cross-wire concurrent mappings, compete for a reusable local endpoint, keep a removed mapping alive, complete connect/CPace/login after removal, or grow viewer memory while the remote delayed authorization. Unbounded mapping creation and accepted setup tasks added local resource-exhaustion paths.FIX R-T17 deletes shared target state and requires a bounded, validated, private-field PortForwardTarget in the tunnel-only login API. Bracketed IPv6 remains canonical on the wire. Mapping listeners use the shared exclusive path; Windows sets SO_EXCLUSIVEADDRUSE before bind and its native hostile test explicitly sets SO_REUSEADDR on the rejected second socket. General Data lifecycle channels are replaced by cancellation plus a one-slot coalescing RDP launch signal. A named OS thread and independent current-thread Tokio runtime own the bounded-command supervisor and every mapping task through EOF global drain. A process-lifetime reaper is the sole OS-thread join owner; its closed 32-slot queue accepts only nonblocking handoffs, returns normal-close results, and fail-stops on bootstrap, full, or disconnected handoff rather than blocking or detaching. Each mapping owns cancellation plus a JoinSet for all setup/relay work; removal/duplicate/global close drain before completion, and ready task results are reaped before another accept. Private process mapping admission is capped at 32; accepted connections acquire nonblocking 32-per-mapping and 128-process permits held through relay, with literal boundary/recovery tests. Over-limit sockets are dropped before outbound setup. The local reader is constructed only after PeerInfo, and all tunnel I/O errors terminate explicitly. Port forwarding is a desktop capability: its lifecycle variants, internal session methods, and consumer are absent on mobile; mobile session construction and deep-link/connect routes reject port-forward and RDP before creating or reinterpreting a session; and the shared FFI returns an explicit unsupported-operation error before changing peer configuration or sending a command. Linux native tests cover its behavioral contracts; Windows/macOS source and cross-compile gates retain the shared implementation, while native platform tests remain required and are not claimed by Linux.
128Windows machine-credential local-authority, LPE, and storage class — the replaceable service-owned server child previously acted as durable credential writer and could reach path-selected or runtime-created machine storage. A local caller or filesystem race that influenced that child, root selection, ACL state, or replacement path could cross from user/session authority into the LocalSystem credential namespace, replace the credential accepted by the unattended service, or induce an ambiguous durable result. Profile fallback, ACL repair, reparse traversal, pathname reopen, and insufficient post-replacement durability proof are one local-authority/storage class, not independent conveniences.FIX R-S11b/R-S11f/R-S11h/R-S11i close the class. The MSI alone provisions ProgramData\<Product>\config with a protected inheritable SYSTEM/Administrators full-control DACL and accepted SYSTEM-or-Administrators owner. Runtime startup proves the existing handle-relative non-reparse root, identity/type/owner/DACL, persistent-ACL volume behavior, LocalSystem read/write authority, and matching-serial volume handle, with no create, repair, profile, alternate-root, rewrite, or pathname fallback. The stable SCM supervisor is the only durable writer and volume-handle owner; the retained child has only a generation-bound read-only replica. Mutation uses pre-rename sync, handle-relative replacement, renamed-file NtFlushBuffersFile, then matching-volume FlushFileBuffers; post-replacement ambiguity is process-fatal. Queue uncertainty is Pending. The process-random HMAC-SHA256 process ledger is capped at 64 entries, evicts only the oldest terminal replay result, never evicts active work, and is not crash-durable exactly-once; client recovery is capped at 600 seconds and treats authoritative failure or shutdown as terminal. Password ingress is the first-instance raw _service_password protocol with exact process/token/session proof and operation-bound status ACK. SensitivePassword owns one originating or fixed inbound allocation through an Arc; final secret allocations, fixed raw body/stack frames, unused tails, and capabilities wipe on drop/error without claiming all allocator or OS copies are wiped. The accepted operational cost is a potentially blocking whole-volume flush and administrative volume access for every credential namespace mutation.
129libvpx VP9 encoder superframe-index heap write — CVE-2026-1861 and CVE-2026-2447 track the same bounds defect in the VP9 encoder's write_superframe_index. Both libvpx v1.15.2 and v1.16.0 predate the upstream fix. This is an encoder finding, not evidence that the viewer decoder residual in Appendix C #2b has been removed or newly proven vulnerable.FIX + ACCEPT DECODER RESIDUAL R-B13 retains libvpx v1.15.2 as overlay port revision 1 and applies exact upstream commit d5f35ac8d93cba7f7a3f7ddb8f9dc8bd28f785e1. The source SHA-512 is 824fe8719e4115ec359ae0642f5e1cea051d458f09eb8c24d60858cf082f66e411215e23228173ab154044bafbdfbb2d93b589bb726f55b233939b91f928aae0; the patch SHA-512 is 2980e0504e207047d55e6c98dcc55c2a3c06315b4ec04d59c42d786657e03ba0e1c73a0718ac6635990aac25fc642b204a1d56e13501ce2bd9625996ad0310d8. The 32-entry Windows tools manifest is pinned by SHA-256 c888ee51f816d8fb811ff5e62971b973449d0dadfef683d4cae83119f0e83a8e and individually SHA-512-pins all offline tools, including MinGW x64 pkgconf and PowerShell Core 7.2.24. file://-only overlay inputs, blocked guest origin access, complete platform cache identity, and a forced clean Windows rebuild prevent stale vulnerable bytes from surviving. Appendix C #2b remains the separately accepted in-process VP8/VP9 decoder residual and SHOULD-sandbox obligation.
130Same-host release-smoke coexistence with an operational older RustDesk service — the mandatory Linux runtime smoke previously passed large inline shell programs through host-visible Docker argv and launched the tested binary with the production-shaped visible arguments rustdesk --server. An older installed release may still contain a privileged process-text cleanup selector for that shape. Running the release smoke on the same host could therefore make a test process selectable by old service cleanup and create an unacceptable interaction with an operational service. This is a current-release harness defect. It is distinct from, and does not close, the upcoming init-system-independent redesign of Linux service-child ownership; that future work must replace process-text lifecycle authority without changing the supported non-systemd deployment contract.FIX The mandatory release smoke invokes mounted stage files by path, never copies their bodies into Docker or shell argv, and launches the exact opened regular executable with fexecve under neutral test-only argv[0]=rd-smoke-server and exact argument 1 --server. Source gates prove Rust role parsing discards argv zero, consumes the role from argument one, and derives executable identity through current_exe(); any semantic dependency on argv zero fails the gate. Before Docker starts, an unprivileged host guard records a stable, private, exact PID/start-time/cmdline baseline for the historical rustdesk +--server selector. It scans the full host /proc throughout every stage and at final drain, admits only identical baseline records, and fails on every new match without signaling any process. A separate exact process proof binds each smoke server PID/start time to the expected executable device/inode through /proc/<pid>/exe and to the exact NUL-delimited neutral argv. The selector regression fixture selects production and former inline-shell shapes, rejects launcher and neutral-server shapes, admits an inert pre-existing baseline, and rejects a new production-shaped record. Runtime stages retain Docker's isolated PID/network namespaces, publish no host port, mount source read-only after the build, rewrite only the container listener to 127.0.0.1:21118, require one IPv4 TCP listener and zero UDP, and terminate only PID/start-time-bound stage-owned processes. The harness contains no sudo/polkit request, service operation, process-name scan, pkill, or signal to a pre-existing host process. scripts/verify-release.sh retains this contract in its mandatory runtime-smoke gate. The separate Linux service-child lifecycle redesign remains open for upcoming releases.
131Windows legacy uninstall-registry metadata selected current service policy — after Windows Installer became the sole machine-state authority, runtime code still searched retired Inno Setup {54E86BC2-6C85-41F3-A9EB-1A94AC9B1F93}_is1 keys in both registry views before the current MSI-owned product key. Any nonempty legacy InstallLocation selected that entire stale namespace for installed-state classification, build metadata, and the LocalSystem-owned share_rdp policy read/write. A naturally stale administrator-owned record could therefore make the current package appear absent or split machine policy into a retired package namespace. This is service-policy and authority correctness, not a demonstrated promptless standard-user-to-SYSTEM primitive: HKLM uninstall metadata is normally administrator-owned, and the typed _service mutation still requires an elevated caller. The old abstraction nevertheless contradicted R-S11b's rule that the enforcing service owns one exact machine policy and preserved compatibility authority for an installer this fork had deleted.FIX R-S11e-23 deletes the Inno GUID selector, the manually addressed Wow6432Node branch, generic uninstall-key discovery, generic registry-value reads, and registry-selected executable fallback. Microsoft documents the physical Wow6432Node location as system-reserved and not an application pathname API, and provides KEY_WOW64_64KEY for an explicit 64-bit view. Runtime now derives one subkey, HKLM\Software\Microsoft\Windows\CurrentVersion\Uninstall\<current constrained product name>, matching the sole current MSI component. Reads and the typed LocalSystem share_rdp write explicitly use the 64-bit view. Installed-state classification additionally requires that key's nonempty InstallLocation to equal the fixed supported Program Files service root and requires the exact product executable to be an opened non-reparse regular file; it has no default-path, legacy-key, or alternate-view fallback. Build-date and RDP-policy reads are typed fixed-name helpers rather than a generic key/value facade. Native Windows tests and source gates pin the single namespace and reject every retired selector token.
132Windows privacy-display recovery serialized an arbitrary elevated HKLM write — the inherited RegRecovery object serialized a caller-selectable registry path, value name, old and new raw bytes, and integer value types into the ordinary reg_recovery option. Windows --server startup deserialized that object and opened the selected HKLM path with write access; its non-forced comparison did not authenticate the record because a preparer could copy the target's current value into the serialized new field. The hardened installed child reads an installer-provisioned protected config root and has no durable config-write authority, which blocks a promptless ordinary-user write and also means this persistence scheme did not work for the supported installed path. A deliberately elevated direct user-profile server could nevertheless turn same-user profile data into an administrator-approved arbitrary HKLM value write. This is an approved-elevation confused-deputy shape, not evidence of a promptless standard-user-to-SYSTEM exploit.FIX R-S11j/R-S11e-24 delete the config key, serde/JSON representation, startup replay, exported startup helper, caller-selected path/key/type fields, and integer-to-registry-type converter. Recovery is now a private process-local collection whose elements contain only one enumerated direct Connectivity child name and its prior Recent RegValue captured by the same process. Restore derives the fixed HKLM GraphicsDrivers/Connectivity parent and fixed value name, rejects nested/control/overlong subkeys, retains every changed child rather than the inherited nondeterministic first match, attempts every restoration, and consumes the collection once during normal privacy-mode turn-off. Microsoft documents SetDisplayConfig as the supported API for display topology and temporary/saved display configurations; a future durable recovery may use that API or a typed service-owned transaction, but this closure does not claim crash recovery. Native Windows tests pin the fixed target derivation, invalid-subkey rejection, and multi-display collection; source gates reject any serialized/config/startup replay regression.
133Linux service-owned configuration followed ambient HOME/XDG_CONFIG_HOME — directories-next intentionally resolves a normal user's project directory from XDG_CONFIG_HOME or HOME/.config, and the inherited Config::get_home likewise accepted ambient HOME. The Linux --service supervisor and service-owned child used those same process-global helpers. Systemd commonly launches system services with a controlled environment, but OpenRC, runit, sudo, and manual service launch are supported deployment shapes; application authority therefore depended on launcher policy. The headless root-child branch compounded that ambiguity by clearing its environment and then copying the supervisor's ambient HOME back into the child. A privileged process could consequently select its credential/config namespace from caller-selected environment state rather than the effective uid's account record. This is a service-policy and namespace confused-deputy defect, not a demonstrated promptless privilege-escalation primitive: exploitation still depends on how a privileged launcher admits and preserves the environment and on filesystem ownership of the selected target.FIX R-S11k/R-S11e-25 give Linux service-owned roles a separate, immutable process-local configuration root. After signed application identity loading and before any configuration access, --service and the exact service-owned child query the current effective uid through the password database, validate an absolute clean home and constrained project component, and bind Config::get_home/Config::path to <passwd home>/.config/<project>. Failure or inconsistent initialization terminates the role. The root/headless child still starts from env_clear, but its only HOME is independently passwd-derived; XDG_CONFIG_HOME is not propagated. Ordinary user-owned server/viewer/UI processes keep the standard XDG behavior. A subprocess regression test preserves hostile ambient HOME/XDG_CONFIG_HOME values while proving they cannot select the initialized service root, and the verifier binds role detection, initialization order, fail-closed behavior, child environment construction, requirements, and this ledger entry. Final exact Debian artifact execution remains part of R-B2/R-S11c-27 rather than being inferred from source tests.
134Linux root service child re-imported ambient session/audio environment — after clearing inheritance, the root/headless launch branch copied DISPLAY, XAUTHORITY, WAYLAND_DISPLAY, DBUS_SESSION_BUS_ADDRESS, TERM, PULSE_LATENCY_MSEC, and PIPEWIRE_LATENCY from the supervisor process. The service loop already held the selected Desktop, but start_server(None, ...) discarded it and overloaded that None to mean both “retain root” and “use ambient session state.” A manual or non-systemd privileged launcher that preserved caller-controlled variables could therefore choose IPC/display/audio endpoints consumed by the root server child. This is a privileged-launch environment confused-deputy defect and parser/endpoint exposure, not a demonstrated promptless local privilege escalation: exploitation still depends on a privileged launcher admitting hostile environment state.FIX R-S11l/R-S11e-26 separates the child principal into RootService or ActiveDesktopUser while requiring both variants to receive the selected Desktop. The launcher retains env_clear, derives root HOME from the effective uid's passwd record, derives non-root identity/home/runtime values from the validated credential drop, and copies X11/Wayland/D-Bus selectors only from the desktop snapshot. Ambient Pulse/PipeWire forwarding and the process-global set_x11_env handoff are deleted. Terminal selection no longer reads supervisor TERM, TMUX, or STY; it uses a bounded desktop-uid observation or fixed capability fallback. The networkless actual-binary lifecycle fixture launches the root service under hostile HOME/XDG, X11, Wayland, D-Bus, terminal, Pulse, and PipeWire values, exact-checks the root allowlist and passwd home, rejects every hostile value, and retains the existing exact non-root allowlist proof. The source gate binds the typed principal/desktop API, deleted ambient reads/mutation, runtime fixture, and mandatory smoke result. Final exact Debian artifact execution remains part of R-B2/R-S11c-27.
135Linux service-owned startup inherited working-directory authority — Rust Command inherits the parent's cwd by default, and Linux carries cwd across fork/execve. The service supervisor did not normalize its own cwd and try_start_server_ did not specify one for either root or privilege-dropped children. In addition, debug builds gave cwd-relative ./custom.txt precedence over the executable-relative sidecar and returned without trying the deployed sidecar whenever that ambient file was readable, even if its signed payload was invalid. Release builds did not contain that debug branch, and an attacker cannot forge the required RustDesk Ed25519 signature; this is therefore a debug/manual privileged-launch identity/namespace confused-deputy and future-relative-path authority defect, not evidence of a production release exploit or promptless standard-user-to-root escalation. A replayed valid publisher-signed payload could nevertheless select APP_NAME before service config and IPC namespaces were bound, while an invalid ambient file could suppress the deployed sidecar.FIX R-S11m/R-S11e-27 delete the cwd custom-client source entirely. load_custom_client now accepts only an absolute current_exe-derived sidecar path (with the existing macOS Resources mapping); mobile embeddings retain their explicit in-memory signed payload. Exact Linux service roles change cwd to / before global initialization and fail closed on error, and the service-child command independently sets current_dir("/") before spawn. The networkless actual-debug-binary lifecycle fixture launches normal, recovery, hostile-record, pre-pidfd, root, and active-user service roles from an attacker-owned directory containing malformed custom.txt; it proves live supervisor and child cwd are exactly / and rejects any evidence that the ambient file was parsed. A unit regression pins executable-relative path derivation, and the source gate rejects any debug/cwd loader, missing or late cwd bind, missing runtime proof, or documentation drift. Final exact Debian artifact execution remains part of R-B2/R-S11c-27.
136Linux service-owned startup inherited non-stdio descriptor authority — Linux preserves an open descriptor across execve unless it has FD_CLOEXEC. The service bootstrap closed neither launcher descriptors nor later supervisor descriptors before spawning root and privilege-dropped children. A pre-fix current debug binary launched directly as a service-owned server retained descriptor 198 to a root-owned mode-0600 file in its live process even though the descriptor lacked close-on-exec. Such a descriptor bypasses the pathname permission that would prevent a dropped-uid child from reopening the object. The reproduction proves a real capability leak at the supported manual/supervisor boundary; it does not show that an ordinary user can cause a correctly configured privileged launcher to admit an arbitrary descriptor. Standard service managers and sudo commonly close or mark unrelated descriptors, so severity depends on privileged-launch policy: this is a root/manual-launch confused-deputy and root-to-child capability leak, not a demonstrated promptless standard-user-to-root escalation.FIX R-S11n/R-S11e-28 close every non-stdio descriptor at exact Linux service-role entry before cwd, global, custom-client, config, IPC, or network initialization. The service-child pre-exec hook first applies close_range(CLOSE_RANGE_CLOEXEC) to all descriptors above stderr, with a raw fcntl fallback bounded by canonical /proc/sys/fs/nr_open; only the forked active-user child clears close-on-exec on its identity-checked executable descriptor, which the final image closes after parent-liveness proof. The final supervisor/child bootstrap uses close_range with a raw close fallback and fails closed if any non-absent descriptor cannot be removed. Descriptors 0–2 remain the deliberate launcher I/O contract. The networkless actual-binary lifecycle fixture opens the root-only file without FD_CLOEXEC across every manual launch path and compares its device/inode against every live supervisor, root/active-user child, and direct pre-pidfd child descriptor. The source/mutation gate binds syscall semantics, fallback, ordering, exact-executable exception, runtime proof, and documentation. Final exact Debian artifact execution remains part of R-B2/R-S11c-27.
137Linux service-originated helper launch inherited descriptor authority — after service bootstrap closure, the root/service process still launches helper images for user-session work and GUI reopen sequencing. The old sudo -E env probe, both run_as_user sudo branches, and the delayed reopen helper built Command objects without RustDesk-owned non-stdio descriptor policy. Linux would preserve any descriptor not carrying FD_CLOEXEC into the helper image, and sudo's default close-from behavior is configurable site policy rather than application authority. A root-open config, IPC/listener, service-runtime, directory, socket, or other kernel object could therefore be exposed to a helper image beyond the intended argv/environment/stdio contract if it was open and not close-on-exec at that launch point. This is a privileged-launch/root-to-helper capability leak and authority hygiene defect, not a demonstrated promptless ordinary-user-to-root primitive.FIX R-S11o/R-S11e-29 adds one Linux helper-launch pre-exec configurator that resolves the canonical descriptor bound in the parent and marks every descriptor above stderr close-on-exec before helper execve. The sudo environment probe, both run_as_user branches, and delayed reopen helpers use it before output or spawn; failure to configure the hook fails closed or skips the nonessential probe. The direct service-child executable-fd exception is not available to these helpers. Source and mutation gates bind the helper implementation, both sudo branches, the probe, the reopen helpers, this requirement, and the ledger entry. Final exact Debian artifact execution remains part of R-B2/R-S11c-27.
138Linux service-owned pkcheck launch inherited descriptor authority — the Linux service-owned unattended-password authorization path resolved and metadata-validated the fixed /usr/bin/pkcheck executable, null-routed stdio, and supplied only the intended action/process/interaction argv, but it still launched the helper through a direct std::process::Command::new(pkcheck).spawn() shape. After the root service accepted a caller and before it committed the credential, any root-service descriptor that lacked FD_CLOEXEC could therefore cross into the external polkit authorization helper. This is a root-service-to-helper descriptor-capability leak and authority hygiene defect, not a demonstrated promptless ordinary-user-to-root primitive: the helper executable and parent directories were already trusted root-owned paths, and exploitation depends on a live service descriptor being both sensitive and not close-on-exec.FIX R-S11p/R-S11e-30 applies the shared Linux helper descriptor pre-exec configurator before the pkcheck spawn. The authorization path now builds a mutable command, sets null stdio and exact argv, registers the parent-bound close-on-exec hook, logs and rejects the service-owned password operation if that hook cannot be configured, and only then executes command.spawn(). Source and mutation gates bind the pkcheck command shape, descriptor-policy-before-spawn ordering, direct-spawn absence, R-S11p, this Appendix C row, and the hardening ledger. Final exact Debian artifact execution remains part of R-B2/R-S11c-27.
139Linux same-executable child inherited descriptor authority — the shared run_me/run_me_with_env helper launched the current RustDesk executable with selected argv and environment additions but no RustDesk-owned descriptor policy. This helper is not viewer-only: the root service-owned server's headless connection-manager path deliberately uses it when it does not cross to another user. Any non-stdio descriptor that lacked FD_CLOEXEC at that point could therefore enter the CM image, while URL, tray, and whiteboard launches shared the same unsound default. This is a root-service-to-child capability leak on the service-owned CM route and ambient-authority hygiene defect on user-owned routes, not a demonstrated promptless ordinary-user-to-root primitive; exploitation requires a sensitive live descriptor without close-on-exec at the launch point.FIX R-S11q/R-S11e-31 applies the existing Linux helper pre-exec configurator inside run_me_with_env before its only spawn. Failure to configure the parent-resolved hook is converted to an I/O error and no child is launched. The same-executable API carries no descriptor exception and continues to expose only argv, explicit environment additions, and stdio. A Linux actual-child unit regression uses a shell exec to inject descriptor 9, proves the intermediate test image holds the target device/inode, then launches the same test image through run_me_with_env and proves the final image has no matching live /proc/self/fd entry. Source and mutation gates bind the call and its ordering, the actual-child proof, the service-owned CM consumer, R-S11q, this Appendix C row, and the hardening ledger. Final exact Debian artifact execution remains part of R-B2/R-S11c-27.
140Linux external-helper descriptor allowlist authority — the initial helper closure lived in the root crate and protected sudo/reopen/pkcheck/same-executable paths, but it could not be called by shared hbb_common launchers or dependent crates. Source enumeration found direct production launches for loginctl, notification helpers, xdg-screensaver, xrandr, w, systemctl, FUSE unmount, and the feature-inert hardware-codec checker without an application-owned descriptor contract. The root service repeatedly reaches loginctl while refreshing desktop state and can reach the display/service helpers, so an ambient sensitive descriptor lacking FD_CLOEXEC could enter an external image. Clipboard FUSE also made _FUSE_COMMFD inheritable in the parent before spawn, creating a process-wide race window. This is privileged root/service-to-helper capability leakage on service-reachable paths and descriptor hygiene elsewhere, not a demonstrated promptless ordinary-user-to-root primitive; exploitation still requires a sensitive live inheritable descriptor.FIX R-S11r/R-S11e-32 moves the ordinary-helper contract into hbb_common::platform::linux, adds a validated explicit descriptor allowlist, and migrates every enumerated production Linux helper. The pre-exec child first marks the full non-stdio range close-on-exec, then clears the flag only for validated allowlisted descriptors; the convenience API supplies an empty list. Clipboard mount allowlists only the exact _FUSE_COMMFD socket and no longer clears its flag in the parent; unmount is stdio-only. An actual three-image test proves the same device/inode survives as descriptor 9 only in the explicit case and disappears under the default policy, while a second regression rejects stdio, duplicate, and out-of-range entries. Source and mutation gates bind the shared implementation, all enumerated launch families, the parent-race absence, both runtime branches, R-S11r, this row, and the hardening ledger. Final exact Debian artifact execution remains part of R-B2/R-S11c-27.
141Desktop fatal-signal callback authority — every non-mobile release image installed a process-wide SIGSEGV handler through libc::signal. The handler allocated and symbolized a backtrace, formatted strings, read and wrote configuration, logged, launched a Linux desktop-notification helper, invoked a callback held in mutable global state, called native input-cleanup backends, and finally used process::exit(0). A synchronous memory-integrity fault may interrupt those same allocators, locks, loggers, configuration paths, and native backends in inconsistent state, making re-entry undefined or deadlocking; reporting success also hid abnormal signal termination from supervisors, crash diagnostics, and core-dump policy. This is a crash-path privilege and availability amplifier, not a demonstrated standalone local privilege escalation: reaching it still requires a genuine fault or authority to signal the process, while the deleted Linux helper used fixed executable paths and constant text.FIX R-S11s/R-S11e-33 deletes the fatal handler, its registration API and release startup call, the mutable-global callback and its input-cleanup consumer, the crash-notification launcher/example, and hbb_common's direct backtrace dependency. Desktop release processes now retain the operating system's default fatal-signal disposition, preserving truthful failure status, supervisor Restart=on-failure behavior, and platform crash diagnostics without executing Rust code in a potentially corrupted process. Graceful SIGTERM/SIGINT service shutdown remains separate and unchanged. Source and mutation gates bind the complete symbol/dependency absence, R-S11s, this row, and the hardening ledger.
142macOS service/helper inherited descriptor authority — production macOS launches used ordinary Rust 1.75 Command paths for LaunchDaemon launchctl print authorization, service installation/removal, LaunchAgent lifecycle, root-to-user helpers, same-executable helpers, screen-state helpers, app reopen, and the feature-inert codec checker. The terminal shell entered a different dependency-owned portable-pty fork path whose post-fork cleanup allocated, enumerated /dev/fd, ignored every error, and directly closed arbitrary descriptors including Rust's exec-error pipe. Rust 1.75's macOS posix_spawn path did not request Apple's close-by-default behavior, so any sensitive runtime descriptor lacking FD_CLOEXEC could enter an ordinary child; the PTY path could additionally hide a failed exec. The LaunchDaemon authorization query and root/user transition make this a real privileged-to-helper capability-boundary defect; it is not evidence of a promptless ordinary-user-to-root primitive because exploitation still requires a sensitive live inheritable descriptor. An additional unused hbb_common alert API delegated to osascript 0.3, whose internal helper was PATH-selected and outside application descriptor policy.FIX R-S11t/R-S11e-34 adds one shared macOS ordinary-command pre-exec policy and owns the exact pinned portable-pty 0.8.1 source in-tree. Parent-side code validates a bounded finite RLIMIT_NOFILE and enumerates /dev/fd; each forked child uses only raw fcntl(F_GETFD/F_SETFD) to mark every non-stdio descriptor close-on-exec, including observed inherited descriptors above a lowered soft limit, and treats only EBADF as absence. Marking rather than closing preserves Rust's exec-error pipe until successful exec. Every enumerated production macOS command and the terminal PTY path apply the policy with no non-stdio exception. The unused alert API, direct dependency, lockfile edge, and dependency-owned PATH launch are deleted. Three-image actual-child regressions inject descriptor 9 through the ordinary and actual PTY launch paths, prove its exact device/inode in intermediate test images, and require final images to exclude that object; a third PTY regression proves an exec failure remains visible to the parent. Source, Apple-conformance, and mutation gates bind the complete launch inventory, all five regressions, provenance, and documentation. Native Apple compilation and execution remain explicitly pending under exact-commit R-B2.
